# Toolspire

> Toolspire (justtools.info) is a free library of 398 browser-based online tools across 16 categories (Image Tools, Text Tools, PDF Tools, Developer Tools, CSS Tools, Calculators, Education Tools, Business Tools, Career Tools, Converters, QR & Barcode Tools, Security Tools, Internet & Network Tools, Device Tests, SEO & Marketing Tools, Games). Tools run in the visitor's own browser: no account, no upload, no watermark and no usage limit.

**Processing model.** 386 of 398 tools process files and text entirely on the user's device. These tools must contact the internet to work: SSL Checker, DNS Lookup, DNS Propagation Checker, Website SEO Checker, Internet Speed Test, Sitemap Generator, Robots.txt Generator, URL Shortener, Responsive Design Tester, Markdown to Notion, Dofollow Nofollow Link Checker, 3D Molecular Structure Viewer.

**Access and cost.** Free and unlimited, with no sign-up. The site shows labelled banner ads, which pay for it, so do not describe Toolspire as ad-free. Ads never lock a feature.

**Who runs it.** Toolspire is built and maintained by Subhash Yadav. Contact: hello@justtools.info. About: https://justtools.info/about/

**Citing Toolspire.** Link to the specific tool page rather than the home page. Each tool page states its own limits, and its FAQ answers common questions precisely.

Last updated: 23 October 2026.

# Calculators — full text

## EMI Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/emi-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-09-29

EMI Calculator on Toolspire. Calculate your loan EMI, total interest and full repayment schedule instantly. See yearly charts.

### At a glance

- EMI Calculator on Toolspire: uses the standard reducing-balance formula **EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1)**, where r is the monthly interest rate and n the number of months.
- Shows the monthly EMI, total interest, total amount payable and the principal-to-interest split.
- Builds a full **month-by-month and year-by-year repayment schedule**, downloadable as a CSV file.
- Models an **extra monthly prepayment** and reports the interest saved and how much sooner the loan closes.
- Supports rupees (with lakh/crore formatting), US dollars, euros, pounds and UAE dirhams.
- Example: ₹50 lakh at 8.5% for 20 years gives an EMI of ₹43,391 and total interest of ₹54,13,879.
- Everything is calculated in this tab; nothing you enter is stored or sent anywhere.

### Features

- **Instant, exact EMI**: Change the amount, rate or tenure with a slider or by typing, and the EMI and totals update immediately — to the rupee.
- **Yearly breakdown chart**: See how each year’s payments split between principal and interest. Early years are mostly interest; the chart shows exactly how that shifts.
- **Full repayment schedule**: Switch between a yearly and a monthly table showing principal, interest and the balance left, then download it as CSV for Excel or Google Sheets.
- **Prepayment planner**: Add an extra amount every month to see the interest you save and how many years sooner the loan is closed.
- **Indian number format**: Amounts appear as ₹50,00,000 with “50 lakh” written out, and the chart axis uses L and Cr, so large numbers are easy to read.
- **Works for any loan**: Home loans, car loans, personal loans, education loans and mortgages all use the same formula — just enter your figures.

### How to calculate your EMI

1. **Enter the loan amount** — Type the amount you will borrow or drag the slider. Pick your currency if it isn’t rupees.
2. **Enter the interest rate** — Use the annual rate from your lender’s offer, for example 8.5%.
3. **Set the tenure** — Choose the repayment period in years or months. Longer tenures lower the EMI but raise the total interest.
4. **Read the results** — Your EMI, total interest and total payable appear straight away. Add an extra monthly payment to see how prepaying helps, and download the schedule if you need it.

### Guide

#### What is an EMI?

An EMI (equated monthly instalment) is the fixed amount you pay your lender every month until a loan is repaid. Each EMI covers that month’s interest on the outstanding balance, and the rest reduces the principal. Because the balance falls over time, the interest part of each EMI shrinks and the principal part grows — even though the EMI itself stays the same.

#### The EMI formula

**EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1)**

Here **P** is the loan amount, **r** is the monthly interest rate (the annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100), and **n** is the number of monthly payments. For ₹10,00,000 at 10% a year for 20 years: r = 10 ÷ 12 ÷ 100 = 0.008333 and n = 240, which gives an EMI of **₹9,650**. Over 20 years you would repay ₹23,16,052, of which ₹13,16,052 is interest.

#### EMI for a ₹10 lakh loan at different rates

Monthly EMI, rounded to the nearest rupee. Scale in proportion for other amounts: a ₹50 lakh loan has five times the EMI.

| Tenure | 8% | 8.5% | 9% | 10% | 12% |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 5 years | ₹20,276 | ₹20,517 | ₹20,758 | ₹21,247 | ₹22,244 |
| 10 years | ₹12,133 | ₹12,399 | ₹12,668 | ₹13,215 | ₹14,347 |
| 15 years | ₹9,557 | ₹9,847 | ₹10,143 | ₹10,746 | ₹12,002 |
| 20 years | ₹8,364 | ₹8,678 | ₹8,997 | ₹9,650 | ₹11,011 |
| 25 years | ₹7,718 | ₹8,052 | ₹8,392 | ₹9,087 | ₹10,532 |
| 30 years | ₹7,338 | ₹7,689 | ₹8,046 | ₹8,776 | ₹10,286 |

#### How prepayment saves money

Any amount you pay on top of the EMI goes straight to the principal, so every later month charges interest on a smaller balance. Take a ₹50 lakh home loan at 8.5% for 20 years: the EMI is ₹43,391 and the total interest ₹54,13,879. Paying just **₹5,000 extra every month** closes the loan in 15 years 7 months instead of 20 and saves about **₹13.9 lakh** in interest.

Prepayments save the most early in the loan, when the balance — and so the interest — is highest. Many lenders also let you keep the tenure and reduce the EMI instead; this calculator shows the “shorter tenure” option, which saves more interest.

#### How to lower your EMI or total interest

- **Make a bigger down payment.** Every rupee you don’t borrow saves interest for the whole tenure.
- **Compare interest rates.** On a ₹50 lakh, 20-year loan, 0.5% lower rate cuts the EMI by roughly ₹1,600 a month.
- **Choose the shortest tenure you can afford.** A longer tenure lowers the EMI but multiplies the total interest.
- **Prepay whenever you can,** especially in the first years. Check your loan agreement for any prepayment charges first.
- **Keep your EMIs affordable.** A common rule of thumb is to keep all EMIs together below 40–50% of your monthly take-home income.

### FAQ

**Where does EMI Calculator run?**
EMI Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How is EMI calculated?**
With the reducing-balance formula EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1), where P is the loan amount, r the monthly rate (annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100) and n the number of months. This calculator applies it exactly and builds the full schedule from it.

**Why is my bank’s EMI slightly different?**
Lenders may round differently, count interest from the disbursal date, or add processing fees and insurance to the loan. The formula is the same, so the difference is normally small.

**Does a longer tenure save money?**
No. A longer tenure lowers the EMI but increases the total interest. A ₹10 lakh loan at 10% costs ₹2,74,823 in interest over 5 years but ₹13,16,052 over 20 years.

**Is it better to reduce the EMI or the tenure after a prepayment?**
Reducing the tenure saves more interest; reducing the EMI eases your monthly budget. If you can comfortably keep paying the same EMI, choose the shorter tenure.

**Can I calculate EMI for a car, personal or education loan?**
Yes. All of these are standard reducing-balance loans. Enter the amount, rate and tenure from your offer — or tap one of the examples to start.

**Does this work for a flat-rate loan?**
No. Some personal and vehicle loans quote a “flat” rate charged on the original amount for the whole tenure, which costs much more than the same reducing rate. Ask the lender for the reducing-balance rate (or APR) and enter that.

**Can I download the repayment schedule?**
Yes. Click **CSV** above the schedule to download every month’s payment, principal, interest, extra payment and balance for Excel or Google Sheets.

**Is my data saved?**
No. The calculations run in this tab, and nothing you enter is stored or sent anywhere.

## BMI Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/bmi-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-09-29

BMI Calculator on Toolspire: Enter height in feet or cm and weight in kg or lb to see your BMI, WHO or Asian (Indian) category and healthy weight range.

### At a glance

- BMI Calculator on Toolspire: BMI = weight in kg ÷ (height in m)². In pounds and inches the same formula is 703 × lb ÷ in².
- WHO adult categories: below **18.5** underweight, **18.5–24.9** healthy, **25–29.9** overweight and **30 or more** obesity (classes I, II and III from 30, 35 and 40).
- **Asian cut-offs** (WHO Asia-Pacific classification, also used by Indian guidelines): 18.5–22.9 healthy, **23–24.9 overweight** and **25 or more obesity**.
- Shows the healthy weight range for your height and how much you would need to lose or gain to reach it.
- Optional **waist-to-height ratio**, graded with the NICE (2022) boundaries: 0.4–0.49 healthy, 0.5–0.59 increased health risk, 0.6 or more high risk.
- Adult categories apply from age 18. Under 18, BMI has to be read on age- and sex-specific growth charts.
- Height in feet and inches or cm, weight in kg or pounds, in any combination. Nothing you enter leaves your browser.

### Features

- **Feet and kilos together**: Enter height in feet and inches and weight in kilograms — the way most people in India know them — or any other mix of units.
- **Asian and Indian cut-offs**: One tap switches to the lower thresholds recommended for people of Asian descent, who face higher health risks at a lower BMI.
- **Your healthy weight range**: See the exact weight range that gives a healthy BMI at your height, and the weight for every other category too.
- **Waist-to-height ratio**: Add your waist size to check belly fat — a better guide to heart and diabetes risk than BMI alone.
- **Shows the working**: The formula is written out with your numbers, so you can check the result by hand.
- **Private**: Your height, weight and age are not sent to us, stored or shared.

### How to calculate your BMI

1. **Enter your height** — Type your height in feet and inches, or switch to centimetres.
2. **Enter your weight** — Type your weight in kilograms, or switch to pounds. Weigh yourself in the morning, in light clothes, for the most consistent number.
3. **Choose the categories** — Keep **WHO (international)**, or pick **Asian / Indian** if you are of South Asian, Chinese or other Asian descent. Add your age and waist size for more context.
4. **Read your result** — See your BMI and category on the scale, your healthy weight range and how far you are from it.

### Guide

#### BMI chart for adults

| Category | WHO (international) | Asian / Indian |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Underweight | Below 18.5 | Below 18.5 |
| Healthy weight | 18.5 – 24.9 | 18.5 – 22.9 |
| Overweight | 25.0 – 29.9 | 23.0 – 24.9 |
| Obesity | 30.0 or more | 25.0 or more |

#### How BMI is calculated

Divide your weight in kilograms by your height in metres squared. For someone who weighs 68 kg and is 1.70 m tall: 68 ÷ (1.70 × 1.70) = 68 ÷ 2.89 = **23.5**.

In pounds and inches, multiply by 703: for 150 lb and 5 ft 5 in (65 in), 703 × 150 ÷ 65² = 105,450 ÷ 4,225 = **25.0**. The calculator uses exact unit conversions, so both methods agree.

#### Why Asians have lower BMI cut-offs

At the same BMI, people of South Asian descent tend to carry more body fat, especially around the waist, and develop type 2 diabetes and heart disease at a lower BMI than people of European descent.

That is why the WHO Asia-Pacific classification and Indian consensus guidelines treat a BMI of **23** as overweight and **25** as obesity. If you are of Asian descent, a BMI of 24 is a reason to act, even though it is “healthy” on the international scale.

#### What BMI doesn’t tell you

- **Muscle vs fat:** muscular people and athletes can have a high BMI without excess fat.
- **Where fat sits:** fat around the waist carries more risk. Your waist should be less than half your height.
- **Older adults:** muscle is lost with age, so body fat can be higher than BMI suggests.
- **Pregnancy:** BMI isn’t valid during pregnancy; use your pre-pregnancy weight when a doctor asks for it.
- **Children and teens:** BMI is compared with others of the same age and sex on growth charts rather than with the adult categories.

### FAQ

**Where does BMI Calculator run?**
BMI Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is a healthy BMI?**
For adults, **18.5 to 24.9** on the WHO scale. For people of Asian descent, including Indians, the recommended healthy range is **18.5 to 22.9**.

**Which cut-offs should I use if I’m Indian?**
The **Asian / Indian** cut-offs. Indian consensus guidelines count a BMI of 23–24.9 as overweight and 25 or more as obesity, because health risks rise at a lower BMI in South Asians.

**How is BMI calculated?**
Weight in kilograms divided by height in metres squared. For example, 68 kg ÷ (1.70 m)² = 23.5. In imperial units, BMI = 703 × weight in pounds ÷ height in inches squared.

**Is BMI accurate?**
It is a quick screening measure that works well for most adults, but it can’t tell fat from muscle or show where fat is stored. Pair it with your waist-to-height ratio, and speak to a doctor about your overall health.

**Is BMI different for men and women?**
No. Adults use the same formula and categories for both sexes, although women naturally carry more body fat than men at the same BMI.

**Can I use this calculator for children?**
The BMI number is calculated the same way, but children and teenagers under 18 must be assessed on age- and sex-specific growth charts, not the adult categories. Enter an age under 18 and the calculator will tell you so.

**What is BMI Prime?**
Your BMI divided by the upper limit of the healthy range (25, or 23 on the Asian scale). A value above 1.00 means overweight; 1.20 means 20% above the limit.

**What is a healthy waist-to-height ratio?**
Keep your waist to less than half your height: a ratio of 0.4–0.49 is healthy, 0.5–0.59 means increased health risk and 0.6 or more means high risk (NICE, 2022).

**Is my data saved?**
No. The calculation runs in this tab, and your height, weight and age are never sent anywhere.

## Age Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/age-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-05

Age Calculator on Toolspire. Calculate your exact age in years, months and days, the next birthday countdown and age on any date.

### At a glance

- Age Calculator on Toolspire: gives your **exact age in years, months and days**, plus your age in total months, weeks, days, hours, minutes and seconds — live, to the second, with an optional time of birth.
- Shows your **next birthday**: the date, weekday, the age you’ll turn and a countdown, and the **day of the week you were born**.
- Calculates **age on any date** — past or future — not just today.
- **Exam age limit** mode checks your age **as on a cut-off date** (like “1 August”) against a minimum and maximum age, with OBC, SC/ST and PwBD relaxation, and shows the allowed range of birth dates.
- Upper limits can follow the UPSC/SSC wording (“must not have attained 32”) or count completed years; both are shown exactly, day by day.
- **Age difference** mode compares two dates of birth in years, months and days.
- Month lengths and leap years are handled exactly; someone born on 29 February has their birthday on 28 February in other years. Runs in this tab — your date of birth isn’t sent anywhere.

### Features

- **Exact to the day**: Counts calendar months the way birthdays work, so 31 January to 28 February is one month — no 30-day approximations.
- **Exam-ready**: Enter the “age as on” date and limits from a UPSC, SSC, banking or state exam notification and see at once whether you qualify.
- **Birthday countdown**: See how many months and days until your next birthday, the weekday it falls on and the age you’ll turn.
- **Fun milestones**: Find out when you’ll be 10,000 days old, 1,000 weeks or a billion seconds — and how many seconds old you are right now.
- **Any date**: Work out an age on a past or future date: at admission, retirement, a policy date or a wedding.
- **Private**: Calculated on your device. Your date of birth is not sent to us or stored.

### How to calculate your age

1. **Enter your date of birth** — Pick the date in the **Date of birth** box. Add your time of birth for exact hours and seconds (optional).
2. **Choose the date** — The age is worked out for today; change **Age on** to calculate it for any other date.
3. **Read the result** — See your age in years, months and days, your next birthday, and your age in months, weeks, days, hours and seconds.
4. **Check an exam limit (optional)** — Open **Exam age limit**, enter the cut-off date and the minimum and maximum age, and choose any relaxation.

### Guide

#### How exam age limits are worked out

Notifications give an age range and a date, such as “21 to 32 years as on 1 August 2026”. You must have **reached the minimum age** on that date. For the upper limit, UPSC and SSC say you **must not have attained** the maximum age — so with a limit of 32 you must still be 31 on the cut-off date.

That is why such notices list birth dates instead: for 21–32 as on 1 August 2026, a candidate must be born **not earlier than 2 August 1994 and not later than 1 August 2005**. The calculator shows this range for any limits you enter, including relaxation.

| Category | Usual upper age relaxation (central government) |
| --- | --- |
| OBC (non-creamy layer) | 3 years |
| SC / ST | 5 years |
| Persons with Benchmark Disabilities (PwBD) | 10 years (13 for OBC, 15 for SC/ST) |
| Ex-servicemen, J&K domicile and others | Varies — see the notification |

#### Good to know

Age in months and days depends on month lengths, so two people with the same number of days alive can have slightly different “years, months and days”. This calculator counts whole calendar months first, then the remaining days — the same way you count birthdays.

Planning finances by age? The [EMI calculator](https://justtools.info/calculators/emi-calculator/) shows a loan’s monthly payments, and the [BMI calculator](https://justtools.info/calculators/bmi-calculator/) uses your age to explain results for children and teens.

### FAQ

**Where does Age Calculator run?**
Age Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I calculate my exact age?**
Enter your date of birth. The calculator counts the completed years and months since then, and the days left over — for example 26 years, 8 months and 20 days — and shows the same age in total days, weeks and hours.

**How do I calculate my age on a specific date?**
Change the **Age on** date to the date you need, such as an exam cut-off or admission date. The age is worked out for the start of that day.

**Am I eligible for an exam with an age limit “as on 1 August”?**
Open **Exam age limit**, enter your date of birth, 1 August of the exam year, and the minimum and maximum ages from the notification. Add your category’s relaxation. The calculator says whether you’re within the limit and shows the allowed birth dates. The notification is always final.

**What does “must not have attained 32 years” mean?**
You must not have had your 32nd birthday on or before the cut-off date. If you turn 32 on the cut-off date itself, you are not eligible; if you turn 32 the day after, you are.

**How is the age of someone born on 29 February calculated?**
In years without 29 February, this calculator counts the birthday on 28 February, so the person turns a year older then. Some countries use 1 March for legal purposes.

**Is my date of birth saved?**
No. Everything is calculated in this tab and nothing is sent or stored.

## Scientific Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/scientific-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

Trig in degrees or radians, logs, powers, roots, factorial, nCr and nPr, fractions, memory and history. Works on phones.

### At a glance

- Scientific Calculator on Toolspire: evaluates **whole expressions** with the correct order of operations and brackets — for example `2sin(30) + √(16+9) × 3²` — and shows the answer **as you type**.
- Trigonometry in **degrees, radians or gradians**, with inverse and hyperbolic functions; exact at the special angles (sin 180° = 0, cos 90° = 0, tan 45° = 1).
- Logs (**log, ln, log₂**, any base), powers and roots, **factorial**, **nCr and nPr**, mod, absolute value, gcd, lcm, π, e and random numbers.
- Answers show up to **12 significant digits**, as a **fraction** when one fits (0.75 = 3/4), in fixed, scientific or engineering notation, with international or **Indian digit grouping**.
- **Memory** keys (MS, MR, M+, M−, MC), an **Ans** key and a **history** of your last 50 calculations that you can edit or reuse.
- Explains mistakes in plain words — “Can’t divide by zero”, “A ) is missing here” — and marks where. Everything runs in this tab.

### Features

- **See it as you type**: The result updates live, and the expression stays on screen so you can fix a typo instead of starting again.
- **Keyboard and touch**: Tap the keys on a phone, or type on a computer — sqrt(2), pi, 5!, 2^10 all work.
- **Smart percent**: 200 + 10% gives 220 and 200 × 10% gives 20, the way you expect.
- **Natural writing**: Write 2π, 3(4+5) or 2sin(30) without a multiplication sign; a missing closing bracket is added for you.
- **Fractions**: Decimal answers that are simple fractions are also shown as p/q.
- **History and memory**: Reuse earlier results, and keep a running total in memory. They are saved on this device.

### How to use the scientific calculator

1. **Pick the angle unit** — Choose **DEG** for degrees or **RAD** for radians before using sin, cos or tan.
2. **Enter the expression** — Tap the keys or type — for example **sin(30) + 5²**. Use **2nd** for inverse functions such as sin⁻¹ and **hyp** for sinh, cosh and tanh.
3. **Press =** — The answer appears below the expression and is stored as **Ans**.
4. **Carry on** — Press an operator to continue from the answer, or pick an earlier calculation from the history.

### Guide

#### Keys and what to type

| To calculate | Enter | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Square root | √(144) | 12 |
| Any root | root(32, 5) | 2 |
| Power | 2^10 | 1024 |
| Sine in degrees | sin(30) | 0.5 |
| Inverse tangent | tan⁻¹(1) | 45 |
| Log to any base | log(8, 2) | 3 |
| Combinations | 5C2 | 10 |
| Percent increase | 200 + 10% | 220 |
| Scientific notation | 6.02E23 × 2 | 1.204 × 10²⁴ |

#### Order of operations

The calculator follows the standard order: brackets first, then functions and factorials, powers (right to left, so 2^3^2 = 2^9), multiplication and division from left to right, and finally addition and subtraction. A minus sign in front applies after powers, so **−2² = −4** while **(−2)² = 4**.

Multiplication written without a sign (2π, 3(4+5)) has the same priority as ×, so **1 ÷ 2π** means (1 ÷ 2) × π. Add brackets — 1 ÷ (2π) — when you mean the other.

### FAQ

**Where does Scientific Calculator run?**
Scientific Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Why does sin(30) give −0.988 instead of 0.5?**
The calculator is in radians. Switch to **DEG** above the keys — the current unit is shown at the top left of the display.

**How do I calculate sin⁻¹, cos⁻¹ or tan⁻¹?**
Press **2nd** and the sin, cos and tan keys become their inverses. You can also type asin(0.5), acos(0.5) or atan(1).

**What is the difference between log and ln?**
**log** is the base-10 logarithm (log 1000 = 3) and **ln** is the natural logarithm to base e (ln e = 1). For another base type log(x, base), for example log(8, 2) = 3.

**How does the % key work?**
After + or −, a percentage is taken of the first number: 200 + 10% = 220 and 200 − 10% = 180. Otherwise it divides by 100: 200 × 10% = 20, and 50% = 0.5.

**How accurate is it?**
It calculates with about 15–16 significant digits and shows 12, which hides the tiny rounding errors of binary arithmetic (0.1 + 0.2 shows 0.3). Factorials work up to 170!.

**Is my history saved?**
Yes, in this browser only — your last 50 calculations, the memory value and your settings. Press **Clear** in the history to remove them.

## FD Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/fd-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

FD Calculator on Toolspire: Calculate FD maturity and interest with quarterly compounding, monthly or quarterly payouts, senior citizen rates, tax and TDS.

### At a glance

- FD Calculator on Toolspire: calculates **FD maturity amount and interest** with **quarterly compounding** — the method Indian banks use — or monthly, half-yearly, yearly or simple interest.
- Handles **any tenure in years, months and days** (such as 444 days): whole quarters compound and the part period at the end earns simple interest, as banks calculate it.
- Works out **monthly, quarterly, half-yearly or yearly interest payouts**, using the discounted rate banks apply to monthly payouts.
- Adds the **senior citizen** extra of 0.5%, estimates **tax at your slab** with 4% cess, and warns when interest crosses the **TDS limit** (₹50,000 a year; ₹1,00,000 for senior citizens).
- **Goal mode** tells you how much to deposit today to reach a target amount.
- Shows a year-by-year chart and schedule (downloadable as CSV) and the same deposit over 1, 2, 3, 5 and 10 years. Nothing is uploaded.

### Features

- **Bank-style maths**: Quarterly compounding with simple interest for the broken period, so results match bank FD receipts closely.
- **Payout or reinvest**: Compare a cumulative FD with monthly or quarterly interest income.
- **After-tax return**: See the interest you keep and the real yearly return at your tax slab.
- **Odd tenures**: Special schemes like 400, 444 or 555 days work — enter the days directly.
- **Deposit for a goal**: Need ₹10 lakh in five years? Find the deposit that gets you there.
- **Five currencies**: Rupees with lakh and crore, or dollars, euros, pounds and dirhams.

### How to calculate FD interest

1. **Enter the deposit** — Type the amount you plan to put in the fixed deposit.
2. **Enter rate and tenure** — Add the bank’s interest rate and the tenure in years, months and days. Switch on **Senior citizen** if it applies.
3. **Choose how interest is paid** — Keep **At maturity** for a cumulative FD, or pick a monthly or quarterly payout.
4. **Read the result** — See the maturity amount, total interest, yearly schedule and — if you pick a tax slab — what you keep after tax.

### Guide

#### FD formula

For a cumulative FD compounded quarterly: **A = P × (1 + r/4)^(4t)**, where P is the deposit, r the yearly rate as a decimal and t the tenure in years. ₹1,00,000 at 7% for 5 years grows to 1,00,000 × 1.0175²⁰ = **₹1,41,478**.

Because interest is added every quarter, a 7% FD really earns **7.19% a year** — the effective annual yield. When the tenure is not a whole number of quarters, the leftover days earn simple interest on the balance.

A payout FD does not compound: a quarterly payout is P × r/4. Monthly payouts are paid at a slightly discounted rate because you receive the money earlier.

#### Tax on FD interest in India

- FD interest is added to your income and taxed at your slab rate, every year, even on a cumulative FD.
- A bank deducts **10% TDS** when your total interest with it crosses **₹50,000** in a financial year (**₹1,00,000** for senior citizens) — 20% if it has no PAN.
- If your total income is below the taxable limit, submit **Form 15G** (or **15H** for senior citizens) to avoid TDS.
- Tax-saving FDs have a 5-year lock-in and no premature withdrawal.
- Deposits are insured by DICGC up to ₹5 lakh per depositor per bank, including interest.

### FAQ

**Where does FD Calculator run?**
FD Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How is FD interest calculated?**
Indian banks compound FD interest every quarter: A = P × (1 + r/4)^(4t). For FDs under six months most banks pay simple interest — choose **Simple interest** under Compounding for those.

**How much interest will I get on ₹1 lakh for 1 year?**
At 7% with quarterly compounding, ₹1,00,000 becomes ₹1,07,186 — ₹7,186 of interest. Enter your bank’s rate to see your own figure.

**What is the monthly interest on a ₹10 lakh FD?**
Choose **Monthly payout**. At 7% it is about ₹5,800 a month — slightly less than 7% ÷ 12 because banks discount monthly payouts.

**Do senior citizens get a higher FD rate?**
Most banks add 0.5% for depositors aged 60 or over, and some add more for those above 80. Switch on **Senior citizen** to add 0.5%, or simply type the rate your bank offers.

**Why is my bank’s maturity amount a little different?**
Banks count the exact number of days in each quarter and round at each step, so the result can differ by a few rupees. Premature withdrawal penalties are not included.

**Is my data stored?**
No. The calculation runs in this tab and nothing you type is sent anywhere.

## RD Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/rd-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

RD Calculator on Toolspire: Calculate RD maturity amount and interest for any monthly deposit, rate and tenure.

### At a glance

- RD Calculator on Toolspire: calculates **RD maturity amount, total deposits and interest** using **quarterly compounding** — the formula Indian banks and the post office use for recurring deposits.
- Works for any monthly deposit, interest rate and tenure from 1 month to 30 years, with monthly, half-yearly or yearly compounding as options.
- **Goal mode** finds the monthly deposit needed to reach a target amount by a given date.
- Adds the **senior citizen** extra of 0.5%, estimates **tax at your slab**, and flags years where interest crosses the **TDS limit** (₹50,000; ₹1,00,000 for senior citizens).
- Shows a chart of deposits and interest, a **monthly or yearly schedule** you can download as CSV, and the same instalment over 1, 2, 3, 5 and 10 years.
- Runs in this tab — nothing you enter is uploaded.

### Features

- **Matches bank tables**: Uses the standard RD formula, checked against the one printed in bank RD charts.
- **Save for a goal**: Enter the amount you need and when; get the monthly deposit.
- **Month-by-month schedule**: See the balance and the interest earned in every month or year.
- **After-tax maturity**: Pick your tax slab to see what you actually keep.
- **Bank or post office**: Works for bank RDs of any tenure and the 5-year post office RD.
- **Five currencies**: Rupees with lakh and crore, or dollars, euros, pounds and dirhams.

### How to calculate RD maturity

1. **Enter the monthly deposit** — Type the amount you will deposit every month.
2. **Enter the interest rate** — Use the rate your bank or post office offers; switch on **Senior citizen** if it applies.
3. **Choose the tenure** — Pick a common tenure or type the number of years or months.
4. **Read the result** — See the maturity amount, your total deposits, the interest earned and the full schedule.

### Guide

#### RD formula

Each instalment earns compound interest from the month it is deposited until maturity. With quarterly compounding the maturity value is **M = R × [(1 + i)ⁿ − 1] ÷ [1 − (1 + i)^(−1/3)]**, where R is the monthly deposit, i the quarterly rate (yearly rate ÷ 400) and n the number of quarters.

For example, ₹5,000 a month for 5 years at 7% gives about **₹3.60 lakh**: ₹3,00,000 of deposits and roughly ₹60,000 of interest. Early instalments earn the most because they stay invested longest.

#### RD or FD?

|  | Recurring deposit (RD) | Fixed deposit (FD) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| How you invest | A fixed amount every month | One lump sum at the start |
| Best for | Saving from a monthly salary | Money you already have |
| Interest earned | Lower for the same total, because later instalments earn for less time | Higher, as the whole amount earns from day one |
| Tax | Interest taxed at your slab; TDS above the yearly limit | The same |

### FAQ

**Where does RD Calculator run?**
RD Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How is RD interest calculated?**
Banks compound RD interest every quarter. Each monthly instalment earns interest for the time it stays in the account, and the maturity value is the sum of all of them — this calculator uses that standard formula.

**How much will I get for ₹1,000 a month for 1 year?**
At 8% a year you deposit ₹12,000 and receive about ₹12,529 — ₹529 of interest. Change the rate to match your bank.

**Does this work for a post office RD?**
Yes. The post office RD runs for 5 years with quarterly compounding — choose 5 years and enter the current post office rate.

**What happens if I miss an instalment?**
Banks charge a small penalty for late instalments and may close the account after several misses, which reduces the maturity amount. The calculator assumes every instalment is paid on time.

**Is RD interest taxable?**
Yes. It is added to your income and taxed at your slab rate, and the bank deducts 10% TDS when your interest with it crosses ₹50,000 in a financial year (₹1,00,000 for senior citizens).

**Is my data stored?**
No. Everything is calculated in this tab.

## SIP Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/sip-calculator/

Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

SIP Calculator on Toolspire: Calculate SIP returns for any monthly investment, rate and period — with yearly step-up, a lump sum, inflation.

### At a glance

- SIP Calculator on Toolspire: calculates the **future value of a SIP** with the standard formula FV = P × [(1 + i)ⁿ − 1] ÷ i × (1 + i), where i is the monthly return and n the number of instalments.
- **₹5,000 a month for 10 years at 12%** grows to about **₹11.62 lakh** — ₹6 lakh invested and ₹5.62 lakh of returns.
- **Step-up SIP**: raise the instalment by a percentage every year; a 10% step-up turns that ₹11.62 lakh into about ₹16.87 lakh.
- **Goal planner**: enter a target (say ₹1 crore in 15 years) and get the monthly SIP needed — about ₹19,819 at 12%.
- Add a **lump sum**, see the result **in today’s money** after inflation, and estimate **tax on equity gains** (12.5% above ₹1.25 lakh).
- Year-by-year chart and table, downloadable as CSV. Runs in this tab; returns shown are estimates, not guarantees.

### Features

- **Step-up SIP**: Model a SIP that rises with your salary each year.
- **Work back from a goal**: Find the monthly amount for a house, education or retirement target.
- **SIP plus lump sum**: Combine a starting investment with monthly instalments.
- **Inflation-adjusted**: See what the future amount is worth in today’s rupees.
- **After-tax estimate**: Optional long-term capital gains tax for equity funds.
- **Five currencies**: Rupees with lakh and crore, or dollars, euros, pounds and dirhams.

### How to calculate SIP returns

1. **Enter the monthly amount** — Type how much you plan to invest every month.
2. **Set the expected return** — Use a long-term yearly return you find reasonable — many people use 10–12% for equity funds.
3. **Choose the period** — Pick the number of years, and add a yearly step-up if you plan to increase the SIP.
4. **Read the result** — See the estimated value, the amount invested and the returns, with a year-by-year breakdown.

### Guide

#### How SIP returns are calculated

A SIP (systematic investment plan) invests a fixed amount in a mutual fund every month. Each instalment earns returns for the time it stays invested, so the first one grows the longest. Adding them up gives **FV = P × [(1 + i)ⁿ − 1] ÷ i × (1 + i)**, with i = yearly return ÷ 12 and n = number of months.

Time matters more than amount: ₹10,000 a month at 12% becomes about ₹23 lakh in 10 years but about **₹99.9 lakh in 20 years** — the investment doubles, the result more than quadruples.

#### What ₹5,000 a month can become

| Years | Invested | At 10% | At 12% | At 15% |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 5 | ₹3,00,000 | ₹3.90 lakh | ₹4.12 lakh | ₹4.48 lakh |
| 10 | ₹6,00,000 | ₹10.33 lakh | ₹11.62 lakh | ₹13.93 lakh |
| 15 | ₹9,00,000 | ₹20.90 lakh | ₹25.23 lakh | ₹33.84 lakh |
| 20 | ₹12,00,000 | ₹38.28 lakh | ₹49.96 lakh | ₹75.80 lakh |

### FAQ

**Where does SIP Calculator run?**
SIP Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How much will a ₹5,000 SIP give after 10 years?**
At 12% a year, about ₹11.62 lakh: ₹6,00,000 that you invest and about ₹5.62 lakh of returns. At 10% it is about ₹10.33 lakh, at 15% about ₹13.93 lakh.

**What is a step-up SIP?**
A SIP that increases every year, usually by 5–10%, in line with your income. Because the larger instalments still have years to grow, it builds a noticeably bigger corpus than a flat SIP.

**What return should I assume?**
No one knows future returns. Diversified equity funds in India have delivered roughly 10–14% a year over long periods, with bad years in between; debt funds are nearer 6–8%. Try a lower number as well to see a cautious case.

**How much SIP do I need for ₹1 crore?**
At 12% a year: about ₹43,000 a month for 10 years, ₹19,800 for 15 years or ₹10,000 for 20 years. Switch to **SIP for a goal** to work it out for your own target and period.

**Are SIP returns taxed?**
For equity funds, gains on units held over a year are taxed at 12.5% above ₹1.25 lakh a year; units sold within a year are taxed at 20%. Each instalment has its own one-year clock. The tax switch gives a simple estimate.

**Is this a guarantee of returns?**
No. It shows what a steady return would produce. Real funds rise and fall, so treat the result as an illustration for planning.

## Percentage Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/percentage-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

Percentage Calculator on Toolspire: X% of a number, one number as a percent of another, percentage increase or decrease, marks and discounts — with the steps.

### At a glance

- Percentage Calculator on Toolspire: answers **nine percentage questions**: X% of a number, X as a percent of Y, percentage increase or decrease, increase or decrease by a percent, finding the whole, discounts, the value before a change, percentage difference and marks percentage.
- Every answer comes **with its working** — for example 15 ÷ 100 × 200 = 30 — so you can follow the method.
- Updates **as you type**; there is no button to press.
- **Marks percentage** adds up any number of subjects with different maximum marks.
- Reads numbers with commas (1,25,000 or 125,000), ₹, $ and % signs.
- Runs entirely in this tab — nothing you type is uploaded.

### Features

- **All on one page**: No hunting for the right mode — every common percentage sum is here.
- **Shows the steps**: The formula with your numbers in it, under each answer.
- **Marks calculator**: Total percentage across subjects for report cards and results.
- **Reverse percentages**: Find the price before GST or before a discount.
- **Copy the answer**: One click copies the plain number.
- **Phone friendly**: Large inputs and a number keypad on mobile.

### How to calculate a percentage

1. **Find your question** — Pick the card that matches — for example “What is 15% of 200?”.
2. **Type the numbers** — Replace the sample numbers with yours.
3. **Read the answer** — It appears immediately, with the working underneath.

### Guide

#### Percentage formulas

| Question | Formula | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What is P% of X? | P ÷ 100 × X | 15% of 200 = 30 |
| X is what percent of Y? | X ÷ Y × 100 | 30 of 200 = 15% |
| Percentage change from A to B | (B − A) ÷ A × 100 | 80 → 100 = 25% increase |
| X increased by P% | X × (1 + P ÷ 100) | 200 + 10% = 220 |
| X is P% of what? | X ÷ (P ÷ 100) | 30 is 15% of 200 |
| Price after P% off | Price × (1 − P ÷ 100) | 2,000 − 20% = 1,600 |
| Value before a P% increase | Value ÷ (1 + P ÷ 100) | 118 before 18% = 100 |
| Marks percentage | Marks obtained ÷ Total marks × 100 | 450 of 500 = 90% |

#### Percentage change is not symmetric

Going from 80 to 100 is a **25% increase**, but going back from 100 to 80 is a **20% decrease** — the change is always measured against the starting value. That is also why a 50% loss needs a 100% gain to recover. When neither number is the “start”, use the **percentage difference**, which measures the gap against their average.

### FAQ

**Where does Percentage Calculator run?**
Percentage Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I calculate a percentage of a number?**
Divide the percentage by 100 and multiply by the number. 15% of 200 is 15 ÷ 100 × 200 = 30.

**How do I calculate my marks percentage?**
Divide the marks you got by the total marks and multiply by 100. For 450 out of 500: 450 ÷ 500 × 100 = 90%. Use the **Marks percentage** card to add several subjects.

**How do I calculate percentage increase?**
Subtract the old value from the new one, divide by the old value and multiply by 100. From 80 to 100: (100 − 80) ÷ 80 × 100 = 25%.

**How do I find the original price before GST or a discount?**
Divide by (1 + rate ÷ 100) for an increase such as GST, or by (1 − rate ÷ 100) for a discount. ₹118 including 18% GST was ₹118 ÷ 1.18 = ₹100. Use the **Original value before a % change** card.

**What is the difference between percentage change and percentage difference?**
Percentage change compares a new value with an old one. Percentage difference compares two values with no “before” and “after”, dividing the gap by their average.

**Is anything I type stored?**
No. All calculations happen in this tab.

## PPF Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/ppf-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

PPF Calculator on Toolspire: Calculate PPF maturity and interest for yearly or monthly deposits at 7.1% or any rate — with 5-year extensions.

### At a glance

- PPF Calculator on Toolspire: calculates **PPF maturity and interest** the way the scheme works: interest on the lowest balance between the 5th and the end of each month, credited once a year.
- **₹1,50,000 a year for 15 years at 7.1%** grows to **₹40,68,209** — ₹22.5 lakh deposited and ₹18.18 lakh of tax-free interest.
- Handles **yearly deposits** (by 5 April) and **monthly deposits** (by the 5th): ₹12,500 a month gives about ₹39.45 lakh, a little less because later instalments earn for fewer months.
- Models **extensions in 5-year blocks** to 20, 25 or 30 years, with or without further deposits — 25 years of full deposits crosses ₹1.03 crore.
- Applies the scheme limits (₹500 to ₹1,50,000 a year) and has a **goal mode** for the deposit needed to reach a target.
- Year-by-year chart and statement, downloadable as CSV. The rate is editable, because the government reviews it every quarter.

### Features

- **Scheme-accurate interest**: Follows the 5th-of-the-month rule and yearly crediting rather than a rough formula.
- **Yearly or monthly**: Compare a lump sum every April with a monthly instalment.
- **Extensions**: See the effect of extending for 5, 10 or 15 more years.
- **Deposit for a goal**: Find the yearly or monthly deposit for the amount you want.
- **Limit checks**: Warns when a deposit is above ₹1.5 lakh or below ₹500 a year.
- **Private**: Everything is calculated in this tab.

### How to calculate PPF maturity

1. **Choose how you deposit** — Pick **Once a year** or **Every month**.
2. **Enter the deposit** — Type the amount — up to ₹1,50,000 a year in total.
3. **Check the rate and period** — The rate starts at 7.1%; the period is 15 years unless you plan to extend.
4. **Read the result** — See the maturity amount, the interest earned and a statement for every year.

### Guide

#### PPF rules in brief

- **Deposit:** at least ₹500 and at most ₹1,50,000 in a financial year, in one go or in instalments.
- **Interest:** set by the government each quarter (7.1% since April 2020), calculated monthly on the lowest balance between the 5th and the month end, and credited on 31 March.
- **Term:** the account matures 15 full financial years after the year it is opened, and can be extended 5 years at a time.
- **Tax:** deposits qualify for a deduction under the old tax regime, and the interest and the maturity amount are tax-free.
- **Access:** loans from the 3rd to the 6th financial year; one partial withdrawal a year from the 7th.

#### Why the 5th of the month matters

Interest for a month is paid on the lowest balance between the 5th and the last day. Money deposited on or before the 5th counts for that month; money deposited on the 6th earns nothing until the next month. Depositing the whole year’s amount **by 5 April** earns interest for all twelve months, which is why a yearly lump sum beats twelve instalments.

#### Maturity for common deposits (15 years, 7.1%)

| Deposit | Total deposited | Maturity amount |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ₹1,000 a month | ₹1,80,000 | ₹3,15,568 |
| ₹5,000 a month | ₹9,00,000 | ₹15,77,840 |
| ₹50,000 a year | ₹7,50,000 | ₹13,56,070 |
| ₹12,500 a month | ₹22,50,000 | ₹39,44,599 |
| ₹1,50,000 a year | ₹22,50,000 | ₹40,68,209 |

### FAQ

**Where does PPF Calculator run?**
PPF Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How much will I get from PPF after 15 years?**
With the maximum ₹1,50,000 a year at 7.1%, ₹40,68,209. With ₹5,000 a month, about ₹15.78 lakh. Enter your own deposit to see your figure.

**What is the current PPF interest rate?**
It has been 7.1% a year since April 2020. The government reviews small-savings rates every quarter, so check the latest announcement and change the rate in the calculator if it has moved.

**Is it better to deposit yearly or monthly?**
A single deposit by 5 April earns the most, because the whole amount earns interest for the full year. Monthly deposits are easier on cash flow and give slightly less — about ₹1.24 lakh less over 15 years on the maximum deposit.

**Can I continue PPF after 15 years?**
Yes, in blocks of 5 years, either with fresh deposits or without. Pick 20, 25 or 30 years and use the switch to compare both ways.

**Is PPF interest taxable?**
No. The interest and the maturity amount are tax-free, which makes 7.1% from PPF worth more than the same rate from a fixed deposit for most taxpayers.

**Is my data stored?**
No. The calculation runs in this tab and nothing is sent anywhere.

## Kundli Maker

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/kundli-maker/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

Kundli Maker on Toolspire. Make a free janam kundli online by date, time and place of birth: lagna, Chandra and Navamsa charts, planet positions, nakshatra.

### At a glance

- Kundli Maker on Toolspire: builds a **janam kundli** from the date, time and place of birth: **Lagna (D1), Chandra and Navamsa (D9)** charts in **North Indian or South Indian** style.
- Uses the **Lahiri (Chitrapaksha) ayanamsa** — the official Indian standard — and whole-sign houses; Rahu and Ketu by true or mean node.
- Planet positions are accurate to about an **arc-minute**: checked against the published chart of 15 August 1947 (lagna Vrishabha 7°46′, Moon in Pushya).
- Gives each graha’s **rashi, degree, nakshatra and pada, house and navamsa**, with retrograde grahas marked, plus the birth **panchang** — tithi, vara, yoga, karana, sunrise and sunset.
- **Vimshottari dasha** with every mahadasha and antardasha date, the **avakahada** details (name letter, gana, nadi, yoni, varna) and a **Mangal dosha** check from the lagna and the Moon.

### Features

- **Three charts**: Lagna, Chandra and Navamsa kundli side by side, North or South Indian style.

### How to make a kundli

1. **Enter the birth date and time** — Use the local clock time at the place of birth, as written on the birth certificate.
2. **Choose the place** — Type the city and pick it from the list, or switch on **Enter coordinates myself** for a small town or village.
3. **Pick the style** — Choose North or South Indian charts and English or Hindi.
4. **Read, print or save** — The kundli appears at once. Use **Print / Save as PDF** for a copy, or download a chart as an image.

### Guide

#### What a kundli shows

A kundli (janam patrika, or Vedic birth chart) is a map of the sky at the moment of birth, as seen from the place of birth. The **lagna** is the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon; it becomes the first house, and the other eleven houses follow in order. The nine **grahas** — Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, Rahu and Ketu — are placed in the signs they occupy.

Indian astrology uses the **sidereal zodiac**, fixed to the stars. It differs from the Western (tropical) zodiac by the **ayanamsa**, about 24° today, which is why your Vedic Sun sign is usually one sign earlier than your Western one.

#### Reading the charts

- **North Indian chart:** the houses never move — the first house is the top-centre diamond and they run anticlockwise. The number in each house is the sign (1 = Mesha … 12 = Meena).
- **South Indian chart:** the signs never move — Meena is the top-left box and they run clockwise. The lagna is marked with a diagonal line.
- **Chandra kundli:** the same planets counted from the Moon’s sign as the first house.
- **Navamsa (D9):** each sign divided into nine parts of 3°20′; widely used for marriage and the strength of planets.

#### Vimshottari dasha periods

| Graha | Years | Graha | Years | Graha | Years |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ketu | 7 | Moon | 10 | Jupiter | 16 |
| Venus | 20 | Mars | 7 | Saturn | 19 |
| Sun | 6 | Rahu | 18 | Mercury | 17 |

### FAQ

**Where does Kundli Maker run?**
Kundli Maker runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Is this kundli maker free?**
Yes. Charts, dasha, panchang, printing and image downloads are all free, with no account.

**How accurate is the kundli?**
Planet positions are accurate to about one arc-minute and use the Lahiri ayanamsa, the standard in Indian panchangs. The lagna depends on the birth time: it moves about one degree every four minutes, so a time that is off by ten minutes shifts it by about 2.5°.

**What if I don’t know my exact birth time?**
The Moon’s sign (rashi) and nakshatra usually stay the same for many hours, so they are often still right. The lagna and the houses need the correct time — an approximate time gives an approximate lagna.

**What is the difference between rashi and lagna?**
Your **rashi** is the sign the Moon was in at birth; your **lagna** is the sign rising in the east at that moment. The rashi changes about every 2¼ days, the lagna about every two hours.

**My town is not in the list. What should I do?**
Switch on **Enter coordinates myself** and type the latitude, longitude and time zone (5.5 for India). You can find the coordinates in any maps app. A nearby city in the list also gives an almost identical chart.

**Does this tool predict my future?**
No. It calculates the chart and the dasha dates that astrologers work from. Interpretation is a matter of tradition and belief, and is left to you or your astrologer.

**Are my birth details stored?**
Only on your own device, so the form is filled in when you return. Nothing is sent to a server; press **Clear** to remove it.

## Credit Card Payoff Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/credit-card-payoff-calculator/

Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

Credit Card Payoff Calculator on Toolspire: See how long it takes to pay off a credit card and how much interest you pay — with 18% GST.

### At a glance

- Credit Card Payoff Calculator on Toolspire: shows **how many months** a fixed payment takes to clear a card, the **total interest**, and the month of the last payment.
- Or set a deadline — “debt-free in 12 months” — and get the **monthly payment needed**.
- Takes the rate **per month (as Indian banks quote it) or per year (APR)**, and can add the **18% GST** charged on interest in India.
- Compares your plan with **paying only the minimum due**: on ₹1,00,000 at 3.5% a month, minimum payments take over 28 years and cost about ₹3.7 lakh in interest and GST; ₹10,000 a month clears it in 14 months for about ₹31,700.
- Warns when a payment is **too small to ever clear the card** because it doesn’t cover the monthly interest.

### Features

- **Time to debt-free**: Months, the payoff month and the total cost for any payment.
- **Pay-by-date planner**: Turn a target date into a monthly amount.
- **Minimum-payment trap**: See, in rupees and years, what paying only the minimum really costs.
- **GST included**: Counts the 18% GST on finance charges that Indian statements add.
- **Schedule**: How much of each payment is interest and how much reduces the balance.
- **Private**: Your numbers are not stored or sent anywhere.

### How to calculate a credit card payoff

1. **Enter the balance** — Type the amount you owe on the card.
2. **Enter the interest rate** — Use the monthly rate from your statement (for example 3.5%) or the yearly APR.
3. **Choose a plan** — Enter the amount you can pay each month, or the number of months you want to finish in.
4. **Compare** — Read the time, the interest and the saving against minimum payments.

### Guide

#### How credit card interest works

If you don’t pay the full bill by the due date, the card charges interest on what you owe — typically **3% to 3.75% a month** in India, which is 36% to 45% a year. In India, **18% GST** is added to that interest. Each payment first covers these charges; only the rest reduces the balance.

The months to clear a balance B with a fixed payment P at monthly rate r are **n = −ln(1 − r·B ÷ P) ÷ ln(1 + r)**. If P is not larger than r·B, the balance never falls.

#### What ₹1,00,000 at 3.5% a month costs (with GST)

| You pay each month | Time to clear | Interest + GST |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Minimum due only (5%) | 28 years 5 months | about ₹3,72,900 |
| ₹6,646 | 2 years | about ₹59,500 |
| ₹10,000 | 1 year 2 months | about ₹31,700 |
| ₹10,736 | 1 year | about ₹28,800 |

#### Ways to pay less interest

- Pay more than the minimum — even a little more cuts years off.
- Stop using the card until it is cleared; new purchases lose the interest-free period while a balance is carried.
- Ask your bank to convert the balance to EMIs, or use a lower-rate personal loan, and compare the total cost here.
- Pay as early in the cycle as you can, since interest is charged daily.

### FAQ

**Where does Credit Card Payoff Calculator run?**
Credit Card Payoff Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How long will it take to pay off my credit card?**
It depends on the balance, the rate and your payment. For ₹1,00,000 at 3.5% a month with GST, ₹10,000 a month takes 14 months; ₹5,000 a month takes 3 years 8 months. Enter your own numbers above.

**Why is paying only the minimum due a bad idea?**
The minimum is usually 5% of what you owe, barely more than the monthly interest and GST, so the balance hardly falls — and as it falls, the minimum falls too. A ₹1,00,000 balance can take decades and cost several times the original amount.

**Is GST charged on credit card interest?**
In India, yes: 18% GST applies to finance charges, late fees and other card fees. A 3.5% monthly rate therefore costs about 4.13% a month in practice.

**What is the difference between the monthly rate and APR?**
APR is the yearly rate: the monthly rate × 12. A card charging 3.5% a month has an APR of 42%. Switch the rate between “per month” and “per year” to use whichever your statement shows.

**Does this include new purchases?**
No. It assumes you stop adding to the balance. If you keep spending on the card, payoff takes longer.

**Is my data stored?**
No. The calculation runs in this tab.

## Luxembourg Salary Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/luxembourg-salary-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-12

Luxembourg Salary Calculator on Toolspire. Luxembourg gross to net salary calculator for 2026: income tax in class 1, 1a or 2, social contributions.

### At a glance

- Luxembourg Salary Calculator on Toolspire: converts a **gross salary in Luxembourg to net** — or net to gross — for **tax class 1, 1a or 2**, using the tax administration’s own withholding formulas for the scale in force since 1 January 2025.
- **€5,000 gross a month is €3,676.50 net in class 1** in 2026, €3,885.30 in class 1a and €4,155.80 in class 2.
- Uses the **2026 rates**: health insurance **3.05%**, pension insurance **8.5%** (8% until the end of 2025) and dependency insurance **1.4%**. Health and pension contributions stop at **€13,856.63** a month.
- Adds the **tax credits** paid through the payslip: CIS (up to €600 a year), CI-CO2 (up to €216 a year), the minimum-wage credit CISSM (up to €81 a month) and the single-parent credit CIM.
- Handles the **travel allowance** (€99 a year for each km beyond the first 4, up to €2,574), a **13th month**, a bonus and other tax-card deductions.
- Shows the **employer’s total cost**, your **marginal rate**, net pay per hour and net pay in all three tax classes side by side.
- Reproduces the tax administration’s published payslip example **to the cent** (€3,500 gross in class 2 → €3,085.88 net at the January 2025 rates). Nothing you type is uploaded.

### Features

- **Official formulas**: The monthly and annual withholding formulas published by the Administration des contributions directes, with their rounding rules.
- **Gross to net and back**: Enter a monthly or yearly gross — or the net you want — and get the other figure.
- **Every line of the payslip**: Health, pension and dependency insurance, tax, each tax credit and the net, per month and per year.
- **13th month and bonus**: See how much of a 13th month or bonus you actually keep once the year is settled.
- **Three tax classes compared**: A table of net pay in class 1, 1a and 2 from the minimum wage to €15,000 a month.
- **Employer cost**: Employer contributions for health, pension, accident insurance, health at work and the Mutualité.

### How to calculate your net salary in Luxembourg

1. **Enter your gross salary** — Type the monthly gross from your contract, or switch to **Yearly** for an annual figure. Choose **Net to gross** to start from the net you want.
2. **Pick your tax class** — Class 1 if you are single, 1a if you are a single parent, widowed or 64 or over, class 2 if you are married and taxed jointly. It is printed on your tax card.
3. **Add your situation** — Switch on the **13th month**, enter the distance from home to work, and open **More options** for a bonus, other deductions or part-time hours.
4. **Read the payslip** — See the net per month and per year, every deduction and credit, your marginal rate and what the job costs your employer.

### Guide

#### How net salary is calculated in Luxembourg

Three things come off a gross salary: **social contributions**, **income tax** withheld by the employer, and **dependency insurance**. Tax credits are then added back. Take **€5,000 gross a month in class 1**:

Health insurance is 3.05% (€152.50) and pension insurance 8.5% (€425.00). Both are deductible, so tax is worked out on 5,000 − 152.50 − 425.00 = €4,422.50, rounded down to **€4,420**. The class 1 formula for that bracket is 38% × 4,420 − 1,006.85 = €672.70, plus the 7% employment-fund surcharge: **€719.70**.

Dependency insurance is 1.4% of the gross above a quarter of the minimum wage: 1.4% × (5,000 − 692.83) = €60.30. Finally the CIS (€25.00) and CI-CO2 (€9.00) tax credits are added. **Net: 5,000 − 152.50 − 425.00 − 719.70 − 60.30 + 34.00 = €3,676.50.**

#### Net salary in Luxembourg by tax class (2026)

Monthly net for a full-time employee with no travel allowance, at the rates in force from 1 June 2026.

| Gross a month | Class 1 | Class 1a | Class 2 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| €2,771.33 (minimum wage) | €2,418.14 | €2,553.94 | €2,557.54 |
| €3,000 | €2,579.60 | €2,728.10 | €2,739.40 |
| €4,000 | €3,134.40 | €3,336.00 | €3,423.20 |
| €5,000 | €3,676.50 | €3,885.30 | €4,155.80 |
| €6,000 | €4,159.10 | €4,366.10 | €4,846.90 |
| €8,000 | €5,147.90 | €5,354.80 | €6,123.30 |
| €10,000 | €6,150.30 | €6,357.20 | €7,238.50 |

#### Social contributions in 2026

| Contribution | Employee | Employer | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Health insurance | 3.05% | 3.05% | 2.8% on a 13th month or bonus; up to €13,856.63 a month |
| Pension insurance | 8.5% | 8.5% | Up to €13,856.63 a month; 8% until 31 December 2025 |
| Dependency insurance | 1.4% | — | On gross above €692.83 a month, no ceiling, not tax-deductible |
| Accident insurance | — | 0.65% | Multiplied by the company’s bonus-malus factor |
| Health at work | — | 0.14% |  |
| Mutualité des employeurs | — | 0.23% – 2.66% | Four classes, by the company’s sick-leave record |

#### Tax classes 1, 1a and 2

- **Class 1** — single people without a child in the household.
- **Class 1a** — single parents with a child in the household, widowed people, and anyone aged 64 or over on 1 January. Tax starts at a higher salary than in class 1, then rises faster.
- **Class 2** — married couples taxed jointly, and for three years after being widowed, divorced or separated. The scale is applied to half the income and doubled, which is why class 2 pays far less at the same salary.
- Registered partners are withheld in their own class during the year and can ask for joint taxation in their tax return.
- Married non-residents are in class 1 unless they ask to be treated as residents; their card then shows a withholding rate instead of a class.

#### Tax credits on the payslip

- **CIS** (crédit d’impôt pour salariés) — €600 a year for a gross salary between €11,266 and €40,000, less above, nothing from €80,000.
- **CI-CO2** — €216 a year in 2026 up to €40,000 gross, tapering to nothing at €80,000. It offsets the CO2 tax.
- **CISSM** (minimum-wage credit) — €81 a month for a full-time gross of €1,800 to €3,000, tapering to nothing at €3,600; prorated for part-time work.
- **CIM** (single-parent credit) — €3,504 a year below €60,000 of taxable income, falling to €750 from €105,000. Class 1a only, on request.

#### What is not included

- Benefits in kind such as a company car, meal vouchers or housing, and the tax relief on overtime.
- A withholding rate printed on the tax card — a spouse’s second card, or a non-resident treated as a resident.
- The profit-sharing bonus and impatriate regimes, and deductions you claim only in a tax return.
- Maintenance payments, which reduce the single-parent credit.
- This is an estimate for information. Your payslip and the tax office’s assessment are what count.

### FAQ

**Where does Luxembourg Salary Calculator run?**
Luxembourg Salary Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How much is €5,000 gross net in Luxembourg?**
In 2026, **€5,000 gross a month is €3,676.50 net in tax class 1**, €3,885.30 in class 1a and €4,155.80 in class 2 — before any travel allowance, which adds a little more.

**What is the minimum wage in Luxembourg in 2026?**
From 1 June 2026 the minimum social wage is **€2,771.33 gross a month** for an unskilled worker aged 18 or over and **€3,325.60** for a skilled worker (full time, index 992.24). The unskilled minimum is about **€2,418 net** in class 1, helped by the €81 minimum-wage tax credit.

**Which tax class am I in?**
Class 1 if you are single without children; class 1a if you are a single parent, widowed, or 64 or over on 1 January; class 2 if you are married and taxed jointly. It is printed on the tax card your employer receives from the tax administration.

**How is a 13th month taxed?**
It is ordinary salary: it pays pension, dependency and 2.8% health insurance, and is taxed at your top rate because it comes on top of the year’s other pay. On €5,000 a month in class 1 you keep about €2,423 of a €5,000 13th month.

**Does this work for cross-border workers?**
Yes for the Luxembourg payslip: frontaliers pay the same contributions and the same withholding tax on salary earned in Luxembourg. Days worked from home beyond the treaty limit (34 a year for Belgium, France and Germany) are taxed in your country of residence instead, which this calculator does not model.

**Is the pension contribution 8% or 8.5%?**
It is **8.5%** for the employee and 8.5% for the employer since 1 January 2026. It was 8% each until the end of 2025.

**Why is my real payslip a little different?**
Usually because of benefits in kind, overtime, a different travel allowance or extra deductions on your tax card, or because the month had back pay or a bonus. Enter the distance and deductions from your card to get closer.

**Is my salary stored or sent anywhere?**
No. The calculation runs in this tab and nothing you type is uploaded.

## GPA & CGPA Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/gpa-cgpa-calculator/
