Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-13

GPA & CGPA Calculator on Toolspire: Calculate SGPA, GPA and CGPA from credits and grades, convert CGPA to percentage with your university’s formula.

### At a glance

- GPA & CGPA Calculator on Toolspire: calculates **SGPA (semester GPA)** from each course’s credits and grade: Σ(credits × grade points) ÷ Σ credits, rounded to two decimals.
- Calculates **CGPA** across semesters — credit-weighted when you enter credits — and reproduces the worked examples in the UGC’s credit-system guidelines.
- Supports the **UGC 10-point scale** (O = 10, A+ = 9, A = 8, B+ = 7, B = 6, C = 5, P = 4, F = 0), the **US 4.0 scale** with Honors and AP weighting, and a 5-point scale — or type grade points directly.
- Converts **CGPA to percentage** and back with the formula your university uses: CGPA × 9.5, CGPA × 10, (CGPA − 0.75) × 10, (CGPA − 0.5) × 10, 7.1 × CGPA + 11, or your own.
- An **8.2 CGPA** is 77.9% with CGPA × 9.5, 82% with CGPA × 10 and 74.5% with (CGPA − 0.75) × 10.
- **Target planner**: the average SGPA you need in the remaining semesters to reach a goal CGPA, and the highest CGPA still possible.
- Your courses and semesters are saved on your device only; nothing is uploaded.

### Features

- **SGPA from credits and grades**: Add your courses, pick a letter grade or type grade points, and see the working.
- **CGPA across semesters**: Enter each semester’s SGPA and credits — or send a finished semester over with one tap.
- **CGPA to percentage**: Formulas commonly used by CBSE, DU, VTU, AKTU, Anna, GTU, KTU, MAKAUT, JNTUH and Mumbai University — or your own.
- **Target CGPA planner**: Find the average you need from here, and whether the goal is still within reach.
- **US GPA too**: The 4.0 scale with plus and minus grades, and weighted GPA for Honors, AP and IB courses.
- **Saved for next semester**: Your entries stay in this tab, ready when the next results come out.

### How to calculate SGPA and CGPA

1. **Pick your grading scale** — Choose the 10-point scale used in India, the US 4.0 scale or a 5-point scale.
2. **Enter your courses** — For each course type the credits and choose the grade. If your university uses different letters, switch to **Grade points** and type the number from your marksheet.
3. **Read your SGPA** — The SGPA appears with the total credits and credit points. Tap **Add to CGPA** to carry it over as a semester.
4. **Get your CGPA** — On the **CGPA** tab enter the SGPA and credits of every semester to get your cumulative figure.
5. **Convert or plan** — Use **CGPA to %** with your university’s formula, or **Target** to see what you need in the semesters left.

### Guide

#### SGPA and CGPA formulas

**SGPA = Σ(credits × grade points) ÷ Σ credits** for the courses of one semester. Each course’s grade points are multiplied by its credits, so a 4-credit course counts twice as much as a 2-credit one.

**CGPA = Σ(semester credits × SGPA) ÷ Σ credits** over all semesters so far. The UGC’s own illustration: six semesters of 20, 20, 26, 26, 24 and 24 credits with SGPAs of 6.9, 6.7, 9.15, 7.38, 7.75 and 8.0 give (20 × 6.9 + 20 × 6.7 + 26 × 9.15 + 26 × 7.38 + 24 × 7.75 + 24 × 8.0) ÷ 140 = **7.71**.

| Course | Credits | Grade | Grade points | Credit points |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Mathematics | 4 | O | 10 | 40 |
| Physics | 4 | A+ | 9 | 36 |
| Programming | 3 | A | 8 | 24 |
| English | 2 | B+ | 7 | 14 |
| Physics lab | 2 | O | 10 | 20 |
| Total | 15 |  |  | 134 → SGPA 8.93 |

#### UGC 10-point grading scale

The letter grades and grade points the University Grants Commission recommends for the choice-based credit system. Universities set their own marks ranges for each grade.

| Letter grade | Grade points | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| O | 10 | Outstanding |
| A+ | 9 | Excellent |
| A | 8 | Very good |
| B+ | 7 | Good |
| B | 6 | Above average |
| C | 5 | Average |
| P | 4 | Pass |
| F | 0 | Fail |
| Ab | 0 | Absent |

#### CGPA to percentage formulas

There is no single national rule: each board and university publishes its own conversion, and some change it between schemes — KTU, for example, moved from 10 × CGPA − 2.5 to CGPA × 10 in 2023. The table shows the formulas commonly reported. **Use the one printed on your marksheet or in your university’s circular**; that is what employers and admission offices accept.

| Formula | Commonly used by | 8.2 CGPA becomes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CGPA × 9.5 | CBSE (Class 10 CGPA), Delhi University | 77.9% |
| CGPA × 10 | Anna University, KTU and many others | 82% |
| (CGPA − 0.75) × 10 | VTU, AKTU, MAKAUT | 74.5% |
| (CGPA − 0.5) × 10 | GTU, JNTU Hyderabad | 77% |
| 7.1 × CGPA + 11 | Mumbai University | 69.22% |
| 7.25 × CGPA + 11 | Mumbai University | 70.45% |

#### US GPA on the 4.0 scale

An A is 4.0, A− 3.7, B+ 3.3, B 3.0, B− 2.7, C+ 2.3, C 2.0, C− 1.7, D+ 1.3, D 1.0, D− 0.7 and F 0. GPA is the credit-weighted average, exactly like SGPA.

A **weighted GPA** adds 0.5 for an Honors course and 1.0 for an AP or IB course, so it can go above 4.0. Colleges usually look at both. Schools differ on the details — some count A+ as 4.3 — so check your school’s handbook.

#### SGPA, CGPA and GPA — what is the difference?

- **SGPA** (semester grade point average) covers the courses of one semester.
- **CGPA** (cumulative grade point average) covers every semester completed so far.
- **GPA** is the general term, and the one used in the US; a semester GPA is the same idea as an SGPA.
- Some universities call the same figures SPI and CPI, or GPA and CGPI.

#### Converting an Indian CGPA to a 4.0 GPA

There is no official conversion. Dividing by 10 and multiplying by 4 gives a rough figure — an 8.0 CGPA is about 3.2 — but US universities and credential evaluators such as WES convert course by course from your marks, so their figure can differ a good deal. When applying abroad, report your CGPA on its own scale unless the form asks otherwise.

### FAQ

**Where does GPA & CGPA Calculator run?**
GPA & CGPA Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I calculate SGPA?**
Multiply each course’s credits by its grade points, add those up, and divide by the total credits. Five courses worth 15 credits with 134 credit points give 134 ÷ 15 = **8.93**.

**How do I calculate CGPA from SGPA?**
Multiply each semester’s SGPA by its credits, add the results, and divide by the total credits. If every semester has the same credits, the CGPA is simply the average of the SGPAs.

**How do I convert CGPA to percentage?**
It depends on your university. Common formulas are **CGPA × 9.5** (CBSE, Delhi University), **CGPA × 10** (Anna University, KTU), **(CGPA − 0.75) × 10** (VTU, AKTU, MAKAUT) and **(CGPA − 0.5) × 10** (GTU, JNTUH). An 8.2 CGPA is 77.9%, 82%, 74.5% or 77% respectively — check your marksheet for the right one.

**Why is CGPA multiplied by 9.5?**
CBSE set 9.5 as the factor for the CGPA it printed on Class 10 marksheets, so a perfect 10 CGPA corresponds to 95%. Many colleges borrowed the same factor, but it is not a universal rule.

**What SGPA do I need to reach my target CGPA?**
Use the **Target** tab. For example, with a 7.5 CGPA after 80 credits, finishing on 8.0 after another 80 credits needs an average of 8.5 from here. If the answer is above 10, the target is out of reach and the calculator shows the best CGPA still possible.

**Does a failed course count in SGPA and CGPA?**
Yes — an F carries 0 grade points and its credits still count, which pulls the average down. Most universities replace it with the new grade once you clear the backlog; enter the grade that appears on your latest marksheet.

**What is the difference between weighted and unweighted GPA?**
An unweighted GPA treats every course the same, on a 4.0 scale. A weighted GPA adds 0.5 for Honors and 1.0 for AP or IB courses to reward harder classes, so it can exceed 4.0.

**Is my data stored?**
Only on your own device. Your courses, semesters and chosen formula are kept in this tab’s local storage so they are there next time; **Clear** removes them. Nothing is sent to a server.

## GST Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/gst-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-13

GST Calculator on Toolspire. GST calculator for India: add or remove GST at 5%, 18% or 40%, split CGST, SGST and IGST.

### At a glance

- GST Calculator on Toolspire: **Adds GST** to a price or **removes GST** from a price that already includes it, at the current slabs of **5%, 18% and 40%** — plus 0%, 0.25%, 1.5% and 3%, and the old 12% and 28% for earlier bills.
- Splits the tax into **CGST + SGST** for a sale within one state, or **IGST** for a sale to another state.
- **₹10,000 + 18% GST = ₹11,800** (CGST ₹900 + SGST ₹900). Taking 18% GST out of ₹10,000 leaves **₹8,474.58** before tax and ₹1,525.42 of GST.
- Totals a **bill with several items at different rates**, with a rate-wise summary of taxable value, CGST, SGST or IGST, and round-off — downloadable as CSV.
- Works out the **GST payable after input tax credit**: the tax on a sale less the tax already paid on the purchase.
- Has a **GST rate finder** for over 90 everyday goods and services, with the rates in force since **22 September 2025** (and tobacco at 40% since 1 February 2026).
- Shows the working and the amount in words. Everything runs in this tab; nothing is uploaded.

### Features

- **Add or remove GST**: From a price before tax to the total, or from an MRP or bill total back to the taxable value.
- **CGST, SGST, IGST**: Equal CGST and SGST for a sale in the same state, IGST across states — rounded to the paisa.
- **Current 2026 slabs**: 5%, 18% and 40% one tap away, with the special 3% for gold and the old 12% and 28% for past invoices.
- **Multi-item bill**: Up to 30 items, each with its own quantity, price and rate, prices with or without GST.
- **Input tax credit**: See the net GST a registered seller actually pays, and the profit on the deal.
- **Rate finder**: Search mobile, cement, gold, hotel, medicine or insurance and tap the rate to use it.

### How to calculate GST

1. **Choose add or remove** — Pick **Add GST** if your price is before tax, or **Remove GST** if the price already includes it.
2. **Enter the amount** — Type the price in rupees.
3. **Pick the GST rate** — Tap 5%, 18% or 40%, type any other rate, or search the **rate finder** below for your product.
4. **Choose the type of sale** — Keep **The same state** for CGST + SGST, or switch to **Different states** for IGST.
5. **Read the result** — See the taxable value, each tax and the total, with the working and the amount in words. Copy it, or build a full bill below.

### Guide

#### GST calculation formulas

**To add GST:** GST = price × rate ÷ 100, and total = price + GST. For ₹10,000 at 18%: GST = 10,000 × 18 ÷ 100 = ₹1,800, so the total is **₹11,800**.

**To remove GST (reverse calculation):** value before GST = total ÷ (1 + rate ÷ 100), and GST = total − value. For a ₹11,800 bill at 18%: 11,800 ÷ 1.18 = **₹10,000**, so the GST inside it is ₹1,800.

Do not simply take the percentage off the total. 18% of ₹11,800 is ₹2,124 — too much, because GST is charged on the value before tax, not on the total.

#### GST rates in 2026

Since 22 September 2025 GST has two main slabs, a merit rate of 5% and a standard rate of 18%, and a 40% rate for a short list of luxury and sin goods. The 12% and 28% slabs were abolished.

| GST rate | What it covers |
| --- | --- |
| Nil | Fresh food, milk, bread and roti, paneer, notebooks and pencils, individual health and life insurance, 36 life-saving medicines |
| 0.25% / 1.5% | Rough diamonds / cut and polished diamonds |
| 3% | Gold, silver, platinum and jewellery |
| 5% | Packaged food, butter and ghee, toothpaste, soap and shampoo, medicines, clothes and footwear up to ₹2,500, tractors, bicycles, hotel rooms up to ₹7,500, gyms and salons |
| 18% | The standard rate: mobile phones, laptops, TVs, air conditioners, cement, small cars, motorcycles up to 350 cc, and most services |
| 40% | Aerated and energy drinks, larger cars and SUVs, motorcycles above 350 cc, yachts, betting and casinos, cigarettes and pan masala |

#### How much of an inclusive price is GST?

When a price already includes GST, the tax is a smaller share of it than the rate suggests. Divide the price by the number in the last column to get the value before tax.

| GST rate | GST as a share of the inclusive price | Divide the price by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 3% | 2.91% | 1.03 |
| 5% | 4.76% | 1.05 |
| 12% (old) | 10.71% | 1.12 |
| 18% | 15.25% | 1.18 |
| 28% (old) | 21.88% | 1.28 |
| 40% | 28.57% | 1.40 |

#### CGST, SGST and IGST

- **CGST** (central GST) and **SGST** (state GST) apply together to a sale within one state. Each is half the rate: 18% GST is 9% CGST + 9% SGST.
- **UTGST** replaces SGST in union territories without a legislature, such as Chandigarh and Ladakh.
- **IGST** (integrated GST) applies to a sale from one state to another, and to imports. It is the full rate as one tax: 18% IGST.
- The total tax is the same either way — only where the money goes differs.

#### What changed with GST 2.0

The GST Council’s 56th meeting on 3 September 2025 replaced the four slabs of 5%, 12%, 18% and 28% with **5% and 18%**, plus **40%** for a few goods. Most 12% items moved to 5%, and most 28% items — air conditioners, large TVs, cement, small cars, motorcycles up to 350 cc — moved to 18%. The new rates apply from **22 September 2025**.

The **compensation cess** on cars, aerated drinks and coal ended on the same day, which is why larger cars are now a flat 40% instead of 28% plus cess. Cigarettes, pan masala and other tobacco products moved to 40% on **1 February 2026**, charged on the printed retail price, with a separate excise duty or health cess on top.

#### Input tax credit

A GST-registered business does not pay the whole GST it collects. It deducts the GST it paid on its own purchases — the **input tax credit** — and pays the difference. Buy goods for ₹10,000 + ₹1,800 GST and sell them for ₹12,000 + ₹2,160 GST: the tax to pay is 2,160 − 1,800 = **₹360**, which is 18% of the ₹2,000 of value added.

### FAQ

**Where does GST Calculator run?**
GST Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I calculate GST on an amount?**
Multiply the amount by the GST rate and divide by 100. For ₹5,000 at 18%: 5,000 × 18 ÷ 100 = ₹900 of GST, so the total is ₹5,900. Within a state that is ₹450 CGST + ₹450 SGST.

**How do I remove GST from a total?**
Divide the total by 1 + the rate: by **1.05** for 5%, **1.18** for 18%, **1.40** for 40%. A ₹5,900 bill at 18% is 5,900 ÷ 1.18 = ₹5,000 before GST, with ₹900 of tax. Choose **Remove GST** above to do it automatically.

**What are the GST rates in 2026?**
The main slabs are **5%** and **18%**, with **40%** on a short list of luxury and sin goods. Essentials are nil-rated, gold and silver are 3%, and diamonds 0.25% or 1.5%. The 12% and 28% slabs ended on 22 September 2025.

**Is 12% or 28% GST still charged?**
Not on new sales. Those slabs were abolished on 22 September 2025 — tobacco products stayed at 28% plus cess until 1 February 2026 and are now 40%. Both rates are still offered here so you can check older invoices.

**What is the difference between CGST, SGST and IGST?**
CGST and SGST are charged together, half the rate each, when buyer and seller are in the same state. IGST is charged alone, at the full rate, when they are in different states or the goods are imported.

**What is the GST on mobile phones, gold and cars?**
Mobile phones are **18%**. Gold and silver jewellery is **3%**, with 5% on making charges. Small cars are **18%**; larger cars and SUVs are **40%**; electric vehicles are 5%. Search the rate finder above for other items.

**How is GST rounded?**
Each tax is rounded to the nearest paisa, and CGST and SGST are always equal — so when GST is taken out of a price within a state, the value before tax can sit one paisa below price ÷ (1 + rate). Many businesses then round the bill total to the nearest rupee: switch on **Round off the total** in the bill section.

**Is anything I enter stored or uploaded?**
No. The calculation happens in this tab and nothing is sent to a server.

## Attendance Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/attendance-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-15

Your attendance %, how many classes you can skip or must attend to reach 75%, subject-wise tracking and a term planner.

### At a glance

- Attendance Calculator on Toolspire: attendance % = classes attended ÷ classes held × 100. The calculator gives the **exact number of classes to attend in a row** to reach your minimum, or **how many you can miss** and stay at it.
- At **75%**, each class you miss has to be balanced by three you attend: 30 of 50 (60%) needs **30 more in a row**, while 45 of 50 (90%) can miss **10**.
- Works with any minimum – 75% (CBSE board exams and many colleges), 70% per subject (law, under the BCI rules), 75% theory and 80% practical (MBBS) – using exact whole-number arithmetic, so 75% of 40 is exactly 30.
- An option for colleges that **round to the nearest whole percent**, where 74.5% counts as 75%.
- Counts approved **medical, duty or OD leave** as present if your college does, and with the classes left this term says how many you must attend – or that the minimum can no longer be reached.
- **Subject-wise tracker** with one-tap Present and Absent buttons and Undo, the overall percentage, the subjects below the minimum and a CSV download.
- **Term planner**: enter classes per weekday, holidays and the last day of classes, then tick the days you plan to miss to see your percentage after every day. Everything is saved in this tab only.

### Features

- **The answer students want**: “You can miss the next 4 classes” or “Attend the next 12 in a row” – with the percentage before and after, and the working.
- **Every subject at a glance**: One row per subject with its percentage, a bar against your minimum and what it allows, plus the overall figure most colleges also check.
- **Tap after each class**: Present and Absent buttons keep the numbers current without retyping them; Undo fixes a wrong tap.
- **Plan the whole term**: From your timetable and holidays it lists every class day left, so you can see which days you can afford to miss – and which you cannot.
- **Any rule**: Set 60, 75, 80, 85% or any figure, and choose exact or rounded comparison to match your college’s handbook.
- **Private**: No account and no upload: your numbers stay in this browser for the next time you check.

### How to calculate attendance and the classes you can skip

1. **Set your minimum** — Type your college’s requirement or tap 75%, 80% and so on. Choose **Rounded %** if your college rounds to whole percent.
2. **Enter your classes** — On **Calculator**, type the classes held and attended (or missed). Add leave counted as present and the classes left this term if you know them.
3. **Read the answer** — See your percentage, how many classes you can miss or must attend in a row, and the what-if table.
4. **Track each subject** — On **Subjects**, enter each subject’s numbers once, then tap **Present** or **Absent** after every class.
5. **Plan the term** — On **Term planner**, fill in your weekly timetable, holidays and the last day of classes, and tick the days you plan to miss.

### Guide

#### Attendance formulas

**Attendance % = attended ÷ held × 100.** To find how many classes you must attend in a row to reach a minimum p (as a fraction, 75% = 0.75), solve (attended + x) ÷ (held + x) ≥ p, which gives **x ≥ (p × held − attended) ÷ (1 − p)**, rounded up.

To find how many you can miss and still have p, solve attended ÷ (held + y) ≥ p, which gives **y ≤ attended ÷ p − held**, rounded down.

| You have | Attendance | At 75% | At 80% |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 30 of 50 | 60% | attend the next 30 | attend the next 50 |
| 36 of 50 | 72% | attend the next 6 | attend the next 20 |
| 40 of 50 | 80% | can miss 3 | can miss 0 |
| 45 of 50 | 90% | can miss 10 | can miss 6 |
| 54 of 60 | 90% | can miss 12 | can miss 7 |

#### How many classes can I miss?

The minimum decides how many classes you must attend for every one you miss. Over a whole term this is the largest share of classes you can miss:

| Minimum | Attend for every class missed | Most you can miss |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 60% | 1.5 | 40% of classes |
| 65% | 1.86 | 35% of classes |
| 70% | 2.33 | 30% of classes |
| 75% | 3 | 25% – 1 in 4 |
| 80% | 4 | 20% – 1 in 5 |
| 85% | 5.67 | 15% of classes |
| 90% | 9 | 10% – 1 in 10 |

#### Attendance rules you may meet

Colleges and universities set their own rules on top of these, often per subject and overall, and some count labs as two or more periods. Check your handbook – and count periods the same way it does.

| Where | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| CBSE Class 10 and 12 | 75% to sit the board exams (Examination Bye-Laws); shortages of up to 25% can be condoned for medical emergencies, national or international sport and other serious reasons, with documents |
| MBBS in India (NMC) | 75% in theory and 80% in practical and clinical training |
| Law degrees in India (BCI) | 70% in each subject; a college may condone down to 65% in a subject if the overall attendance is 70% |
| Schools in the United States | missing 10% or more of school days, excused or not, is “chronic absenteeism” |
| Schools in England | missing 10% or more of sessions (half days) is “persistent absence” |

#### If you are short of attendance

- Act early: every week left in the term is classes that can still pull the percentage up.
- Attend everything for a while – the What if table shows how fast the figure climbs.
- Get approved medical, sports or duty leave on record now, with documents; it is much harder afterwards.
- Ask how condonation works at your college: who decides, how far below the minimum is allowed, and the deadline.
- Watch each subject, not just the overall figure – one short subject can stop you sitting its exam.

### FAQ

**Where does Attendance Calculator run?**
Attendance Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I calculate my attendance percentage?**
Divide the classes you attended by the classes held and multiply by 100. For example, 42 classes attended out of 56 held is 42 ÷ 56 × 100 = **75%**.

**How many classes can I miss and still have 75%?**
Divide the classes you have attended by 0.75 and subtract the classes held so far, then round down. With 45 of 50, that is 45 ÷ 0.75 − 50 = **10** classes. Enter your numbers above to get yours.

**How many classes do I need to attend to reach 75%?**
Use (0.75 × held − attended) ÷ 0.25, rounded up. With 30 of 50 (60%), you need (37.5 − 30) ÷ 0.25 = **30** classes in a row, which takes you to 60 of 80 = 75%.

**Does medical leave count as attendance?**
It depends on your institution. Many colleges add approved duty or on-duty (OD) leave to attendance, and some count medical leave or allow it when deciding condonation. Enter such classes under **Leave counted as present** only if your college counts them.

**Is the 75% rule per subject or overall?**
Often both: many universities require the minimum in each subject and overall, and a strong overall figure can hide one subject that is short. Use the **Subjects** tab to see every subject against the minimum.

**What if I can’t reach 75% this term?**
The calculator tells you when even attending every remaining class is not enough. Speak to your teacher or department straight away about medical or duty leave and condonation – most rules allow some relaxation only with documents and before a deadline.

**Are my numbers saved?**
Yes, in this browser on this device, so they are there next time. Nothing is sent anywhere, and you can clear them at any time.

## Car Loan EMI Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/car-loan-emi-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-16

Car loan EMI calculator for India: EMI on the on-road price, the real cost of a flat rate, processing fee and APR, tenure comparison and early closure.

### At a glance

- Car Loan EMI Calculator on Toolspire: starts from the **on-road price**: ex-showroom price + RTO and road tax + insurance + accessories, less your down payment.
- Example: a ₹9.2 lakh loan at 9% for 5 years has an EMI of **₹19,098** and total interest of **₹2,25,861**.
- Converts a **flat rate** to the reducing-balance rate it really is: **7% flat for 5 years costs 12.50% a year**, 8% flat costs 14.13%.
- Adds the **processing fee with 18% GST** to the cost and shows the true yearly cost (APR) – a 0.5% fee lifts 9% to about 9.25%.
- **Compares 3- to 7-year loans** side by side: at 9%, stretching ₹9.2 lakh from 3 to 7 years halves the EMI but raises the interest from ₹1.33 lakh to ₹3.23 lakh.
- **Early closure**: the outstanding amount, the foreclosure charge with GST and what you save. RBI rules bar pre-payment charges on floating-rate loans to individuals sanctioned or renewed from 1 January 2026.
- Checks the **20/4/10 rule of thumb** and builds a full year-by-year and month-by-month schedule. Nothing you enter leaves your browser.

### Features

- **The whole price, not just the sticker**: Road tax, insurance and accessories add a good deal to the ex-showroom price. Enter them once and the loan follows.
- **See through flat rates**: A flat rate charges interest on the full loan for the whole term, so it looks about half as expensive as it is. The calculator shows the real rate to compare.
- **Fees count too**: The processing fee and its GST are added to the cost and folded into an APR, so offers with different fees compare fairly.
- **Pick a sensible tenure**: A table of 3- to 7-year loans shows how a smaller EMI trades against more interest. Tap a row to switch to it.
- **Plan an early close**: Choose when you might pay off the loan and the charge your lender takes, and see whether it is worth it.
- **Private**: Everything is worked out on your device. Your last figures are kept in this browser for next time.

### How to calculate your car loan EMI

1. **Enter the on-road price** — Type the ex-showroom price, RTO and road tax, insurance and accessories from the dealer’s quotation.
2. **Set your down payment** — Type an amount or tap 10%, 20% or 30%; the loan amount updates.
3. **Enter the rate and tenure** — Choose **Reducing balance** for a bank-quoted rate or **Flat** for a dealer’s flat rate, then pick 3–7 years.
4. **Add the processing fee** — Enter the fee as a % of the loan; GST is added at 18%.
5. **Read the result** — See the EMI, total interest, APR and total cost, compare tenures, and check what closing early would save.

### Guide

#### Car loan EMI formula

Banks work out a car loan EMI on the reducing balance: **EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1)**, where P is the loan, r the monthly rate (annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100) and n the number of months. Each EMI pays the month’s interest on what you still owe, and the rest reduces the loan.

With a **flat rate**, interest is worked out on the full loan for the whole term – P × rate × years – and added to the loan before dividing by the months. Because you are paying interest on money you have already repaid, a flat rate costs much more than the same number on a reducing balance.

| Flat rate | Tenure | Equivalent reducing rate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 6% | 3 years | 11.08% |
| 7% | 5 years | 12.50% |
| 7% | 7 years | 12.16% |
| 8% | 5 years | 14.13% |
| 9% | 5 years | 15.71% |

#### EMI for ₹9.2 lakh at 9% by tenure

A car loses value every year, so a long loan can leave you owing more than the car is worth. Keeping the tenure short keeps interest down and the loan below the car’s value.

| Tenure | EMI | Total interest |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 3 years | ₹29,256 | ₹1,33,207 |
| 4 years | ₹22,894 | ₹1,78,923 |
| 5 years | ₹19,098 | ₹2,25,861 |
| 6 years | ₹16,583 | ₹2,74,012 |
| 7 years | ₹14,802 | ₹3,23,364 |

#### The 20/4/10 rule

A popular rule of thumb for buying a car: put down at least **20%**, borrow for no more than **4 years**, and keep the EMI – ideally all car costs, including fuel and insurance – within **10%** of your income. It is a guide rather than a rule, but it keeps a car affordable for the whole loan.

### FAQ

**Where does Car Loan EMI Calculator run?**
Car Loan EMI Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How is car loan EMI calculated?**
With the reducing-balance formula EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1), where P is the loan amount, r the monthly interest rate and n the number of months. For ₹9.2 lakh at 9% a year for 60 months, r = 0.0075 and the EMI is ₹19,098.

**Is a flat interest rate cheaper?**
No – it only looks cheaper. A flat rate charges interest on the original loan for the whole term even as you repay it. A 7% flat rate over 5 years costs the same as 12.5% a year on a reducing balance. Choose **Flat** in the calculator to see the equivalent rate before you compare offers.

**Is the loan on the ex-showroom or the on-road price?**
Lenders fund a share of the car’s price – some of the ex-showroom price, others of the on-road price – and you pay the rest as a down payment. This calculator starts from the on-road price, the total you actually pay, so the down payment covers whatever the loan does not.

**Can I close my car loan early?**
Usually yes, after a minimum number of EMIs set by your lender, sometimes with a foreclosure charge on the outstanding amount plus GST. Car loans are often fixed-rate, so check your sanction letter. Under RBI’s pre-payment directions, floating-rate loans to individuals for non-business purposes sanctioned or renewed from 1 January 2026 carry no pre-payment charge.

**What is a good tenure for a car loan?**
Shorter is cheaper: the table shows that the same loan costs about 2.4 times as much interest over 7 years as over 3. Many people pick the shortest tenure whose EMI fits comfortably in their budget – often 4 or 5 years.

**Does the processing fee matter?**
Yes. A fee paid upfront means you borrow less than you repay on, which raises the true cost. The calculator adds 18% GST to the fee and shows the APR, so a lower rate with a high fee can be compared fairly with a higher rate and no fee.

## Home Loan EMI Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/home-loan-emi-calculator/

Last updated: 2026-10-16

Home Loan EMI Calculator on Toolspire: EMI, interest and schedule, prepayments that cut years off, rate changes, RBI LTV limits.

### At a glance

- Home Loan EMI Calculator on Toolspire: example: a **₹50 lakh** loan at **8.5%** for **20 years** has an EMI of **₹43,391** and total interest of **₹54,13,879** – more than the loan itself.
- **Prepayments**: an extra ₹1 lakh once a year on that loan saves **₹18.55 lakh** of interest and closes it **6 years** sooner; ₹5,000 more every month saves ₹13.89 lakh.
- Choose whether a prepayment **shortens the tenure** or **lowers the EMI**, and add one-time prepayments such as a bonus.
- **Floating rates**: a rise from 8.5% to 9.5% after 3 years keeps the EMI but adds 3 years 3 months and ₹16.9 lakh of interest – or raises the EMI to ₹46,265 to finish on time.
- Checks **RBI’s loan-to-value limits** – 90% for loans up to ₹30 lakh, 80% up to ₹75 lakh, 75% above – and shows the largest loan the property allows.
- **Tax benefit** (old regime): interest up to ₹2 lakh and principal within ₹1.5 lakh of 80C a year, per co-owner borrower – ₹93,448 saved in year 1 at the 30% slab. The new regime gives none for a self-occupied home.
- **Eligibility** from income and other EMIs, a year-by-year schedule with prepayments and a CSV download. Nothing you enter leaves your browser.

### Features

- **More than an EMI**: Loan, rate, tenure and processing fee give the EMI, the APR with the fee and the full repayment schedule.
- **Prepayment planner**: Monthly, yearly and one-time prepayments together, with the interest saved and the years cut – the best lever most borrowers have.
- **Rates that move**: Most home loans float with the repo rate. See what a change does to the tenure or the EMI.
- **Within RBI limits**: The loan is checked against the loan-to-value ceilings, with the largest loan the price allows one tap away.
- **Tax saved, year by year**: Interest and principal deductions are capped exactly, doubled for two co-owner borrowers, and priced at your slab plus cess.
- **Borrow what you can repay**: Enter your income and other EMIs to see the loan lenders are likely to offer.

### How to calculate your home loan EMI

1. **Enter the price and loan** — Type the property price and the loan amount; the share of the price and RBI’s limit appear below.
2. **Set the rate and tenure** — Enter the interest rate and choose a tenure from 10 to 30 years, and the processing fee if any.
3. **Add prepayments** — Add an extra amount every month or year, or one-time prepayments, and choose whether they shorten the tenure or lower the EMI.
4. **Try a rate change** — Switch on **See a rate change** to see a floating rate move after a few years.
5. **Check tax and eligibility** — Pick your regime and slab for the tax saved, and enter your income to see the loan it supports.

### Guide

#### EMI on ₹50 lakh at 8.5% by tenure

Going from 20 to 30 years lowers the EMI by under ₹5,000 but adds over ₹34 lakh of interest. A longer tenure with regular prepayments keeps the EMI flexible while still cutting the cost.

| Tenure | EMI | Total interest |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 10 years | ₹61,993 | ₹24,39,141 |
| 15 years | ₹49,237 | ₹38,62,656 |
| 20 years | ₹43,391 | ₹54,13,879 |
| 25 years | ₹40,261 | ₹70,78,406 |
| 30 years | ₹38,446 | ₹88,40,443 |

#### Reduce the EMI or the tenure?

When you prepay, lenders usually let you keep the EMI and finish sooner, or keep the end date and pay a smaller EMI. **Shortening the tenure saves far more interest**, because the whole EMI keeps working against a smaller balance. Lowering the EMI makes sense when you need breathing space in the monthly budget.

Under RBI’s pre-payment directions, floating-rate loans to individuals for non-business purposes sanctioned or renewed from 1 January 2026 cannot carry pre-payment charges, whatever the source of the money.

#### RBI loan-to-value limits

The rest is your down payment (margin). Stamp duty and registration are usually not counted in the property value for this, so plan for them separately.

| Loan amount | Largest loan as a share of the property value |
| --- | --- |
| Up to ₹30 lakh | 90% |
| Above ₹30 lakh up to ₹75 lakh | 80% |
| Above ₹75 lakh | 75% |

#### Home loan tax benefits

Each co-owner who also pays the EMIs can claim up to the limits separately. The Income-tax Act, 2025, in force from 1 April 2026, keeps these deductions under new section numbers.

| Deduction | Old regime | New regime |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Interest, self-occupied home (section 24(b)) | up to ₹2 lakh a year | not allowed |
| Principal repaid (section 80C) | within the ₹1.5 lakh 80C limit | not allowed |
| Interest, let-out home | against the rent; a loss up to ₹2 lakh can reduce other income | against that home’s rent only |

### FAQ

**Where does Home Loan EMI Calculator run?**
Home Loan EMI Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How is home loan EMI calculated?**
EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1), where P is the loan, r the monthly interest rate (annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100) and n the number of months. ₹50 lakh at 8.5% for 240 months gives ₹43,391.

**Is it better to prepay or invest?**
Prepaying saves the loan’s interest rate with certainty, after tax. If you are in the old regime and still within the ₹2 lakh interest deduction, the saving is a little lower. Many borrowers do both – keep an emergency fund and investments going, and prepay windfalls such as bonuses.

**Should I reduce my EMI or tenure after prepaying?**
Reducing the tenure saves much more interest; reducing the EMI eases your monthly budget. Switch between **Shorten the tenure** and **Lower the EMI** in the calculator to see the difference for your loan.

**How much home loan can I get on my salary?**
Lenders typically let all your EMIs together take up 40–60% of your take-home pay. With ₹1 lakh a month and no other EMIs, 50% allows an EMI of ₹50,000 – about ₹57.6 lakh at 8.5% for 20 years. The property’s loan-to-value limit can cap it further.

**Can I claim home loan benefits in the new tax regime?**
Not for a home you live in: the new regime allows neither the interest deduction nor 80C for principal. For a let-out home, interest can still be set off against that home’s rental income.

**What happens when the repo rate changes?**
Repo-linked loans reset at intervals set in your agreement. Lenders usually keep the EMI and change the tenure; you can ask to change the EMI instead. Use **See a rate change** to compare both.

## Income Tax Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/income-tax-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-16

Income tax calculator for India, FY 2026-27 and 2025-26: compare the new and old regimes with HRA, 80C, 80D, NPS and home loan, slab by slab.

### At a glance

- Income Tax Calculator on Toolspire: new-regime slabs for **FY 2026-27**, unchanged by Budget 2026: nil up to ₹4 lakh, then 5%, 10%, 15%, 20% and 25% in steps of ₹4 lakh, and **30% above ₹24 lakh**.
- No tax up to **₹12 lakh** of taxable income in the new regime thanks to the rebate (section 87A, section 156 of the 2025 Act) – a salary of **₹12.75 lakh** after the ₹75,000 standard deduction – with marginal relief just above.
- Old regime: nil up to ₹2.5 lakh (₹3 lakh at 60–79, ₹5 lakh at 80+), then 5%, 20% and 30%, with a rebate up to ₹5 lakh and a ₹50,000 standard deduction.
- **HRA** is the least of the HRA received, rent minus 10% of basic, and 50% of basic in metro cities (40% elsewhere). From FY 2026-27, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Pune and Ahmedabad join Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata and Chennai at 50%.
- Old-regime deductions with their limits: 80C ₹1.5 lakh, NPS 80CCD(1B) ₹50,000, 80D, 80E, 80G, 80TTA/80TTB and home-loan interest up to ₹2 lakh. The new regime allows the employer’s NPS contribution up to 14% of basic.
- Surcharge of 10%, 15% and 25% (37% in the old regime above ₹5 crore) **with marginal relief**, and 4% health and education cess.
- Shows which regime is cheaper, by how much, and the **extra deductions the old regime would need** to break even. Everything stays in this tab.

### Features

- **Both regimes side by side**: Every figure is worked out under the new and the old regime at once, with the cheaper one marked and the difference in rupees.
- **The full working**: Gross income, exemptions, deductions, taxable income, slab-by-slab tax, rebate, marginal relief, surcharge and cess – line by line.
- **HRA done properly**: The three-way HRA rule with the right city rate for the year, including the four cities added to the 50% list from 2026-27.
- **Monthly or yearly**: Type your salary per month or per year, and see the tax per month to compare with the TDS on your payslip.
- **Break-even deductions**: If the new regime wins, see how much more you would need to invest or claim before the old regime would pay off.
- **Private**: Your salary and deductions stay on this device; they are kept in this browser for next time.

### How to calculate your income tax

1. **Choose the year and your age** — Pick FY 2026-27 or FY 2025-26, and your age group for the old-regime slabs.
2. **Enter your salary** — Type your gross salary, basic pay, HRA received and rent paid – per month or per year – and your city.
3. **Add other income** — Add interest, other income and any house property: home-loan interest, or rent from a let-out home.
4. **Add deductions** — Enter 80C, NPS, health insurance and other deductions; they count only in the old regime.
5. **Compare** — Read the tax under both regimes, which one is lower, and the full breakdown slab by slab.

### Guide

#### New tax regime slabs, FY 2025-26 and 2026-27

A rebate of up to ₹60,000 makes the tax nil when taxable income is ₹12 lakh or less. Just above ₹12 lakh, marginal relief caps the tax at the income above ₹12 lakh, until about ₹12.7 lakh.

| Taxable income | Rate |
| --- | --- |
| Up to ₹4,00,000 | Nil |
| ₹4,00,001 – ₹8,00,000 | 5% |
| ₹8,00,001 – ₹12,00,000 | 10% |
| ₹12,00,001 – ₹16,00,000 | 15% |
| ₹16,00,001 – ₹20,00,000 | 20% |
| ₹20,00,001 – ₹24,00,000 | 25% |
| Above ₹24,00,000 | 30% |

#### Old tax regime slabs

A rebate of up to ₹12,500 makes the tax nil when taxable income is ₹5 lakh or less.

| Taxable income | Below 60 | 60 to 79 | 80 and over |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Up to ₹2,50,000 | Nil | Nil | Nil |
| ₹2,50,001 – ₹3,00,000 | 5% | Nil | Nil |
| ₹3,00,001 – ₹5,00,000 | 5% | 5% | Nil |
| ₹5,00,001 – ₹10,00,000 | 20% | 20% | 20% |
| Above ₹10,00,000 | 30% | 30% | 30% |

#### New or old regime: tax on salary

With cess, for a resident under 60 with only salary income, using the standard deduction, ₹1.5 lakh under 80C and ₹25,000 under 80D in the old regime. Large HRA and home-loan claims can tip the balance – enter yours above.

| Salary | New regime | Old regime with ₹1.75 lakh of deductions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ₹8 lakh | ₹0 | ₹28,600 |
| ₹10 lakh | ₹0 | ₹70,200 |
| ₹12.75 lakh | ₹0 | ₹1,32,600 |
| ₹15 lakh | ₹97,500 | ₹2,02,800 |
| ₹20 lakh | ₹1,92,400 | ₹3,58,800 |
| ₹25 lakh | ₹3,19,800 | ₹5,14,800 |
| ₹30 lakh | ₹4,75,800 | ₹6,70,800 |

#### What changed in 2026

The **Income-tax Act, 2025** replaced the 1961 Act from 1 April 2026. It speaks of a single “tax year” instead of the previous year and assessment year, and renumbers the sections – the 87A rebate, for example, is now section 156 – but Budget 2026 kept the rates, the ₹12 lakh rebate limit and the ₹75,000 standard deduction.

Under the new Income-tax Rules, 2026, HRA in Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Pune and Ahmedabad is worked out at 50% of salary, like Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata and Chennai. For FY 2025-26 those four cities stay at 40%.

### FAQ

**Where does Income Tax Calculator run?**
Income Tax Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Is income up to ₹12 lakh tax-free?**
In the new regime, yes: if your taxable income is ₹12 lakh or less, the rebate wipes out the tax. For salaried people, the ₹75,000 standard deduction makes a salary of up to ₹12.75 lakh tax-free. The rebate does not apply to income taxed at special rates, such as capital gains on shares.

**Which tax regime is better for me?**
It depends on your deductions. The new regime has lower rates but few deductions; the old one rewards large claims such as HRA, 80C, 80D and home-loan interest. Enter your figures and the calculator shows both totals, the cheaper regime and how much more you would need to claim for the old one to win.

**How is HRA exemption calculated?**
It is the least of three amounts: the HRA you receive; the rent you pay minus 10% of basic pay and DA; and 50% of basic and DA in Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata and Chennai – plus Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Pune and Ahmedabad from 2026-27 – or 40% elsewhere. It is available only in the old regime.

**Can I switch between the old and new regimes?**
If you have no business income, you can choose either regime every year when you file your return. Tell your employer your choice so that TDS is deducted correctly; the new regime applies if you do not.

**What is marginal relief?**
It stops a small rise in income from causing a bigger rise in tax. Just above ₹12 lakh in the new regime, your tax cannot be more than the income above ₹12 lakh. The same idea applies at the surcharge thresholds of ₹50 lakh, ₹1 crore, ₹2 crore and ₹5 crore.

**Does this calculator include capital gains?**
No. It covers salary, pension, house property, interest and other income taxed at slab rates for resident individuals. Gains on shares and mutual funds are taxed at special rates with their own rules, so add them separately or ask a tax professional.

## Student Loan Payoff & Refinance Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/student-loan-payoff-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-16

Student Loan Payoff & Refinance Calculator on Toolspire: Student loan payoff calculator: add every loan, pay extra with the avalanche or snowball method.

### At a glance

- Student Loan Payoff & Refinance Calculator on Toolspire: handles up to 15 loans, each with its own balance, APR and monthly payment, federal or private.
- Example: $40,000 across three loans at 5–9% on 10-year payments costs **$16,417** in interest; **$200 a month extra** with the avalanche method cuts that to **$9,073** and finishes **47 months sooner**.
- **Avalanche** puts the extra on the highest-rate loan first and always costs the least interest; **snowball** clears the smallest balance first. When a loan is paid off, its payment rolls on to the next.
- Shows your **debt-free date**, the month each loan is paid off and a combined year-by-year schedule you can download as CSV.
- **Refinance**: choose which loans to refinance and compare a new rate, term and fees with keeping them – monthly payment, total interest, lifetime saving and the months to recover any fees.
- Warns when federal loans are included: refinancing them with a private lender gives up income-driven repayment, Public Service Loan Forgiveness and federal deferment and forbearance.
- Interest is worked out monthly at APR ÷ 12. Nothing you enter leaves your browser.

### Features

- **All your loans together**: Federal and private loans with different rates and payments, in one plan with one debt-free date.
- **Avalanche or snowball**: Compare the interest-saving and the quick-wins approach on your own numbers.
- **Every extra dollar counted**: Add an extra monthly amount and see the interest saved and the months cut, loan by loan.
- **Honest refinancing**: A lower payment can still cost more over a longer term. The calculator shows both, with fees included.
- **Federal protections flagged**: Refinancing federal loans privately is permanent; the calculator says what you would give up.
- **Private**: No account, no credit check and no data sent anywhere.

### How to plan your student loan payoff

1. **List your loans** — Enter each loan’s balance, APR and monthly payment from your servicer, and tick **Federal** for federal loans.
2. **Add an extra payment** — Type how much more you can pay each month, or tap a preset.
3. **Pick a method** — Choose **Highest rate** (avalanche) to save the most, or **Smallest balance** (snowball) for quick wins.
4. **Read your plan** — See your debt-free date, total interest, what you save and when each loan is paid off.
5. **Test a refinance** — On **Refinance**, tick the loans, enter the offer’s APR, term and fees, and compare.

### Guide

#### Avalanche or snowball?

Both methods pay every loan its normal payment and send any extra money to one loan at a time. **Avalanche** targets the highest interest rate, which is mathematically the cheapest. **Snowball** targets the smallest balance, so the first loan disappears sooner – a boost that helps some people keep going. On the example below the difference is small, so the best method is the one you will stick to.

| Extra a month | Debt-free in (avalanche) | Interest (avalanche) | Interest (snowball) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $0 | about 10 years | $16,417 | $16,417 |
| $100 | 7 years 7 months | $11,568 | $12,430 |
| $200 | 6 years 2 months | $9,073 | $9,842 |
| $500 | 4 years | $5,604 | $6,097 |

#### When refinancing makes sense

- You have **private loans** at a high rate and can qualify for a clearly lower one – the example’s $15,000 private loan at 9% costs $7,802 in interest over 10 years, but $4,535 at 5.5%.
- You will **keep or shorten the term**: stretching the term lowers the payment but can raise the total interest even at a lower rate.
- Any **fees** are repaid by the lower payments well before the loan ends.
- You do **not** need federal benefits – income-driven repayment, Public Service Loan Forgiveness, generous deferment and forbearance. Refinancing federal loans privately ends them for good.

### FAQ

**Where does Student Loan Payoff & Refinance Calculator run?**
Student Loan Payoff & Refinance Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I pay off student loans faster?**
Pay more than the minimum and direct the extra to one loan at a time – the highest-rate loan saves the most interest. When that loan is gone, add its payment to the next. Ask your servicer to apply extra payments to principal on the loan you choose, not to future payments.

**Is it better to pay off the highest interest or the smallest balance first?**
Highest interest first (avalanche) always costs least. Smallest balance first (snowball) gives earlier wins. Try both in the calculator: with similar rates the difference is often a few hundred dollars.

**Should I refinance my federal student loans?**
Only after weighing what you give up. A private refinance can lower the rate, but the loans lose federal protections such as income-driven repayment, Public Service Loan Forgiveness and federal deferment and forbearance, and that cannot be reversed. Many borrowers refinance only their private loans.

**Does refinancing always save money?**
No. A lower rate over a longer term can cost more in total interest, and fees add to the cost. The calculator compares the lifetime cost of keeping your loans with the offer, fees included.

**How is student loan interest calculated?**
Most US student loans charge simple daily interest; this calculator uses the monthly equivalent, APR ÷ 12 on the balance, which gives very close results. Your servicer’s figures may differ by small amounts because of day counts and payment dates.

## Mortgage Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/mortgage-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-16

Mortgage Calculator on Toolspire: US mortgage calculator with PMI, property tax, home insurance and HOA: monthly payment, when PMI ends.

### At a glance

- Mortgage Calculator on Toolspire: adds up the whole payment – **principal and interest, property tax, homeowners insurance, PMI and HOA** (PITI) – and shows each part.
- Example: a **$400,000** home with **20% down** at **6.5%** for 30 years has a principal and interest payment of **$2,022.62**, and **$408,142** of interest over the loan.
- **PMI** applies with less than 20% down. Under the Homeowners Protection Act you can ask to cancel it at **80%** of the original value; it ends automatically at **78%** on the original schedule, and at the latest after the loan’s midpoint.
- With 10% down on that home and PMI at 0.5%, PMI of **$150 a month** is paid for **7 years 11 months** if you ask to cancel at 80%, or 9 years 1 month until it ends automatically – **$14,250** against $16,350.
- **Extra payments**: $200 a month more on the $320,000 loan saves **$105,429** of interest and pays it off **6 years 7 months** sooner – and lets you ask to drop PMI sooner.
- Checks the **28/36 guideline** and estimates the home price your income supports. Property tax can be a rate or a yearly amount.
- Full amortization schedule with a PMI column, by year or by month, as CSV. Nothing you enter leaves your browser.

### Features

- **The real monthly payment**: Lenders and listings often quote principal and interest only. Taxes, insurance, PMI and HOA can add hundreds a month – here they are all in.
- **PMI, start to finish**: The PMI payment, the month it ends – on request at 80% or automatically at 78% – and the total you will pay.
- **Pay it off sooner**: Add extra principal every month or once a year and see the interest saved and the years cut.
- **Affordability check**: Compare your payment with the 28/36 guideline and see the price that fits your income.
- **Amortization schedule**: Every payment split into principal, interest and PMI, with the balance left, by year or by month.
- **Private**: No account and no lead forms: your numbers stay in this tab.

### How to calculate a mortgage payment

1. **Enter the home price and down payment** — Type the price, then the down payment in dollars or tap 3%, 5%, 10% or 20%.
2. **Set the rate and term** — Enter the interest rate and pick a 10-, 15-, 20- or 30-year term.
3. **Add taxes and insurance** — Enter the property tax rate or yearly amount, homeowners insurance and any HOA dues.
4. **Check PMI** — With under 20% down, enter the PMI rate from your quote and choose whether you will ask to cancel it at 80%.
5. **Read the result** — See the monthly payment and its parts, when PMI ends, the payoff date and the total cost; add extra payments to compare.

### Guide

#### What is in a mortgage payment

- **Principal and interest** – the loan payment itself: P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1), with r the monthly rate and n the number of payments.
- **Property tax** – set by your county or city, usually paid through an escrow account with the mortgage.
- **Homeowners insurance** – required by lenders, also often paid through escrow.
- **PMI** – private mortgage insurance on conventional loans with less than 20% down; it protects the lender, not you.
- **HOA dues** – paid to a homeowners association, usually separately from the mortgage.

#### 15-year or 30-year mortgage

For a $320,000 loan at 6.5%. Shorter loans usually come with lower rates too, which widens the gap further. A 30-year loan with regular extra payments keeps the lower required payment while cutting much of the interest.

| Term | Principal & interest | Total interest |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 15 years | $2,787.54 | $181,758 |
| 20 years | $2,385.83 | $252,600 |
| 30 years | $2,022.62 | $408,142 |

#### When PMI ends

Under the Homeowners Protection Act, for loans on a primary home made since July 1999: you may ask in writing to cancel PMI once the balance reaches **80% of the original value** (the lower of the price and the appraisal), if your payments are up to date. Extra payments bring that date forward. PMI **must end automatically** when the original schedule reaches **78%**, and in any case by the first month after the **midpoint** of the loan term if you are current. FHA mortgage insurance follows different rules.

### FAQ

**Where does Mortgage Calculator run?**
Mortgage Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How is a mortgage payment calculated?**
The loan part uses P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1): P is the loan, r the yearly rate ÷ 12 and n the number of months. For $320,000 at 6.5% over 360 months that is $2,022.62. Property tax, insurance, PMI and HOA are then added on top.

**How do I avoid or get rid of PMI?**
Put down at least 20% on a conventional loan. If you put down less, you can ask to cancel PMI once the balance is 80% of the original value – sooner if you pay extra principal – and it ends by itself at 78%. A big rise in value can also help, but lenders usually require an appraisal for that.

**How much house can I afford?**
A common starting point is the 28/36 guideline: total housing costs within 28% of gross monthly income, and all debt payments within 36%. Enter your income and debts in the calculator to see the price that fits, then compare with what lenders approve.

**Are extra mortgage payments worth it?**
Every extra dollar of principal saves the loan’s interest rate on it for the rest of the loan. $200 a month more on a $320,000 loan at 6.5% saves about $105,000 and 6½ years. Check that your loan has no prepayment penalty and ask your servicer to apply extra payments to principal.

**What property tax rate should I use?**
Use the rate or yearly bill for the specific home: listings and county assessor websites show the current tax. Rates vary a lot between states and counties, and taxes are based on the assessed value, which can differ from the price.

## Auto Loan Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/auto-loan-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-16

Auto Loan Calculator on Toolspire: US auto loan calculator with trade-in, payoff owed, sales tax, rebates and fees: monthly payment, out-the-door price.

### At a glance

- Auto Loan Calculator on Toolspire: covers everything on the buyer’s order: **vehicle price, cash rebate, down payment, trade-in value, what you still owe on the trade-in, sales tax and fees**.
- Example: a **$35,000** car with an $8,000 trade-in ($3,000 owed), $3,000 down, 7% sales tax and $800 of fees at **6.5% APR for 60 months** is **$580.92 a month**, with $5,165 of interest.
- In most states the **trade-in lowers the taxed price** – $1,890 of tax instead of $2,450 in that example. California, Hawaii, Virginia and Washington DC tax the full price; Michigan limits the credit.
- **Negative equity** – owing more on the trade-in than it is worth – is added to the new loan, and the calculator warns you when it is.
- Shows the **out-the-door price**, the cash due at signing, the total of payments and the total cost with interest, and lets you pay tax and fees upfront or in the loan.
- **Low APR or cash back?** On that example, 1.9% APR beats $2,500 cash back at 6.5% by **$774**. Compare any two offers.
- Compares 36- to 84-month terms, estimates the price a monthly budget covers and builds the amortization schedule. Nothing you enter leaves your browser.

### Features

- **Dealer math, made clear**: Rebate, trade-in, payoff, tax and fees are applied in the right order, so the amount financed matches the buyer’s order.
- **Sales tax done properly**: Choose whether your state credits the trade-in against tax and whether the rebate is taxed.
- **Negative equity flagged**: See exactly how much of an old loan rolls into the new one, and what it does to the payment.
- **Low APR or cash back**: Put a dealer’s special rate against a rebate with your own financing and see which costs less over the loan.
- **Term comparison**: A table of 36- to 84-month payments and interest shows the real price of a lower payment.
- **Private**: No account and no dealer leads – the numbers stay on your device.

### How to calculate a car payment

1. **Enter the price and rebate** — Type the negotiated vehicle price, any cash rebate and your cash down payment.
2. **Add your trade-in** — Enter what the dealer gives for your current car and what you still owe on it.
3. **Set tax and fees** — Enter your sales tax rate, how your state treats trade-ins and rebates, and the title, registration and dealer fees.
4. **Choose the loan** — Enter the APR and pick a term from 36 to 84 months.
5. **Compare** — Read the payment and total cost, compare terms, and test a low-APR offer against cash back.

### Guide

#### How the amount financed is worked out

Sales tax is charged on the price – less the trade-in in states that allow it, and usually before a manufacturer rebate. Whether fees such as the documentation fee are taxed varies by state; the calculator taxes the price only.

- **Price − rebate** – what the car costs after incentives.
- **− trade-in equity** – the trade-in value minus what you owe on it; if you owe more, the difference is added instead.
- **− down payment** – cash you pay at signing.
- **+ sales tax and fees** – if you choose to finance them rather than pay them upfront.

#### Car payment by term

For $29,690 financed at 6.5% APR. Long terms lower the payment but more than double the interest, and leave you owing more than the car is worth for longer.

| Term | Monthly payment | Total interest |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 36 months | $909.97 | $3,069 |
| 48 months | $704.10 | $4,107 |
| 60 months | $580.92 | $5,165 |
| 72 months | $499.09 | $6,244 |
| 84 months | $440.88 | $7,344 |

### FAQ

**Where does Auto Loan Calculator run?**
Auto Loan Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I calculate my car payment?**
Work out the amount financed – price, minus rebate, trade-in equity and down payment, plus any tax and fees you finance – then apply P × r × (1 + r)^n ÷ ((1 + r)^n − 1), where r is the APR ÷ 12 and n the number of months. $29,690 at 6.5% for 60 months is $580.92.

**Does a trade-in reduce sales tax?**
In most states, yes: you pay sales tax only on the price minus the trade-in. California, Hawaii, Virginia and Washington DC tax the full price, and Michigan caps the credit. Check your state’s rules and set the switch to match.

**What happens if I owe more than my trade-in is worth?**
That negative equity is usually added to the new loan, so you pay interest on it and start the new loan upside down. Paying it off in cash, waiting until you have equity, or choosing a cheaper car avoids the trap.

**Is 0% APR or cash back better?**
It depends on the rebate, your own rate and the term. Enter both offers in **Low APR or cash back?** – the one with the lower total cost wins. Large rebates often beat a low rate on shorter loans; low rates win on longer ones.

**What is the out-the-door price?**
The full amount to buy the car: price minus rebates, plus sales tax and fees. Negotiating on the out-the-door price stops add-ons and fees from creeping in after you agree a price.

## Gratuity Calculator

Gratuity Calculator on Toolspire. Estimate gratuity from last drawn basic plus DA and years of service, using 15/26.

### At a glance

- Gratuity Calculator on Toolspire: **Gratuity Calculator** runs in the browser. Nothing you type is uploaded.
- The Payment of Gratuity Act formula is 15 days of wages for every completed year. This page applies that sum and flags the five-year rule.
- The working is shown next to the result so you can check the figure before you copy it.
- There is no account, no watermark and no daily limit.

### Features

- **Copy in one click**: Copy the result without selecting it by hand.
- **Private**: The calculation stays on this device.

2. **Read the result** — The main figure is the answer. The line under it is the working or the assumptions.
3. **Copy it** — Use the copy button if you are pasting into a document, a cheque or a stylesheet.

## Step-up SIP Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/step-up-sip-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Project a monthly SIP that increases by a fixed percent each year, and separate the money you deposit from the gain. Open Step-up SIP Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- Step-up SIP Calculator on Toolspire: the monthly amount is constant for **12 instalments**, then multiplied by (1 + step ÷ 100).
- A **10% step** on ₹5,000 becomes ₹5,500 in year two and ₹6,050 in year three.
- Each month the new instalment is added and the balance then grows by **annual return ÷ 12**.
- At **0% step and 0% return**, one year of ₹1,000 a month is exactly ₹12,000.

### Features

- **Anniversary step**: The SIP rises once a year, which is how most fund houses schedule an increase.
- **Deposit versus gain**: See what you paid in and what growth added.
- **Flat-SIP check**: Set the step to 0 to compare with an ordinary SIP.

### How to project a step-up SIP

1. **Start with year one** — Type the monthly amount you can invest now, not the amount you hope to reach later.
2. **Set the yearly step** — 10 means the instalment rises 10% after every twelve months.
3. **Test more than one return** — Run 8%, 10% and 12%. The gap between them is the uncertainty, not a forecast.

### Guide

#### When the instalment changes

Twelve deposits use the same amount. On the next anniversary the amount is multiplied by (1 + step ÷ 100). Interest is a monthly estimate of NAV growth, not the exact units a fund would allot.

### FAQ

**Where does Step-up SIP Calculator run?**
Step-up SIP Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is a step-up SIP?**
A monthly mutual-fund investment that increases once a year, usually by a percent, so the instalment grows with income.

**How is the final corpus worked out?**
Each month the SIP is added and the balance grows by the annual return divided by 12. After 12 months the SIP amount itself steps up.

**Does zero step match a normal SIP?**
Yes. ₹1,000 a month for a year at 0% return finishes at ₹12,000, which is the ordinary SIP with no growth.

**Is this return guaranteed?**
No. The rate is a what-if. Real NAVs move, and tax can apply when you sell.

## Lumpsum Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/lumpsum-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Lumpsum Calculator on Toolspire. See what a one-time investment can become after yearly compounding, and how much of the result is growth rather than principal.

### At a glance

- Lumpsum Calculator on Toolspire: future value uses **yearly compounding**: amount × (1 + rate) ^ years.
- The detail line splits **principal** and **gain**.
- A ₹1,00,000 lumpsum at 0% for any term stays ₹1,00,000.
- Use it to compare a bonus invested today with a SIP started beside it.

### Features

- **Shows the working**: Future value uses yearly compounding: amount × (1 + rate) ^ years.
- **Editable assumptions**: Rates and years are inputs, so you can test a cautious case and an optimistic one.
- **Private**: The numbers stay in this tab. Nothing is sent to a server.

### How to use this calculator

1. **Enter the amounts** — Use the same currency for every money field. Do not mix monthly and yearly by accident.
2. **Set the rate you want to test** — A past average is not a promise. Run the number twice with a lower rate.
3. **Read the detail line** — The large figure is the headline. The sentence under it is what went into it.

### Guide

#### What the number does and does not mean

Because the full amount is invested immediately, a lumpsum beats a SIP of the same total money if the market rises steadily. It loses if the market falls just after you invest. The calculator cannot know which path you will get; it only applies the rate you type.

### FAQ

**Where does Lumpsum Calculator run?**
Lumpsum Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is a lumpsum investment?**
A single amount invested once, rather than a monthly SIP. The whole principal starts compounding on day one.

**What formula is used?**
Future value = amount × (1 + rate ÷ 100) ^ years. A 12% rate for 10 years multiplies the amount by about 3.11.

**How is this different from a SIP calculator?**
A SIP adds money every month, so later instalments earn for less time. A lumpsum earns on the full amount for the whole term.

**Does this deduct tax or fund expenses?**
No. Expense ratios and capital-gains tax would make the take-home amount smaller.

## XIRR Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/xirr-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

XIRR Calculator on Toolspire. Work out the annualised return of irregular investments and payouts. This is the XIRR figure mutual-fund apps show for a SIP.

### At a glance

- XIRR Calculator on Toolspire: **XIRR** is the annual rate that sets the present value of every dated cash flow to zero.
- Investments are **negative**. Redemptions and the current value are **positive**.
- ₹1,000 invested and ₹1,100 returned exactly one year later is about **10% XIRR**.
- The year count is actual days divided by 365, so a SIP instalment from June is not treated as a full year.

### Features

- **Uneven dates**: Extra purchases and a partial sale can sit on any day.
- **SIP-style result**: This is the annualised number fund apps label XIRR, not a simple point-to-point CAGR.
- **Fails honestly**: If every amount has the same sign, the page refuses instead of inventing a rate.

### How to calculate XIRR

1. **List purchases as negatives** — Each line is a date, a comma, and the amount. A buy of 10,000 on 1 Jan 2025 is 2025-01-01, -10000.
2. **Add money that came back** — Dividends and sales are positive. So is the value of units you still hold, dated today.
3. **Read the annual rate** — 10% a year means the dated flows behave like a 10% compound return, not that every rupee earned 10%.

### Guide

#### XIRR is not CAGR

CAGR uses one start value, one end value and one time span. A SIP has many start dates. XIRR gives each instalment credit only for the days it was actually invested, which is why a fund that “returned 12% CAGR” on its NAV can show a lower XIRR on your SIP.

### FAQ

**Where does XIRR Calculator run?**
XIRR Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is XIRR?**
The annualised return for cash flows that are not evenly spaced. It is the right figure for a SIP, a staggered stock purchase, or a private investment with odd dates.

**Why are my investments entered as negative?**
The formula treats cash you pay as an outflow. Cash you could withdraw is an inflow. Mixing up the signs produces a nonsense rate.

**Which date do I use for units I still hold?**
Today’s date, and the amount you would receive if you redeemed at the latest NAV. Leaving that value out ignores the investment that is still working.

**Can two flows on the same day be combined?**
Yes. Add the amounts if they share a date, or keep them on separate lines. The rate uses the date, not the number of lines.

## Retirement Corpus Calculator

Retirement Corpus Calculator on Toolspire: Estimate the corpus a monthly investment can build before retirement.

### At a glance

- Retirement Corpus Calculator on Toolspire: the corpus is a **monthly SIP** compounded every month until the retirement year you type.
- There is **no annuity** in this figure. It is the pot, not a monthly pension.
- At **0% return**, the corpus equals monthly amount × 12 × years.
- A lower return late in the term matters more, because the balance is largest then.

### Features

- **Formula on the page**: The corpus is a monthly SIP compounded every month until the retirement year you type.
- **A second figure**: At 0% return, the corpus equals monthly amount × 12 × years.
- **Not uploaded**: The inputs stay in this browser.

### How to use the Retirement Corpus Calculator

1. **Match the time unit** — Monthly fields want a monthly number. Yearly fields want a yearly number.
2. **Try a cautious rate** — If there is a return, run it again two percentage points lower and plan with that.
3. **Use the detail line** — It states the deposit, the gap, or the gain that sits inside the headline number.

### Guide

#### Before you treat this as a plan

The formula is the future value of an annuity: each month you add the deposit, then the balance grows by annual return divided by 12. It ignores taxes, fund expenses and the chance that you pause the SIP.

### FAQ

**Where does Retirement Corpus Calculator run?**
Retirement Corpus Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What does this retirement number mean?**
It is the projected value of a fixed monthly investment at the return you typed. Spending it later is a separate decision.

**Does it include EPF or NPS?**
Only if you type those contributions as the monthly amount. The page does not know your salary.

**Why is my SIP calculator different?**
A step-up SIP raises the instalment. This one keeps the monthly amount flat for the whole career.

**Is the return after inflation?**
No. If you type 10% and inflation is 6%, the real growth is closer to 4%. Type a real rate if you want today’s purchasing power.

## Emergency Fund Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/emergency-fund-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Emergency Fund Calculator on Toolspire. Turn monthly essential expenses into an emergency fund of 3, 6 or 12 months.

### At a glance

- Emergency Fund Calculator on Toolspire: the target is **monthly essentials × months of cover**.
- Three months is a thin buffer. **Six months** is the usual household target. Twelve is for a single income or a shaky job.
- The gap is the target minus what you have **already set aside**.
- Keep this money in a savings account or a liquid fund, not in shares you may have to sell at a loss.

### Features

- **Formula on the page**: The target is monthly essentials × months of cover.
- **A second figure**: The gap is the target minus what you have already set aside.
- **Not uploaded**: The inputs stay in this browser.

### How to use the Emergency Fund Calculator

1. **Match the time unit** — Monthly fields want a monthly number. Yearly fields want a yearly number.
2. **Try a cautious rate** — If there is a return, run it again two percentage points lower and plan with that.
3. **Use the detail line** — It states the deposit, the gap, or the gain that sits inside the headline number.

### Guide

#### Before you treat this as a plan

Multiply the expenses you cannot skip by the number of months you want to survive without a salary. Subtract savings that are actually liquid. A fixed deposit that charges a penalty can still count; a locked PPF account should not.

### FAQ

**Where does Emergency Fund Calculator run?**
Emergency Fund Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How big should an emergency fund be?**
Six months of essential expenses is the usual target. Use three only if you have a second income, and twelve if your work is irregular.

**What counts as an essential expense?**
Rent or EMI, groceries, utilities, insurance, school fees and loan payments. Skip eating out and shopping.

**Should the fund be invested in equity?**
No. The point is that you can spend it next week. A falling market is often when people lose jobs.

**Does the calculator include my credit card limit?**
No. A card is debt, not a fund. Only count cash or liquid savings you already have.

## Sukanya Samriddhi Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/sukanya-samriddhi-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Sukanya Samriddhi Calculator on Toolspire: Estimate a Sukanya Samriddhi maturity after 15 yearly deposits and six silent years.

### At a glance

- Sukanya Samriddhi Calculator on Toolspire: deposits are added for **15 years**, then the balance compounds for **6 more years** with no new money.
- Interest is applied **once a year** at the rate you type. The notified Sukanya rate changes; 8.2% is only the starting example.
- A yearly deposit above **₹1,50,000** is still calculated, and the detail line says it is over the scheme cap.
- At **0%**, maturity equals 15 times the yearly deposit, because nothing is added in the silent years.

### Features

- **Fifteen paying years**: The loop adds the deposit in years 1 to 15 and skips it in years 16 to 21.
- **Cap warning, not a block**: Amounts over ₹1.5 lakh still produce a figure so you can see the gap, with a note in the detail.
- **Annual, not monthly**: This is not a SIP. One deposit is assumed at the start of each of the first 15 years.

### How to use the Sukanya Samriddhi Calculator

1. **Use a yearly deposit** — If you save monthly, multiply by 12 first. The scheme is credited as a year of deposits, not a monthly SIP.
2. **Type the rate from the notification** — Replace 8.2 if the government has revised the Sukanya rate. The page does not fetch the latest rate.
3. **Read the cap note** — If the detail mentions ₹1,50,000, the maturity you see is higher than the scheme would accept.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting

The model adds the deposit, then multiplies the balance by (1 + rate) at the end of each of 21 years. A real Sukanya account pays interest on the lowest balance between the 10th and the last day of the month, and the post office compounds that yearly. Partial years and missed deposits are not modelled.

### FAQ

**Where does Sukanya Samriddhi Calculator run?**
Sukanya Samriddhi Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**When does a Sukanya account mature?**
Twenty-one years after the account is opened. Deposits are allowed for only the first fifteen of those years.

**Is 8.2% the current Sukanya rate?**
It is the example prefilled here. The rate is notified each quarter. Type the rate on your passbook or the latest gazette figure.

**What is the maximum yearly deposit?**
₹1,50,000 in a financial year is the usual scheme ceiling. The calculator still runs above that and flags it.

**Does this include the tax break?**
No. The maturity shown is the account balance. It does not add Section 80C or show tax on withdrawal.

## Home Loan Eligibility Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/home-loan-eligibility-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Home Loan Eligibility Calculator on Toolspire: Estimate a home loan from monthly income, existing EMIs, a FOIR share and the rate and tenure you would accept.

### At a glance

- Home Loan Eligibility Calculator on Toolspire: eligible EMI room is **income × FOIR − existing EMIs**. The loan is the principal that room can service.
- The principal uses the **reducing-balance EMI** formula. At 0% it is simply room × months.
- FOIR is capped here at **80%**. A result of ₹0 means current EMIs already consume the share you typed.
- Tenure is rounded to **whole months**. 20 years is 240 EMIs.

### Features

- **Income left after old EMIs**: Car loans, personal loans and other home loans all sit in the existing-EMI box.
- **Rate and tenure change the principal**: The same EMI room buys a smaller loan when the rate is higher or the tenure is shorter.
- **A hard zero**: If capacity is not positive, the page shows ₹0 instead of a negative loan.

### How to use the Home Loan Eligibility Calculator

1. **Use gross or net consistently** — Banks differ. If you type in-hand pay, do not also subtract PF again inside existing EMIs.
2. **Put every current EMI in one box** — Credit-card minimums that you revolve every month belong here too if the lender will see them.
3. **Drop the FOIR if the bank is stricter** — Try 40% as well as 50%. Use the smaller loan when you compare flats.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting
