Capacity is the monthly payment still available. The loan equals that payment times the annuity factor (1 − (1+r)^−n) / r, with r as the monthly rate. It ignores the property value, the down payment, the applicant’s age and the credit score. A bank can offer less.

### FAQ

**Where does Home Loan Eligibility Calculator run?**
Home Loan Eligibility Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is FOIR in a home loan?**
Fixed obligation to income ratio: the percentage of monthly income the lender will allow all EMIs to consume. This page lets you type it. It does not know your bank’s grid.

**Why is eligibility lower than the flat’s price?**
This is the loan, not the price. You still need the down payment, stamp duty and registration on top.

**Should I type CTC or in-hand salary?**
Use the income figure your bank will accept. In-hand is the cautious choice if you are unsure.

**Does a higher tenure always help?**
It raises the principal the same EMI can support, and it also raises total interest. Check the amortization page for that interest.

## Flat vs Reducing Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/flat-vs-reducing-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Flat vs Reducing Calculator on Toolspire. Compare a flat-rate EMI with a reducing-balance EMI on the same principal, annual rate and tenure.

### At a glance

- Flat vs Reducing Calculator on Toolspire: flat interest is **principal × annual rate × years**, spread evenly across the months.
- The reducing EMI is the standard loan formula on the **outstanding balance**.
- At **0%** both EMIs equal principal ÷ months, so you can see the page is comparing like with like.
- The headline shows the **reducing EMI**. The detail line has the flat EMI and both totals.

### Features

- **Same nominal rate, different cost**: Ten percent flat costs more than ten percent reducing because the full principal stays in the interest sum.
- **Totals, not just the EMI**: A smaller EMI can still be the dearer loan. Compare the two totals in the detail.
- **Zero-rate check**: With no interest, both methods return the principal split by the number of months.

### How to use the Flat vs Reducing Calculator

1. **Use the rate written on the quote** — Do not convert a flat rate into a reducing rate yourself before typing. This page does that comparison.
2. **Keep tenure in years** — A 36-month loan is 3 years. The page rounds years to the nearest month count.
3. **Compare the totals** — The reducing EMI is the big number. The sentence under it is where the flat quote shows its real cost.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting

Flat interest ignores repayments: the lender multiplies the opening principal by the annual rate and by the years, then divides principal plus that interest by the number of months. Reducing interest recalculates each month on the balance left. A flat quote at 10% is a different product from a reducing quote at 10%.

### FAQ

**Where does Flat vs Reducing Calculator run?**
Flat vs Reducing Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Why is the flat EMI higher?**
Interest is computed on the original principal for every year, even after you have repaid part of it. The reducing method shrinks that base.

**Which number should I negotiate?**
Ask for the reducing-balance rate, then compare total money paid. A low flat rate can still cost more.

**Is the headline the flat EMI?**
No. The large figure is the reducing-balance EMI. The flat EMI is in the detail so the two totals sit together.

**Does this include processing fees?**
No. Add fees and insurance yourself. They are not part of either interest method.

## NPS Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/nps-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Project an NPS corpus from a fixed monthly contribution and an annual return, then split it 60% lump sum and 40% annuity. Open NPS Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- NPS Calculator on Toolspire: the corpus is a **monthly contribution** compounded every month, the same shape as a flat SIP.
- The detail splits that corpus **60% lump sum and 40% annuity purchase**. No annuity rate is assumed, so there is no pension figure.
- At **0%**, the corpus equals contribution × months, and the split is exact.
- Employer contribution is included only if you **add it into the monthly amount**.

### Features

- **Corpus first, pension not invented**: The page stops at the amount that would buy an annuity. It does not guess the annuity rate.
- **A fixed 60/40 illustration**: Exit rules and thresholds change. Treat 40% as a labelled assumption, written in the detail.
- **One return for the whole term**: Equity, corporate bond and government schemes are not modelled separately. You type one blended rate.

### How to use the NPS Calculator

1. **Add employer money into the monthly box** — If your employer puts in ₹2,000 and you put in ₹3,000, type ₹5,000.
2. **Use a blended return** — A 75% equity allocation will not earn the same as a government-bond allocation. Type the mix you actually hold.
3. **Read the 60/40 line as a sketch** — The lump sum is not cash in hand after tax. Check the current NPS exit rules before you plan spending.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting

Each month the contribution is added and the balance grows by annual return ÷ 12. At the end, 40% of the corpus is labelled as the annuity purchase and 60% as the lump sum. That split is a teaching assumption. It skips tax, the Tier II account, and the rule changes that depend on corpus size.

### FAQ

**Where does NPS Calculator run?**
NPS Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Is the NPS return guaranteed?**
No. You type the return. The schemes are market-linked. A lower rate is the useful second run.

**Why is there no monthly pension?**
A pension needs an annuity rate from an insurer. This page only shows how much of the corpus would be used to buy one.

**Does 40% always have to buy an annuity?**
The exit rule depends on the corpus and the regulations in force. The 40% line is labelled illustrative for that reason.

**How is this different from the retirement corpus page?**
That page is a generic SIP. This one adds the 60/40 exit labels people search for with NPS.

## Amortization Schedule Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/amortization-schedule-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Amortization Schedule Calculator on Toolspire. Build a reducing-balance loan schedule: the EMI, total interest.

### At a glance

- Amortization Schedule Calculator on Toolspire: the EMI is the **reducing-balance** payment: principal × r × (1+r)^n ÷ ((1+r)^n − 1).
- Each month, interest is **balance × monthly rate**, and the rest of the EMI reduces principal.
- The detail lists **months 1 to 12** as interest and principal, then the total interest for the full tenure.
- At **0%**, every EMI is pure principal and total interest is 0.

### Features

- **A year you can read**: Twelve month lines show the shift from interest-heavy to principal-heavy payments.
- **Totals for the rest**: Total interest and total paid cover the whole tenure, including the years that are not listed month by month.
- **Last-month clamp**: The payment is limited to the remaining balance plus interest so the loan cannot go negative from rounding.

### How to use the Amortization Schedule Calculator

1. **Match the sanction letter** — Type the principal the bank will disburse, not the property price, unless the loan is 100% of the price.
2. **Use the yearly rate** — 8.5 means 8.5% a year. The page divides by 12. Do not type the monthly rate.
3. **Look at month 1 versus month 12** — If month 1 is almost all interest, a prepayment early in the loan removes more interest than the same prepayment later.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting

Month one’s interest is the full principal times the monthly rate. That interest share falls only as the balance falls, which is why the first twelve lines are the ones worth reading before you prepay. Fees, insurance and a moratorium are not in the schedule.

### FAQ

**Where does Amortization Schedule Calculator run?**
Amortization Schedule Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Why does the first EMI contain so much interest?**
Interest is charged on the whole outstanding balance. You have not repaid any principal yet, so month 1 is the most expensive month.

**Are all 240 months listed?**
No. The first 12 are written out. Total interest and total paid cover every month of the tenure.

**Does a part-prepayment show here?**
No. The schedule assumes the EMI and the rate stay fixed. A prepayment would need a new run with a smaller principal or a shorter tenure.

**How is this different from the EMI calculator?**
The EMI page is the payment. This page splits that payment into interest and principal and adds the first-year lines.

## VAT Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/vat-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Add VAT to an exclusive price, and extract the VAT already hidden inside an inclusive price, at the percentage you type. Open VAT Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- VAT Calculator on Toolspire: on an exclusive price, VAT is **amount × rate ÷ 100**, and the headline is the price plus that VAT.
- On an inclusive price, VAT is **amount × rate ÷ (100 + rate)**. At 20%, that is one sixth of the shelf price, not 20% of it.
- The detail prints **both readings** so you do not have to guess which way the invoice was written.
- This page is **not the Indian GST calculator**. GST has CGST, SGST and IGST splits; VAT here is a single rate.

### Features

- **Exclusive price becomes the headline**: The large number is what a customer pays if VAT is added on top.
- **Inclusive price stays in the detail**: The second VAT figure is the tax already inside the amount you typed.
- **A single rate**: There is no place-of-supply split. Type the VAT percentage on the invoice.

### How to use the VAT Calculator

1. **Decide which price you were given** — If the quote says “plus VAT”, the headline gross is the one you want. If it says “including VAT”, use the inside figure.
2. **Type the rate on the invoice** — 20 is a common UK example. A 5% reduced rate is just as valid. Do not leave 20 if your invoice says otherwise.
3. **Do not use this for an Indian GST invoice** — Open the GST calculator when you need CGST and SGST. This page will not split them.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting

Adding VAT multiplies the net by the rate. Removing VAT divides by (1 + rate) and subtracts. People mix those up because 20% added is not 20% of the gross: on a £120 inclusive price the VAT is £20, which is 20 ÷ 120 of the gross. The detail shows that second figure next to the add-on figure.

### FAQ

**Where does VAT Calculator run?**
VAT Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I remove VAT from a price?**
Read the second number in the detail: amount × rate ÷ (100 + rate). At 20% that is the amount divided by 6.

**Why is this separate from the GST calculator?**
GST pages are built around Indian CGST, SGST and IGST. A VAT search usually wants one rate added or extracted. Mixing the two pages would answer the wrong question.

**Does the headline include the tax?**
Yes. The headline is the exclusive amount plus VAT. The amount you typed is the net.

**Can the rate be 0?**
Yes. The gross equals the amount, and both VAT figures are zero. Useful as a check.

## Percentage Change Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/percentage-change-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Measure the percent rise or fall from an old number to a new number, using (new − old) ÷ old. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Percentage Change Calculator on Toolspire: the change is **(new − old) ÷ old × 100**. The sign is positive for a rise and negative for a fall.
- A move from **80 to 100 is +25%**. The reverse, 100 to 80, is −20%, because the base is now 100.
- A starting value of **0** has no percentage change. The page returns no result instead of infinity.
- This is one jump between two numbers. It is **not CAGR**, which spreads a change across years.

### Features

- **The base is the old number**: Percent change always divides by where you started, not by the new number and not by the average.
- **Direction in words**: The detail says increase, decrease, or no change, so a minus sign is not the only clue.
- **Two decimal places**: The percent is rounded to the nearest 0.01 so 1/3 of a percent does not spill digits.

### How to use the Percentage Change Calculator

1. **Put the earlier number first** — Old value is the base. If you swap the boxes, you answer a different question.
2. **Keep the units the same** — Do not compare rupees with thousands. 1500 to 1800 is the same percent as 1.5 to 1.8 only if both are in the same scale.
3. **Use CAGR for a multi-year price** — A share that went from 100 to 200 in five years did not “change by 100% a year”. Open the CAGR calculator for the annualised rate.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting

Percentage change answers “how large was this jump compared with the start?”. Percentage difference, which divides by the average of the two numbers, is a different statistic and is not what this page computes. A zero base is undefined, so it is rejected.

### FAQ

**Where does Percentage Change Calculator run?**
Percentage Change Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Is percentage change the same as percentage difference?**
No. Change divides by the old value. Difference usually divides by the average of the two values. This page is the change formula.

**Why is +25% down to the start not −25%?**
Because the base changed. Falling from 100 to 80 divides a drop of 20 by 100, which is 20%, not 25%.

**What if the old value is negative?**
The formula still runs. Read the sign carefully: the base is negative, so the words “increase” and “decrease” follow the arithmetic, which can feel backwards in plain speech.

**How is this different from the percentage calculator?**
That tool finds a percent of a number, or a number from a percent. This tool only compares an old value with a new one.

## Sales Tax Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/sales-tax-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Sales Tax Calculator on Toolspire: Add sales tax to a sticker price, and see how much tax is already inside a price that includes it, at one local rate.

### At a glance

- Sales Tax Calculator on Toolspire: tax added at the register is **sticker × rate ÷ 100**. The headline is sticker plus that tax.
- If the price you typed **already includes tax**, the tax portion is price − price ÷ (1 + rate).
- The rate box accepts a **decimal**, so 8.25% is a valid city-plus-state rate. Nothing here looks up a ZIP code.
- Rounded to the **nearest rupee or dollar unit** in the display. Type a rate, not a ZIP.

### Features

- **Sticker plus tax is the headline**: That matches a tag that does not include sales tax, which is the usual US search.
- **A second reading for tax-inclusive tags**: The detail backs tax out of the amount when the price was already gross.
- **One combined rate**: Add state, county and city yourself into a single percent. The page will not stack three boxes.

### How to use the Sales Tax Calculator

1. **Combine the local rates first** — If the state is 6% and the city is 2.25%, type 8.25. Do not run the page twice and add the taxes; the second run would tax the tax.
2. **Use the pre-tax price in the first box** — The headline assumes the amount is before tax. Use the detail’s second figure only when the amount is already gross.
3. **Do not use a GST or VAT invoice here** — Those taxes have their own pages and their own inclusive formulas. A US sales-tax rate does not belong in the GST tool.

### Guide

#### What the number is actually counting

Sales tax in this model is a single percent added to a pre-tax price. It does not know which goods are exempt, and it does not look up a jurisdiction. The inclusive figure is there because some receipts show only the total. Rounding is to the nearest whole currency unit, so a few cents can move.

### FAQ

**Where does Sales Tax Calculator run?**
Sales Tax Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I add sales tax to a price?**
Type the sticker price and the combined local rate. The headline is what you pay at the register if tax is added on top.

**How do I find tax inside a total?**
Use the second amount in the detail. It removes tax with price − price ÷ (1 + rate), which is the right split when the total already includes tax.

**Why not just use the VAT calculator?**
The VAT page is aimed at a single VAT rate and an exclusive-versus-inclusive invoice. This page is written for a sticker price plus a local sales-tax rate, including decimal rates such as 8.25.

**Will this look up my state’s rate?**
No. You type the rate. Rates change and they differ inside a state. A lookup would go stale.

## Day of Week Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/day-of-week-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Find the weekday of a calendar date using UTC, so the answer does not shift when your laptop changes time zone. Open Day of Week Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- Day of Week Calculator on Toolspire: the weekday comes from **UTC**, not from the time zone of the computer. The same date gives the same day everywhere.
- **1 January 2024** is a **Monday**. That is the built-in check.
- Impossible dates are rejected. **31 February** does not roll forward.
- The names are English weekdays, **Sunday through Saturday**, matching `Date`’s UTC day index.

### Features

- **UTC on purpose**: A date near midnight will not become yesterday just because the browser is set to US Pacific.
- **Real calendar days only**: The UTC date is read back and compared with what you typed. A mismatch is a refusal.
- **One date, one name**: There is no range. The date-difference tool is the one that counts days between two dates.

### How to use the Day of Week Calculator

1. **Pick the civil date** — Use the calendar day as it was written, not the moment you went to bed. UTC is only the clock used to name that day.
2. **Confirm with 1 January 2024** — The page should say Monday. If it says Sunday, the date box is on the previous day.
3. **Do not type a time** — A time of day cannot change the weekday here. The input is a date only.

### Guide

#### Why the zone is fixed

JavaScript’s local date can turn a UTC midnight into the previous evening in the Americas. Naming weekdays from the local zone would make “what day was 1 January?” depend on the reader. This page builds the date with Date.UTC and reads getUTCDay, then checks the year, month and day still match. Historical calendar reforms before the Gregorian calendar are not applied.

### FAQ

**Where does Day of Week Calculator run?**
Day of Week Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What day of the week was 1 January 2024?**
Monday. The page uses that date as its check.

**Does my time zone change the answer?**
No. The weekday is computed in UTC so two people do not get two days for the same written date.

**What about 31 February?**
It is rejected. The page does not silently move it to 2 or 3 March.

**Can this count the days between two dates?**
No. It names one date. The date-difference calculator counts the span.

## ISO Week Number

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/iso-week-number/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Find the ISO-8601 week number of a date, including the ISO year when the week belongs to the year next door. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- ISO Week Number on Toolspire: the ISO week starts on **Monday**. Week 1 is the week with the year’s **first Thursday**, which is also the week that contains 4 January.
- **1 January 2021** is a Friday and belongs to **ISO week 53 of 2020**, not week 1 of 2021.
- The headline is the week number. The detail is the **ISO year**, which can be the previous or next calendar year.
- The date is read in **UTC** and checked the same way as the weekday tool, so 31 February is refused.

### Features

- **ISO year beside the week**: A week number without its ISO year is ambiguous in the first and last days of December and January.
- **Thursday rule**: The date is shifted to the Thursday of its week, and that Thursday’s year is the ISO year.
- **Not a US week**: Weeks here do not start on Sunday, and week 1 is not simply the week of 1 January.

### How to use the ISO Week Number

1. **Use the written date** — The week is a property of the calendar date, not of the hour. Leave the time off.
2. **Read the year in the detail** — If you only copy the week number in early January, you may be quoting the previous ISO year.
3. **Check 1 January 2021** — The page should say week 53 and ISO year 2020.

### Guide

#### Why 1 January is not always week 1

ISO-8601 wants every week to have seven days and to start on Monday. That forces a few days of late December or early January into the neighbouring year. The practical test is the Thursday: whichever year owns the Thursday owns the week. This page moves the date to that Thursday, then counts weeks from 1 January of that year. It does not follow the “week of 1 January” rule some payroll calendars still use.

### FAQ

**Where does ISO Week Number run?**
ISO Week Number runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What ISO week is 1 January 2021?**
Week 53 of ISO year 2020. The Friday sits in the week whose Thursday was 31 December 2020.

**When does week 1 start?**
On the Monday of the week that contains the first Thursday of the calendar year. That Monday can fall in December.

**Is this the same as a US calendar week?**
No. Many US calendars start the week on Sunday and call the partial first week week 1. ISO does neither.

**Can the ISO year differ from the date’s year?**
Yes. That is why the detail prints the ISO year separately from the week number.

## Age Difference Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/age-difference-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Find the age gap between two birth dates in calendar years, months and days, plus the exact day count. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Age Difference Calculator on Toolspire: the gap walks **whole years, then whole months, then the leftover days**, clamping a month-end day so 31 January plus one month lands in February.
- **1 January 2000 to 1 January 2010** is **10 years, 0 months, 0 days**.
- The day count for that pair is **3,653**, which includes the leap days of 2000, 2004 and 2008.
- The detail says which date is **earlier**. Swapping the boxes changes that sentence, not the size of the gap.

### Features

- **Calendar age, not days ÷ 365.25**: Ten years stays 10 years 0 months. A day-count divided by 365.25 would wobble around the anniversary.
- **Leap days in the day count**: The exact days include 29 February when it falls strictly between the dates.
- **Order explained**: The headline is always the positive gap. The detail says whether the first person is older.

### How to use the Age Difference Calculator

1. **Type both birth dates** — The age calculator is for one birthday against today. This page is for two people, or any two dates you name.
2. **Read years and months on the headline** — That is the spoken age gap. The day total underneath is the exact span.
3. **Check the 2000 to 2010 sample** — It should read 10y 0m 0d and 3653 days.

### Guide

#### Anniversary age versus a day count

People say “ten years older” on the anniversary, even though the number of days depends on how many 29 Februarys fell in between. This page reports both. The year-month-day walk moves a cursor forward by whole years while that stays on or before the later date, then by whole months, then counts the remaining days. It is the same calendar idea as an age, applied to two dates you choose rather than to today.

### FAQ

**Where does Age Difference Calculator run?**
Age Difference Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How many years are there between 1 January 2000 and 1 January 2010?**
10 years, 0 months and 0 days. The exact span is 3,653 days.

**Why not divide days by 365?**
Because a birthday anniversary does not move when a leap day sits in the middle. The calendar walk keeps 10 years as 10 years.

**What if I swap the two dates?**
The gap stays the same size. The detail sentence changes to say the first date is later.

**How is this different from the age calculator?**
Age is one birth date against today. This page never looks up today. Both dates are yours.

## One Rep Max Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/one-rep-max-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

One Rep Max Calculator on Toolspire. Estimate a one-rep max from a weight and the reps you lifted, using the Epley formula and the Brzycki formula side by side.

### At a glance

- One Rep Max Calculator on Toolspire: **Epley:** weight × (1 + reps ÷ 30). The headline is this estimate.
- **Brzycki:** weight × 36 ÷ (37 − reps). At **1 rep** Brzycki equals the weight. Epley at 1 rep is weight × 31/30, so 100 becomes **103.3**.
- **100 for 10 reps** is an Epley max of **133.3**. The formulas disagree more as the reps rise.
- Reps above **12** are rejected. A 20-rep set is a conditioning set, not a reliable max test for these equations.

### Features

- **Two formulas, named**: You can see the disagreement instead of trusting whichever calculator hid its formula.
- **They already disagree at one rep**: Brzycki returns the weight you typed. Epley adds one thirtieth, so a single at 100 is reported as 103.3.
- **A rep cap**: Past 12 the page stops, rather than printing a max that the formulas were not checked against.

### How to use the One Rep Max Calculator

1. **Use a set that was actually hard** — A weight you stopped early on will understate the max. These formulas assume the reps were as many as you could do with decent form.
2. **Stay at 12 reps or fewer** — If the set was 15, the page will refuse. That refusal is the formula’s range, not a broken box.
3. **Compare Epley with Brzycki** — If they are far apart, do not pick the larger one as the weight to load next session.

### Guide

#### These are estimates of a single

Epley and Brzycki project a one-rep max from a set of several reps. They are curve fits, not a measurement. Even at one rep they do not match: Brzycki returns the weight, and Epley multiplies by 31/30. A true single also depends on sleep, warm-up and the lift. The page prints both numbers so a 10-rep squat is not turned into one unquestioned load. It will not tell you how to attempt that load.

### FAQ

**Where does One Rep Max Calculator run?**
One Rep Max Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the Epley formula?**
Weight times (1 + reps / 30). Ten reps at 100 estimates 133.3.

**What is the Brzycki formula?**
Weight times 36 / (37 − reps). It is the second number, labelled in the detail.

**Why do the two numbers differ?**
They were fitted on different data. They already disagree at one rep: Brzycki stays on the weight, Epley multiplies by 31/30. The gap grows as reps increase. Treat the pair as a range.

**Can I use this for a 20-rep set?**
No. The page accepts 1 to 12 reps. Beyond that the estimate is too loose to print.

## Macro Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/macro-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Macro Calculator on Toolspire: Turn a calorie target and protein, carbohydrate and fat percentages into grams, using 4, 4 and 9 calories per gram.

### At a glance

- Macro Calculator on Toolspire: protein grams = calories × protein% ÷ 100 ÷ **4**. Carbohydrate uses the same **4**. Fat divides by **9**.
- **2,000 kcal at 30 / 40 / 30** is **150 g protein, 200 g carbohydrate, 66.7 g fat**.
- The percentages must add to **100**, within half a point. 30 / 30 / 30 is rejected rather than quietly rescaled.
- The headline is **protein grams**, because that is the number people set first. Carbs and fat are in the detail.

### Features

- **No silent rescaling**: If the three boxes do not add to 100, there is no result. A 90% split is not treated as complete.
- **4, 4 and 9**: The calorie factors are the Atwater values used on labels. Alcohol is not a fourth macro here.
- **Percent of calories, not percent of grams**: 30% protein is 30% of calories. Because fat is denser, 30% of calories is fewer grams than 30% of the food’s weight.

### How to use the Macro Calculator

1. **Start from a calorie number you already chose** — This page does not estimate how many calories you need. The calorie calculator is the place for that guess. Bring the number here.
2. **Make the three percents add to 100** — Change all three boxes. Leaving 30/40/30 and only raising protein will blank the result until the others come down.
3. **Read grams, not another percent** — The headline is grams of protein. The detail is grams of carbohydrate and fat.

### Guide

#### Percents of calories are not percents of weight

A label’s calories come mostly from protein and carbohydrate at about 4 per gram and fat at about 9. Splitting 2,000 calories 30/40/30 therefore does not mean equal-looking piles of food. Fat’s pile is lighter because each gram carries more calories. The page refuses a split that does not add to 100 so it cannot pretend the missing 10% was redistributed. It does not know your weight, your sport or what you should eat.

### FAQ

**Where does Macro Calculator run?**
Macro Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How many grams is 30% protein of 2,000 calories?**
150 grams. 2,000 × 0.30 ÷ 4 = 150.

**Why is 30% fat not 150 grams too?**
Fat is counted at 9 calories per gram, not 4. 2,000 × 0.30 ÷ 9 is about 66.7 grams.

**What if my percents add up to 90?**
The page returns no result. Fix the split so it adds to 100. It will not spread the missing 10% for you.

**Does this tell me how much to eat?**
No. It converts a calorie target and a split you already chose into grams. It is not a meal plan.

## Body Fat Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/body-fat-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Body Fat Calculator on Toolspire: Estimate body-fat percentage with the US Navy circumference method, typing height, neck, waist and hip in centimetres.

### At a glance

- Body Fat Calculator on Toolspire: the male equation is **495 / (1.0324 − 0.19077 × log10(waist − neck) + 0.15456 × log10(height)) − 450**, in inches.
- The female equation adds the **hip**: log10(waist + hip − neck), with the constants **1.29579, 0.35004 and 0.22100**.
- You type **centimetres**. The page divides by **2.54** before those equations, because the published constants expect inches.
- A man at **70 in, neck 15 in, waist 33 in** (177.8 cm, 38.1 cm, 83.82 cm) lands near **9.1%**.

### Features

- **Centimetres in, inches inside**: The tape most readers have is metric. The formula is not rewritten; the conversion is explicit.
- **Two equations**: The select swaps the male and female Navy formulas. Hip is used only for the female one.
- **Impossible tapes refused**: A waist that is not larger than the neck makes log of a non-positive number. The page returns nothing.

### How to use the Body Fat Calculator

1. **Measure in centimetres** — Height without shoes. Neck under the larynx. Waist at the navel for the male equation, and the hip at the widest point for the female equation.
2. **Pick the matching formula** — The constants are different. Leaving the select on male while typing a hip does not use the hip.
3. **Treat the percent as a band** — A tape pulled tight can move the result by more than a point. This is not a scan.

### Guide

#### What the Navy method actually measures

The equations estimate body fat from circumferences and height. They do not see muscle, and they punish a larger waist relative to the neck. The constants were published for inches, so a centimetre entry is converted with 2.54 and only then logged. A result outside a plausible range usually means the tape was on the wrong landmark or the wrong formula was selected. It is not a diagnosis and not a substitute for a clinical measurement.

### FAQ

**Where does Body Fat Calculator run?**
Body Fat Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**Do I type centimetres or inches?**
Centimetres. The page converts to inches because the US Navy constants were fitted in inches.

**Which waist measurement does the male formula use?**
The abdominal circumference at the navel, with the neck measured below the larynx. A chest measurement will not do.

**Why does the female formula need the hip?**
The published equation adds hip to waist and subtracts neck before the logarithm. Without a positive hip the log is refused.

**Is this the same as BMI?**
No. BMI is weight over height squared. This page never asks for weight. A muscular person can have a moderate Navy estimate and a high BMI, or the reverse.

## Target Heart Rate Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/target-heart-rate-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Estimate a maximum heart rate as 220 minus age, then list the 50, 60, 70 and 80 percent training zones. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Target Heart Rate Calculator on Toolspire: estimated maximum = **220 − age**. At **40** that is **180** beats per minute.
- Zones are **50–60%, 60–70%, 70–80% and 80–90%** of that maximum, rounded to the nearest beat.
- Age **40** zone bounds are **90–108, 108–126, 126–144 and 144–162**.
- Ages outside **10 to 90** are rejected. The formula is a poor fit for children and for the very old, and the page does not pretend otherwise.

### Features

- **The formula is the headline**: You see 220 minus the age you typed, not an unnamed “max”.
- **Four zones, not one target**: A single “fat burn” number hides the fact that these are bands.
- **Nearest beat**: Percentages are rounded to whole beats so they can be read on a watch.

### How to use the Target Heart Rate Calculator

1. **Type age in years** — 40 means the estimate 180. Do not type a resting pulse here. This formula does not use it.
2. **Pick the band your session is for** — 50–60% is the easy band. 80–90% is hard. The page does not tell you which one to train in.
3. **Replace 220 − age if you have a measured max** — Multiply that measured max by the same percentages yourself. The formula will not accept a lab result in the age box.

### Guide

#### A population guess, not your maximum

220 minus age is a convenient line through a cloud of real maxima. Individuals sit well above and below it. The zones are just slices of that line: half, three fifths, seven tenths and four fifths, extended to the next tenth. Use them to label an effort, not to decide that a pulse of 181 at age 40 is dangerous or that 179 is safe. Chest pain, dizziness or a clinician’s limit overrides the table.

### FAQ

**Where does Target Heart Rate Calculator run?**
Target Heart Rate Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the estimated max at age 40?**
180 beats per minute, from 220 − 40.

**What is the 50–60% zone at age 40?**
90 to 108 beats per minute.

**Does this use my resting heart rate?**
No. Karvonen’s formula does, and this page is not that formula. It is only 220 minus age.

**Is 220 minus age accurate?**
It is a rough average. A supervised test can differ by more than ten beats. The page says so because the zones move with the max.

## Ovulation Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/ovulation-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Estimate an ovulation date as the last period plus cycle length minus 14 days, and mark a fertile window around it. Open Ovulation Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- Ovulation Calculator on Toolspire: estimated ovulation = **first day of the last period + (cycle length − 14)** days.
- A period on **1 January 2026** and a **28-day** cycle points at **15 January 2026**.
- The fertile window on the page is **5 days before** that date **through 1 day after**. For the sample, 10 January through 16 January.
- Cycles outside **20–45 days** are rejected. A one-off very short or very long cycle should not be forced through a 28-day sketch.

### Features

- **Cycle length is an input**: The page does not assume 28 unless that is what you leave in the box. A 32-day cycle moves the estimate later.
- **The 14-day luteal sketch**: The subtraction is the simplified luteal length used in calendar methods. It is not a measurement of your luteal phase.
- **A window, not a single fertile day**: Sperm survival is why the detail starts five days before the estimated ovulation, not on the day itself.

### How to use the Ovulation Calculator

1. **Use the first bleeding day** — Spotting rules differ. Use the day you would call day 1 of the cycle in a diary, and keep that definition next time.
2. **Type your usual cycle, not an average you hope for** — If the last three cycles were 26, 35 and 29, a single number is a weak estimate. The page will still run one number.
3. **Do not use the window as birth control** — A calendar cannot see a delayed ovulation. The note on the page is part of the result, not a footer to skip.

### Guide

#### What “minus 14” assumes

The sketch assumes the second half of the cycle, from ovulation to the next period, is 14 days, so ovulation sits at cycle length minus 14. Many people are close to that and many are not. Illness, travel and breastfeeding move it. The fertile window adds the days before, because sperm can wait, and one day after, as a small margin. None of this confirms that ovulation happened. A late period is a reason to take a test or talk to a clinician, not a reason to rerun the calendar.

### FAQ

**Where does Ovulation Calculator run?**
Ovulation Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**When is ovulation on a 28-day cycle?**
About 14 days after the first day of the period. A period starting 1 January 2026 estimates 15 January.

**What is the fertile window here?**
From five days before the estimated ovulation through the day after it. It is a calendar margin, not a lab result.

**What if my cycle is 35 days?**
The estimate moves to day 21 of the cycle (35 − 14), counted from the period start. Type 35. Do not leave 28.

**Can I use this to avoid pregnancy?**
No. It is not contraception. Ovulation can move, and this page cannot see that.

## Unit Price Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/unit-price-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Unit Price Calculator on Toolspire: Divide a pack price by the quantity inside it, then price a different quantity at that same rate per unit.

### At a glance

- Unit Price Calculator on Toolspire: unit price = **pack price ÷ units in the pack**. ₹250 for 5 is **₹50.00** each.
- The detail prices the **units you want** at that rate. 2 × ₹50.00 is **₹100.00**.
- Quantity must be **greater than zero**. A pack of zero units is rejected rather than dividing by zero.
- The display uses **two decimal places**, so a 3-for-₹10 pack shows ₹3.33 a unit, not a rounded rupee that hides the comparison.

### Features

- **Per unit, then a new quantity**: You get the comparison figure and the cost of the amount you actually mean to buy.
- **Two decimals on purpose**: Pack comparisons are often a few paise apart. Rounding to a rupee would make different packs look equal.
- **No unit conversion**: 500 g and 0.5 kg are not reconciled. Type both packs in the same unit or the cheaper one can look dearer.

### How to use the Unit Price Calculator

1. **Use one unit for the whole comparison** — If one label is 750 ml and the other is 1 litre, convert the litre to 1000 ml before you type it.
2. **Put the shelf price in the first box** — Use the price you would pay, after any offer you already understand. A “2 for” offer is the price of both packs and the quantity of both.
3. **Run the second pack before you decide** — The page holds one pack at a time. The lower price per unit is the better rate. Then read the detail for the quantity you need.

### Guide

#### The rate and the basket are different questions

The cheapest rate per unit is not always the right purchase. A lower price per kilogram on a 5 kg bag is a worse buy if you will throw half away. This page separates those questions: the headline is the rate, and the detail is what your chosen quantity costs at that rate. It does not know the product, the tax included in the shelf price, or a second pack. Compare by running each pack with the same unit.

### FAQ

**Where does Unit Price Calculator run?**
Unit Price Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How do I compare two pack sizes?**
Run each pack with its own price and its own quantity, in the same unit. The lower headline is the better rate per unit.

**What is the unit price of ₹250 for 5?**
₹50.00. Two units at that rate cost ₹100.00.

**Why two decimal places?**
So a pack at ₹3.33 a piece does not look the same as a pack at ₹3.49. Those paise decide the comparison.

**Does this add GST?**
No. Type the price as it is on the shelf or the invoice you are comparing. The GST calculator is separate.

## Speed Distance Time Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/speed-distance-time-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Speed Distance Time Calculator on Toolspire. Solve speed, distance or time by leaving exactly one of the three blank. Distance is kilometres, speed is km/h.

### At a glance

- Speed Distance Time Calculator on Toolspire: **Time = distance ÷ speed**. 100 km at 50 km/h is **2 hours**.
- **Distance = speed × time**. 50 km/h for 2 hours is **100 km**.
- **Speed = distance ÷ time**. 100 km in 2 hours is **50 km/h**.
- Exactly **one box must be empty**. Zero speed cannot produce a time, and zero time cannot produce a speed.

### Features

- **The blank is the question**: You do not pick a mode. The empty box is the value being solved.
- **Hours, not a clock**: 1.5 means one and a half hours. 90 in the time box would be read as 90 hours.
- **Kilometres throughout**: Miles are not converted. A mixed unit would give a confident wrong answer.

### How to use the Speed Distance Time Calculator

1. **Clear the box you want answered** — Empty means unknown. A zero is a real zero, and it will not be solved for.
2. **Convert minutes before you type the time** — 45 minutes is 0.75 hours. 30 minutes is 0.5 hours.
3. **Keep all three in km and hours** — If the sign says miles, convert first. The labels on the boxes are the units of the answer too.

### Guide

#### One equation, one unknown

Distance, speed and time are one equation: distance = speed × time. With two of them typed, the third is fixed. With all three typed, the page does not check whether your numbers agree, because you did not say which one to correct. With two blanks, it does not invent a missing value. Time is in decimal hours so the arithmetic stays a single division. A journey time in minutes belongs in the time-duration tool after you convert.

### FAQ

**Where does Speed Distance Time Calculator run?**
Speed Distance Time Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How long does 100 km take at 50 km/h?**
2 hours. Leave the time box blank and type the other two.

**How do I enter 90 minutes?**
Type 1.5 in the time box. The box is hours. 90 would mean 90 hours.

**What if I fill every box?**
There is no result. Clear the one you want calculated.

**Does this include traffic or stops?**
No. It is constant speed over the distance you typed. A fuel-cost page is the one that prices a trip, and it also assumes the distance you give it.

## Present Value Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/present-value-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Discount a future lump sum back to today with present value = future ÷ (1 + rate) raised to the number of years. Open Present Value Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- Present Value Calculator on Toolspire: present value = **future ÷ (1 + rate)^years**, compounded once a year.
- **₹121 in 2 years at 10%** is **₹100** today. 121 ÷ 1.21 = 100.
- A higher rate or a longer wait **shrinks** today’s value. The future cheque does not change.
- This is one lump sum. A stream of payments is the NPV page, not this one.

### Features

- **One future cheque**: Type the amount you will receive once, the yearly rate, and how many years away it is.
- **Annual compounding**: The divisor is (1 + annual rate) to the power of whole or fractional years. It is not divided by 12.
- **The worked example is 121 and 10%**: Two years turns 1.10 × 1.10 = 1.21, and 121 ÷ 1.21 lands on 100.

### How to use the Present Value Calculator

1. **Type the amount you will actually get** — Use the future rupee figure, not today’s price of the thing you hope to buy.
2. **Use a discount rate you could earn elsewhere** — If a fixed deposit pays 7%, try 7% rather than a hopeful 12%.
3. **Read a smaller number as the cost of waiting** — The gap between the future amount and the headline is what those years of delay are worth at that rate.

### Guide

#### Why divide instead of multiply

Compound interest asks what a rupee becomes later. Present value asks what a later rupee is worth now, so the same factor is used as a divisor. ₹121 ÷ (1.10)² is ₹100. The page does not subtract inflation on top of the rate you type. If you want purchasing power, put a real rate in the box yourself.

### FAQ

**Where does Present Value Calculator run?**
Present Value Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the present value of ₹121 in two years at 10%?**
₹100. The growth factor is 1.21, and 121 divided by 1.21 is 100.

**Is the rate monthly?**
No. 10 means 10% a year, applied once a year. A monthly rate belongs in a different model.

**What if the rate is zero?**
The present value equals the future amount. Waiting earns nothing, so a rupee later is still a rupee now.

**How is this different from the lumpsum calculator?**
Lumpsum multiplies today’s money forward. This page divides a future sum backward.

## Effective Annual Rate Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/effective-annual-rate-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Effective Annual Rate Calculator on Toolspire: Turn a nominal annual rate and a compounding count into the effective annual rate, (1 + r/n)^n − 1.

### At a glance

- Effective Annual Rate Calculator on Toolspire: EAR = **(1 + nominal/periods)^periods − 1**.
- **12% compounded monthly** is **(1.01)^12 − 1**, about **12.68%**.
- Compounding **once a year** makes EAR equal the nominal rate.
- More compounds **raise** the effective rate. The nominal percent in the advert does not move.

### Features

- **Periods are a count, not a percent**: 12 means monthly. 4 means quarterly. 1 means the effective rate equals the nominal rate.
- **The formula is the definition**: Nothing is annualised by multiplying a monthly rate by 12. That product is the nominal rate you already typed.
- **12.68 is the check**: Twelve percent with twelve compounds should read 12.68% after rounding to two decimals.

### How to use the Effective Annual Rate Calculator

1. **Copy the advertised yearly rate** — If the note says 12% per annum compounded monthly, type 12 and 12. Do not type 1.
2. **Count the compounds** — Monthly is 12, quarterly is 4, half-yearly is 2, daily is 365 if they truly compound every day.
3. **Compare EAR with a once-a-year deposit** — A 12% annual deposit and a 12% monthly deposit are not the same deal. The gap is this page’s result minus 12.

### Guide

#### Nominal is the label, effective is the year

Banks often quote a nominal yearly rate and then compound more than once. Each compound adds interest on interest inside the year, so a rupee grows by more than the printed percent. (1 + 0.12/12)^12 − 1 is the extra. This page does not know fees, tax or a premature-withdrawal penalty.

### FAQ

**Where does Effective Annual Rate Calculator run?**
Effective Annual Rate Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 12% compounded monthly as an effective rate?**
About 12.68% a year. Each month multiplies the balance by 1.01, twelve times.

**What if it compounds once a year?**
The effective rate equals the nominal rate. Type 1 in the periods box.

**Is EAR the same as APR?**
No. APR is often a nominal or a fee-inclusive borrowing label. EAR here is only the compound-growth identity from the rate and the count you type.

**Why not just multiply 1% by 12?**
That product is 12%, the nominal rate. Effective rate compounds the 1% on the growing balance.

## Sinking Fund Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/sinking-fund-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Find the equal year-end deposit that grows to a target, using payment = target × r ÷ ((1 + r)^years − 1). The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Sinking Fund Calculator on Toolspire: with interest, the deposit is **target × r ÷ ((1 + r)^years − 1)**, paid at each year end.
- At **0%**, the deposit is **target ÷ years**. ₹1,000 over 5 years is **₹200**.
- The deposits are **yearly**, not monthly. A monthly SIP is a different formula.
- A higher rate **cuts** the deposit, because interest does more of the work.

### Features

- **Year-end deposits**: The formula assumes each deposit arrives at the end of the year and then has the remaining years to earn.
- **A zero-rate identity**: No interest means an even split. That is the check that the page is not sneaking in a return.
- **One target, one rate**: The target does not step up, and the rate does not change halfway through.

### How to use the Sinking Fund Calculator

1. **Type the amount you need on the final day** — Include the whole target. Do not subtract what you have already saved unless you reduce the target yourself.
2. **Keep the rate annual** — 8 means 8% a year. Do not type a monthly rate.
3. **Compare with a zero rate** — Run 0% once. The deposit should be the target divided by the years. Anything else is a bug, not a feature.

### Guide

#### A sinking fund is the reverse of compound growth

Compound interest starts from a deposit and walks forward. A sinking fund starts from the pile you need and walks backward to the equal year-end payment that reaches it. At a zero rate that payment is the target divided by the years. With interest, each earlier deposit has fewer years of growth than a lump sum placed today, which is why the payment is larger than target ÷ (1+r)^years. Tax and a missed year are not in the figure.

### FAQ

**Where does Sinking Fund Calculator run?**
Sinking Fund Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What yearly deposit builds ₹1,000 in 5 years at 0%?**
₹200. With no interest the target is simply split into five equal deposits.

**Why is this not the SIP calculator?**
A SIP here would be monthly. This page deposits once each year, at the end of the year.

**Does a higher rate mean I save less?**
Yes. The target stays put, so more growth means a smaller deposit from you.

**When is the deposit made?**
At the end of each year. A deposit on 1 January would earn a full extra year and needs a different formula.

## Straight-Line Depreciation

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/straight-line-depreciation/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Straight-Line Depreciation on Toolspire: Spread an asset’s cost minus salvage in equal yearly depreciation: (cost − salvage) ÷ useful life.

### At a glance

- Straight-Line Depreciation on Toolspire: annual charge = **(cost − salvage) ÷ years**.
- **₹1,000 cost, ₹100 salvage, 5 years** is **₹180** a year.
- After the life, the book value equals the **salvage**, because the charges add up to cost minus salvage.
- Salvage **above cost** is rejected. Depreciation would otherwise add value.

### Features

- **The same charge every year**: Year 5 is the same rupee amount as year 1. That is the point of straight line.
- **Salvage is not depreciated**: Only the gap between cost and what you expect to recover is spread.
- **Life is whole years**: The page rounds the life to a year count before it divides.

### How to use the Straight-Line Depreciation

1. **Use the capitalised cost** — Include what you capitalised. Do not also deduct the same installation cost as an expense.
2. **Type a salvage you can defend** — Zero is allowed. A salvage higher than cost is not.
3. **Do not use this for a block of assets under tax law** — Income-tax written-down value is a different method. The declining-balance page is closer to that shape, and even that is not a return.

### Guide

#### Equal charges, then salvage remains

Straight line takes the portion of cost you do not expect to recover and divides it by the useful life. Five charges of ₹180 on a ₹1,000 asset with ₹100 salvage use up ₹900 and leave ₹100. The method ignores that a machine often loses more value in year one. It also ignores tax blocks, extra shift depreciation and a change of estimate.

### FAQ

**Where does Straight-Line Depreciation run?**
Straight-Line Depreciation runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the yearly charge on ₹1,000 with ₹100 salvage over 5 years?**
₹180. The depreciable amount is ₹900, and 900 ÷ 5 is 180.

**Does the charge fall each year?**
No. Falling charges are the declining-balance method. Straight line repeats the same amount.

**Can salvage be zero?**
Yes. The whole cost is then spread across the life.

**Is this the income-tax depreciation rate?**
No. Tax depreciation in India is generally a block written-down-value calculation. This page is the equal-charge method only.

## Declining Balance Depreciation

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/declining-balance-depreciation/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Apply a fixed percent to the opening book value, and show year-one depreciation plus the book value left. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Declining Balance Depreciation on Toolspire: year-one depreciation = **opening book × rate**.
- **₹1,000 at 20%** is **₹200**, and the closing book is **₹800**.
- Next year the same percent applies to **₹800**, not to the original ₹1,000.
- The rate is the percent you type. The page does not look up a tax block rate.

### Features

- **Only the next year**: The headline is this year’s charge. The detail is the book value the following year would start from.
- **Percent of what remains**: The base is the opening book, which already excludes earlier years’ depreciation if you type that reduced figure.
- **No salvage floor**: The method can keep charging forever on paper. A salvage stop is a policy you apply outside this one-year sum.

### How to use the Declining Balance Depreciation

1. **Type this year’s opening book, not the original invoice if years have passed** — After one 20% year, a ₹1,000 asset opens the next year at ₹800. Type 800 for year two.
2. **Type the percent, not a fraction** — 20 means 20%. 0.2 would be read as a fifth of one percent.
3. **Use straight line if the charge must be equal** — This page will not flatten the charge. Equal amounts are the other depreciation page.

### Guide

#### The percent is fixed and the base falls

Declining balance multiplies whatever book value you still have by the same percent. Twenty percent of ₹1,000 is ₹200, so the next opening book is ₹800, and twenty percent of that would be ₹160. The page stops after one year so you can see that hand-off. It does not build a tax block, add new purchases, or stop at a salvage value.

### FAQ

**Where does Declining Balance Depreciation run?**
Declining Balance Depreciation runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 20% declining balance on ₹1,000?**
₹200 of depreciation, and ₹800 of book value left.

**Why is year two not ₹200 as well?**
Because the base fell to ₹800. Twenty percent of 800 is 160. Type 800 to see that year.

**Is this the Income Tax Act rate?**
Only if the percent you type is the rate for that block. The page does not store those rates.

**How do I force an equal charge?**
Use the straight-line page. Declining balance is unequal by design.

## Current Ratio Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/current-ratio-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Current Ratio Calculator on Toolspire. Divide current assets by current liabilities. A result of 2 means there are two rupees of short-term assets per rupee.

### At a glance

- Current Ratio Calculator on Toolspire: current ratio = **current assets ÷ current liabilities**.
- **200 ÷ 100 = 2**. Two rupees of short-term assets stand behind each rupee of short-term bills.
- Inventory, receivables and cash all sit in the asset box. Nothing is removed.
- Liabilities of **zero** are rejected. The ratio would be undefined.

### Features

- **Both sides are current**: Do not type plant or a long-term loan. Those belong on a different statement line.
- **Inventory is included**: That is the difference from the quick ratio, which subtracts stock.
- **A plain division**: There is no “good” threshold built in. 2 is the sample, not a pass mark.

### How to use the Current Ratio Calculator

1. **Use the same date for both numbers** — A March asset total against a June liability total is not a ratio, it is two photographs.
2. **Keep stock in the assets** — If you want stock out, use the quick-ratio page instead of subtracting here and forgetting.
3. **Read 1 as a one-for-one cover** — Below 1, current bills exceed current assets on the numbers you typed.

### Guide

#### A liquidity snapshot, not a credit rating

The current ratio lines up everything due within a year on the asset side against everything due within a year on the liability side. It treats slow stock the same as cash. That is why a high ratio can still hide a warehouse nobody wants to buy. The page only divides the two totals you type.

### FAQ

**Where does Current Ratio Calculator run?**
Current Ratio Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 200 over 100?**
A current ratio of 2.

**Should inventory be included?**
Yes on this page. Exclude it on the quick-ratio page if you want a tighter test.

**What does below 1 mean?**
Current liabilities are larger than current assets, on the figures you typed. It is not by itself a default.

**Why is zero liabilities rejected?**
Division by zero is not a ratio. A firm with no current bills has no current-ratio number.

## Quick Ratio Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/quick-ratio-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Quick Ratio Calculator on Toolspire. Subtract inventory from current assets, then divide by current liabilities. This is the acid-test ratio.

### At a glance

- Quick Ratio Calculator on Toolspire: quick ratio = **(current assets − inventory) ÷ current liabilities**.
- **200 − 50 = 150**, and **150 ÷ 100 = 1.5**.
- Inventory larger than current assets is rejected, because the quick assets would be negative from a typing error.
- The current ratio on the same numbers is **2**, because it keeps the ₹50 of stock.

### Features

- **Stock comes out**: The subtraction is the whole point. Cash, receivables and other current assets stay.
- **The current ratio is named beside it**: The detail reminds you what the looser ratio would have been.
- **No prepaid-expense debate**: Anything you leave inside current assets, other than the inventory you type, stays in the quick total.

### How to use the Quick Ratio Calculator

1. **Make sure inventory is part of the asset total** — If stock is already excluded from the asset box, type 0 for inventory or you will subtract it twice.
2. **Use current liabilities only** — A five-year loan is not a bill the quick ratio is meant to cover.
3. **Compare 1.5 with 2** — The sample’s gap is the stock. If your gap is most of the assets, the business is a warehouse.

### Guide

#### The acid test ignores the warehouse

The quick ratio asks whether short-term bills could be met without selling inventory. On the sample, ₹50 of a ₹200 asset pile is stock, so only ₹150 is treated as quick, and the ratio is 1.5 rather than 2. Receivables that will not be collected still count, because you did not tell the page they are doubtful.

### FAQ

**Where does Quick Ratio Calculator run?**
Quick Ratio Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the quick ratio for assets 200, stock 50, bills 100?**
1.5. Quick assets are 150.

**How is that different from the current ratio?**
The current ratio keeps the stock and would be 2 on these numbers.

**What if inventory is zero?**
The quick ratio equals the current ratio. There was nothing to remove.

**Can stock exceed current assets?**
The page rejects that. It usually means the two figures are not from the same total.

## Debt to Equity Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/debt-to-equity-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Divide total debt by shareholders’ equity. 150 of debt and 100 of equity is a debt-to-equity ratio of 1.5. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Debt to Equity Calculator on Toolspire: debt to equity = **debt ÷ equity**.
- **150 ÷ 100 = 1.5**. Each rupee of equity is matched by ₹1.50 of debt.
- Equity of **zero or less** is rejected. A negative book equity does not make a useful positive ratio here.
- Short-term and long-term debt are **one box**. Split them before you type if you only want borrowings.

### Features

- **One leverage number**: The headline is the multiple. The detail repeats the two rupee amounts so a swapped pair is obvious.
- **No industry table**: 1.5 is not labelled safe or risky. A utility and a software firm do not share a ceiling.
- **Book equity, as typed**: The page does not replace equity with market capitalisation.

### How to use the Debt to Equity Calculator

1. **Decide what counts as debt** — Trade payables are sometimes included and sometimes not. Use the same choice every period.
2. **Use book equity from the same statement** — Do not mix last year’s equity with this year’s debt.
3. **A rise is more debt or less equity** — Either one increases the ratio. The page cannot tell you which happened.

### Guide

#### Leverage is a multiple, not a moral

Debt ÷ equity is the classic leverage multiple. 1.5 means the borrowed pile is one and a half times the owners’ book stake. It is silent on the interest rate, the maturity and whether the debt is a revolving credit line or a bond. Negative equity is refused rather than shown as a negative multiple that looks like a small debt.

### FAQ

**Where does Debt to Equity Calculator run?**
Debt to Equity Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is debt 150 and equity 100?**
1.5.

**Does a higher ratio mean more borrowing?**
It means more debt relative to equity. Equity falling will raise it even if the loan did not grow.

**Should I use market value?**
Only if you type that value in the equity box. The formula will not look up a share price.

**Why is zero equity rejected?**
The ratio divides by equity. Zero owners’ equity is a different situation from a high multiple.

## Inventory Turnover Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/inventory-turnover-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Inventory Turnover Calculator on Toolspire: Divide cost of goods sold by average inventory, then turn that turnover into days on hand using a 365-day year.

### At a glance

- Inventory Turnover Calculator on Toolspire: turnover = **COGS ÷ average inventory**.
- **500 ÷ 100 = 5** turns. Days on hand = **365 ÷ 5 = 73**.
- Use **cost**, not selling price, for both boxes. Retail sales against cost of stock mixes two units.
- A 365-day year is the divisor. A 360-day bank year is not used.

### Features

- **Turns and days together**: The headline is the turnover count. The detail is how many days that count represents.
- **Average stock is your job**: Opening plus closing, divided by two, is the usual average. The page will not halve a single snapshot unless you already did.
- **COGS only**: The label says cost of goods sold so a revenue figure is a known mistake, not a hidden option.

### How to use the Inventory Turnover Calculator

1. **Match the period** — A year of COGS needs a year-average stock. A month of COGS against a year of stock looks wonderfully fast and is wrong.
2. **Stay at cost** — If the stock ledger is at cost, the sales line you want is cost of goods, not invoices.
3. **Read 73 as the sample’s days** — Five turns in a 365-day year is 73 days, not 73 turns.

### Guide

#### Turns are the reciprocal of days

Five times a year means the average stock is sold, at cost, every 365/5 days. That is 73 days. A seasonal business can show a healthy annual turn and still be buried in stock in one quarter, because the average hides the peak. The page uses the two numbers you type and a 365-day year.

### FAQ

**Where does Inventory Turnover Calculator run?**
Inventory Turnover Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is turnover when COGS is 500 and stock is 100?**
5 turns, or 73 days of inventory.

**Why not divide sales by stock?**
Sales include margin. Turnover at retail is a different, usually larger, number. This page is the cost version.

**What is average inventory?**
Often (opening stock + closing stock) ÷ 2. Type that average, not both ends.

**Why 365 days?**
So days = 365 ÷ turns. A 360-day convention would make the sample 72 days.

## Days Sales Outstanding

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/days-sales-outstanding/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Days Sales Outstanding on Toolspire: Turn receivables and credit sales into days sales outstanding: receivables ÷ credit sales × 365.

### At a glance

- Days Sales Outstanding on Toolspire: DSO = **receivables ÷ credit sales × 365**.
- **100 ÷ 1,000 × 365 = 36.5 days**.
- The sales figure should be **credit sales**. Cash sales that never created a receivable shorten the result if you leave them in.
- A year is **365 days**. Use a matching one-year sales total, not a month, unless you also change the day count yourself outside this page.

### Features

- **Days, not a percent**: The headline is a length of time. A bigger number is slower collection.
- **Credit sales in the name**: The field says credit sales so the cash-till total is not the default input.
- **36.5 is exact**: One tenth of a year of sales sitting in receivables is 36.5 days. 100/1000 is that tenth.

### How to use the Days Sales Outstanding

1. **Take receivables from the same closing date** — An average receivable is sometimes used. If you average, do it before you type.
2. **Strip cash sales** — Only sales that were invoiced on credit belong in the second box.
3. **Do not paste a monthly sales number into a 365-day formula** — Multiply the month by 12 first, or the days will be about twelve times too long.

### Guide

#### Collection time from one fraction

If receivables are a tenth of the year’s credit sales, customers are taking about a tenth of a year to pay. A tenth of 365 is 36.5. The formula assumes sales arrive evenly and that the receivable balance is typical. A big December invoice will stretch a 31 December snapshot. The page does not smooth that.

### FAQ

**Where does Days Sales Outstanding run?**
Days Sales Outstanding runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is DSO for receivables of 100 and credit sales of 1,000?**
36.5 days.

**What sales number should I use?**
Credit sales for the same year as the 365-day count. Leave cash sales out.

**Is a lower DSO always better?**
It means faster collection on these numbers. A lower figure caused by refusing credit entirely is a commercial choice, not a formula win.

**Why can I not type a 30-day month?**
The day count is fixed at 365 so the sample stays exact. Annualise a short period before you type it.

## Payment Gateway Fee Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/payment-gateway-fee-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Payment Gateway Fee Calculator on Toolspire: Add a percent fee and a fixed fee on a payment, and show what remains after that gateway charge.

### At a glance

- Payment Gateway Fee Calculator on Toolspire: fee = **amount × percent ÷ 100 + fixed fee**.
- **₹1,000 × 2% + ₹3 = ₹23**. The remainder is **₹977**.
- The percent applies to the **amount you type**, not to the amount plus the fixed fee.
- A fee larger than the payment is allowed mathematically and shown, so a tiny payment with a large fixed fee does not hide a negative remainder.

### Features

- **Percent and fixed together**: Many gateways charge both. A percent-only fee is a fixed box of 0.
- **Remainder in the detail**: The headline is the fee. What you keep is beside it.
- **No card-network lookup**: You type the rate on your agreement. The page does not know Visa from UPI.

### How to use the Payment Gateway Fee Calculator

1. **Use the rate on the pricing page you actually have** — 2% plus ₹3 is the sample, not a quote from a provider.
2. **Decide whether GST on the fee is inside the fixed box** — If GST is 18% of the fee, it is not automatically added.
3. **Check ₹23 on ₹1,000** — 2% is ₹20 and the fixed ₹3 makes ₹23. If you see ₹20, the fixed fee was left at 0.

### Guide

#### The fee is not a tax

A gateway fee is a merchant charge: a percent of the ticket plus, often, a flat amount. On ₹1,000, 2% is ₹20 and a ₹3 flat makes ₹23, leaving ₹977. GST on that fee, if the invoice adds it, is a second sum. This page will not invent it. It also will not pick a rate by payment method.

### FAQ

**Where does Payment Gateway Fee Calculator run?**
Payment Gateway Fee Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 2% plus ₹3 on ₹1,000?**
₹23 of fees, and ₹977 left.

**Does the percent apply to the fixed fee too?**
No. The percent applies to the payment. The fixed fee is added after that.

**Will this add GST?**
No. If the provider taxes the fee, add that tax yourself or fold it into the fixed amount.

**What if the fixed fee is larger than the payment?**
The remainder goes negative and is shown. A ₹3 fee on a ₹1 payment is a real loss, not a blank page.

## Freelancer Rate Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/freelancer-rate-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Freelancer Rate Calculator on Toolspire: Gross up a desired annual take-home across billable hours and a tax percent.

### At a glance

- Freelancer Rate Calculator on Toolspire: hourly charge = **desired take-home ÷ hours ÷ (1 − tax%)**.
- **₹1,20,000 ÷ 1,000 hours at 0% tax = ₹120** an hour.
- At **20%**, the charge is **₹150**, because one fifth of the invoice does not reach you.
- Tax of **100% or more** is rejected. Nothing would be left to gross up.

### Features

- **The hours are billable hours**: A 2,000-hour year that includes unpaid admin will underprice the work. Leave non-billable time out of the count.
- **Tax gross-up**: The percent comes off the invoice. The formula divides by what remains, it does not subtract the percent from the rate.
- **Zero tax is the identity**: With no leakage, the rate is just the annual goal divided by the hours.

### How to use the Freelancer Rate Calculator

1. **Type what you want to keep, not what you want to invoice** — The gross-up adds the tax on top.
2. **Count hours you can actually bill** — Leave out leave, pitching and bookkeeping, or include their cost in the annual goal instead.
3. **Check 150 against 120** — At 20%, 150 an hour for 1,000 hours invoices ₹1,50,000, and 80% of that is the ₹1,20,000 you wanted.

### Guide

#### Dividing by what you keep

If a fifth of every invoice goes to tax or platform charges, you must invoice ₹150 to keep ₹120. The algebra is desired ÷ hours ÷ (1 − rate). It assumes the percent is a flat share of the invoice. Slabs, GST input credit and a retainer that is not hourly are outside it.

### FAQ

**Where does Freelancer Rate Calculator run?**
Freelancer Rate Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What hourly rate keeps ₹1,20,000 over 1,000 hours with no tax?**
₹120.

**What if 20% of the invoice disappears?**
₹150 an hour. Eighty percent of 150 is 120.

**Should GST be in the percent?**
Only the part you do not get back. GST you collect and remit, with matching input credit, is not income and not a cost in this sketch.

**What about unpaid leave?**
Do not put those hours in the billable box. Fewer hours raise the rate, which is what unpaid time should do.

## ROAS Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/roas-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Divide revenue attributed to ads by ad spend. A ROAS of 5 means ₹5 of revenue for each ₹1 of spend. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- ROAS Calculator on Toolspire: ROAS = **revenue ÷ ad spend**.
- **5,000 ÷ 1,000 = 5**. That is ₹5 back in revenue per ₹1 spent, not ₹5 of profit.
- Spend of **zero** is rejected. A free post has no ROAS.
- A ROAS of **1** means revenue matched spend and the goods still cost something.

### Features

- **Revenue, not margin**: The numerator is the sales you attribute to the ads. Cost of goods stays out.
- **A multiple, not a percent**: 5 means five times. It is not 5%.
- **One campaign’s numbers**: Blending two campaigns in your head before you type hides which one paid.

### How to use the ROAS Calculator

1. **Attribute revenue the way your report does** — Last click and assisted conversions will not match. Pick one and stay with it.
2. **Use spend for the same window** — A week of sales against a month of spend punishes the campaign.
3. **Do not call 5 a profit** — Open the profit-margin page if you need what remains after the product cost.

### Guide

#### Five times revenue is not five times profit

Return on ad spend divides attributed revenue by the media cost. ₹5,000 from ₹1,000 is 5. If the goods cost ₹4,000, the ads did not produce ₹4,000 of profit. They produced ₹5,000 of sales that still have to pay for the goods, the shipping and the people. The page stops at the multiple.

### FAQ

**Where does ROAS Calculator run?**
ROAS Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is ROAS for ₹5,000 revenue and ₹1,000 spend?**
5.

**Is ROAS a percentage?**
Not here. 5 means 5 rupees of revenue per rupee of spend. Some reports print 500% for the same fact.

**Does it include cost of goods?**
No. That exclusion is why a high ROAS can still lose money.

**What about a spend of zero?**
The page rejects it. Division by zero is not an infinite return.

## CTR Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/ctr-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Divide clicks by impressions and multiply by 100. Fifty clicks on 1,000 impressions is a 5% click-through rate. Open CTR Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- CTR Calculator on Toolspire: CTR = **clicks ÷ impressions × 100**.
- **50 ÷ 1,000 = 5%**.
- Clicks **above impressions** are rejected. A person cannot click a view that was not counted, on a clean report.
- CTR is not the conversion rate. Clicks are the numerator here, not purchases.

### Features

- **Views are impressions**: Use the impression count from the same report as the clicks.
