- **A percent with two decimals**: 5 stays 5. A single click on 3,000 views will show the small percent rather than zero.
- **No benchmark**: 5% is the sample arithmetic. It is not an industry target.

### How to use the CTR Calculator

1. **Pair the columns from one export** — Clicks from Google and impressions from a social panel are not a CTR.
2. **Do not paste unique users as impressions** — If one person saw the ad four times, the impression count is what the CTR definition uses.
3. **Check that 50 and 1,000 read 5%** — If the page says 0.05%, the boxes are swapped or the percent was applied twice.

### Guide

#### A click is not a customer

Click-through rate asks what share of impressions earned a click. Fifty out of a thousand is 5%. What those fifty people did next is the conversion rate, which divides actions by visits, not clicks by impressions. Mixing the two makes a landing-page problem look like a media problem.

### FAQ

**Where does CTR Calculator run?**
CTR Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the CTR of 50 clicks on 1,000 impressions?**
5%.

**Can clicks exceed impressions?**
Not in a consistent report. The page rejects that pair.

**Is CTR the same as conversion rate?**
No. CTR stops at the click. Conversion rate starts from the visit or the click and counts a later action.

**Should I use reach or impressions?**
Impressions. Reach is people. CTR’s denominator is times shown.

## CPM Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/cpm-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

CPM Calculator on Toolspire: Find cost per mille: ad spend ÷ impressions × 1,000. ₹200 for 10,000 impressions is a ₹20 CPM. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- CPM Calculator on Toolspire: CPM = **spend ÷ impressions × 1,000**.
- **₹200 ÷ 10,000 × 1,000 = ₹20**.
- The 1,000 is the “mille”. Without it you have cost per impression, which is ₹0.02 on this sample.
- Impressions of **zero** are rejected.

### Features

- **Thousands, on purpose**: The formula multiplies by 1,000 so the number is a price people compare in media plans.
- **Spend over views**: Clicks are not in this formula. A CTR page answers that other question.
- **The per-view figure is in the detail**: ₹0.02 per impression is the same sample, written the long way.

### How to use the CPM Calculator

1. **Use the billed spend** — Agency commission that is not in the ad-account number will make the CPM look cheaper than the invoice.
2. **Use impressions, not reach** — A thousand people and a thousand impressions are the same only if each person was shown the ad once.
3. **Check ₹20** — 200 divided by 10,000 is 0.02, times 1,000 is 20. Missing the 1,000 is the usual slip.

### Guide

#### Mille means thousand

Cost per mille is the cost of a thousand impressions. Dividing ₹200 by 10,000 impressions gives ₹0.02 each, and multiplying by 1,000 gives the ₹20 planners quote. It is a price of attention, measured badly, and it says nothing about whether anyone clicked. Compare CPMs only when the impression definition matches.

### FAQ

**Where does CPM Calculator run?**
CPM Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the CPM of ₹200 and 10,000 impressions?**
₹20.

**Why multiply by 1,000?**
Because the unit is a thousand impressions. The raw cost per view is 1,000 times smaller.

**Does CPM include clicks?**
No. Clicks would make it a cost per click, which is spend ÷ clicks.

**What if I only have reach?**
Do not put reach in the impression box unless each person saw the ad once. The label is impressions.

## Conversion Rate Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/conversion-rate-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Conversion Rate Calculator on Toolspire. Divide completed actions by visits and multiply by 100. Twenty-five actions from 500 visits is a 5% conversion rate.

### At a glance

- Conversion Rate Calculator on Toolspire: conversion rate = **actions ÷ visits × 100**.
- **25 ÷ 500 = 5%**.
- Actions **above visits** are rejected. A report that counts more orders than sessions is using two different definitions.
- This is not CTR. CTR’s numerator is clicks and its denominator is impressions.

### Features

- **You name the action by what you type**: Purchases, leads and downloads all use the same division. The label is “completed actions”.
- **Two decimals**: One action in 300 visits stays visible instead of rounding to zero.
- **No funnel stages**: There is one rate. A three-step funnel is three runs of this page.

### How to use the Conversion Rate Calculator

1. **Use one definition of a visit** — Sessions and users are not interchangeable. Pick the column your analytics tool used.
2. **Count only finished actions** — A started checkout is not a purchase unless that is the action you meant.
3. **Confirm 25 and 500** — The sample is 5%. A result of 500% means the boxes are reversed.

### Guide

#### The rate is only as clean as the two columns

Twenty-five completed actions out of 500 visits is 5%. The arithmetic is dull on purpose. The hard part is agreeing what a visit is and what finished means. Bots in the visit count dilute the rate. Duplicate orders inflate it. The page will not filter either. It divides.

### FAQ

**Where does Conversion Rate Calculator run?**
Conversion Rate Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 25 actions from 500 visits?**
5%.

**Is this click-through rate?**
No. CTR divides clicks by impressions. This divides actions by visits.

**Can the rate exceed 100%?**
Only if actions exceed visits, which the page rejects as an inconsistent pair.

**Should I include bounced sessions?**
Yes, if they are in the visit total you are judging. Removing them answers a different question: conversion among people who stayed.

## Churn Rate Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/churn-rate-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Divide customers lost by customers at the start of the period. Ten lost from 200 is a 5% churn rate. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Churn Rate Calculator on Toolspire: churn = **lost ÷ opening customers × 100**.
- **10 ÷ 200 = 5%**.
- Lost customers **above the opening count** are rejected.
- New signups do not reduce this rate. They are a separate count. A firm can churn 5% and still grow.

### Features

- **Opening balance is the base**: Ending customers would hide the people who left and were replaced.
- **Logo churn, not revenue churn**: Each customer counts as one. A huge account and a tiny one weigh the same.
- **One period**: Ten lost in a month is a monthly rate. Do not compare it with a yearly 5% without saying so.

### How to use the Churn Rate Calculator

1. **Count who was a customer on day one** — That is the denominator. People who joined on day two are not in it.
2. **Count who left and did not return in the period** — A pause you still call active is not churn until your own rule says it is.
3. **Keep the period in the sentence** — The page prints a percent. You have to remember whether the 200 was a month or a year.

### Guide

#### Replacement customers do not cancel churn

If 200 people started the month and 10 of them left, churn is 5% even if 40 new people arrived. Growth and churn are different sentences. Revenue churn, which weights big accounts, needs revenue in both boxes and is not what this count does. The page treats every customer as one.

### FAQ

**Where does Churn Rate Calculator run?**
Churn Rate Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is churn if 10 of 200 leave?**
5%.

**Do new customers lower the rate?**
Not in this formula. They were not in the opening 200.

**What if I use the ending count as the base?**
You would be answering a different question, and a replaced customer would hide a loss. This page will not do that.

**Is 5% a month the same as 5% a year?**
No. The page does not annualise. Label the period yourself.

## Customer Lifetime Value

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/customer-lifetime-value/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Customer Lifetime Value on Toolspire. Estimate a simple lifetime value as average revenue per customer divided by the churn rate. ₹50 at 5% churn is ₹1,000.

### At a glance

- Customer Lifetime Value on Toolspire: LTV = **ARPU ÷ (churn ÷ 100)**.
- **₹50 ÷ 0.05 = ₹1,000**.
- The period of the revenue and the period of the churn must **match**. Monthly ARPU needs a monthly churn percent.
- A churn of **0** is rejected. A customer who never leaves has no finite value in this model.

### Features

- **Revenue, not margin**: ₹50 is what they pay, not what you keep after serving them.
- **Same period on both boxes**: The formula does not convert a monthly churn into a yearly one.
- **Zero churn is refused**: Infinite lifetime is not printed as a huge number.

### How to use the Customer Lifetime Value

1. **Pick a period and stick to it** — If churn is 5% a month, ARPU must be one month of revenue, and the ₹1,000 is a stock of monthly revenues.
2. **Use the churn page’s definition** — Opening-balance churn, not a blended rate that netted off new sales.
3. **Subtract acquisition cost yourself** — This page stops at the revenue stock. A ₹1,000 LTV and a ₹1,200 acquisition is a loss the page will not announce.

### Guide

#### A constant hazard, not a cohort

If every period 5% of the remaining customers leave, the expected number of periods is 1 ÷ 0.05 = 20. Twenty periods of ₹50 is ₹1,000. That is the whole model. Real cohorts churn faster in month one and slower later. Discounting those future rupees would shrink the ₹1,000. Neither adjustment is in this figure.

### FAQ

**Where does Customer Lifetime Value run?**
Customer Lifetime Value runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is LTV for ₹50 ARPU and 5% churn?**
₹1,000. Expected periods are 20.

**What if churn is yearly and revenue is monthly?**
The number is nonsense. Put both on the same clock before you type.

**Why is zero churn rejected?**
The expected lifetime would be endless. The page refuses instead of printing infinity.

**Does this include profit margin?**
No. Multiply by your margin after you leave the page if you want a contribution LTV.

## Payback Period Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/payback-period-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Payback Period Calculator on Toolspire. Divide an upfront cost by the steady monthly net cash it returns. ₹12,000 recovered at ₹1,000 a month takes 12 months.

### At a glance

- Payback Period Calculator on Toolspire: payback in months = **cost ÷ monthly net cash**.
- **₹12,000 ÷ ₹1,000 = 12 months**.
- Monthly cash of **zero or less** is rejected. A project that does not return cash has no payback.
- There is **no discounting**. A rupee in month 12 counts the same as a rupee in month 1.

### Features

- **Equal months only**: If the cash ramps, average it yourself or the single number is a blur.
- **Net cash, not revenue**: Costs that continue each month should already be subtracted.
- **Undiscounted**: The NPV page is where a rate of interest changes the answer.

### How to use the Payback Period Calculator

1. **Put only the upfront outlay in the first box** — Do not also spread that outlay across the months or you will double count it.
2. **Use cash, not accounting profit** — Depreciation is not a monthly cash outflow.
3. **Read 12 as a year of equal months** — The sample has no remainder. A result of 12.5 is twelve months and half of the next.

### Guide

#### Payback ignores everything after the line

Twelve thousand rupees coming back at one thousand a month is recovered in month 12. Cash that arrives in month 13 does not change the payback at all. That blindness is why payback is a liquidity screen, not a decision by itself. Interest is also ignored: the rupee you get in month 12 is treated as good as the rupee you spend today.

### FAQ

**Where does Payback Period Calculator run?**
Payback Period Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How many months to recover ₹12,000 at ₹1,000 a month?**
12.

**What if the monthly cash changes?**
This page cannot see a schedule. It divides by one monthly figure.

**Does it discount?**
No. Discounted payback and NPV need a rate. The NPV page has a rate. This one does not.

**Should monthly cash be profit?**
It should be cash left after the month’s own costs. Depreciation is not cash.

## NPV Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/npv-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

NPV Calculator on Toolspire: Discount a run of equal year-end cash flows and subtract the upfront cost. The rate is yearly. The flows do not change.

### At a glance

- NPV Calculator on Toolspire: NPV = **−cost + the present value of an annuity** of equal year-end flows.
- **₹1,000 out, ₹400 a year for 3 years at 10%** gives an NPV of about **−₹5.26**.
- At **0%** the NPV is cash flow × years − cost. The sample would be ₹200.
- A negative NPV means the discounted inflows do not cover the cost **at the rate you typed**.

### Features

- **Equal cheques only**: Year 3 cannot be larger than year 1 on this page. XIRR is the tool for an uneven dated list.
- **Year-end timing**: The first cheque is one year away, so it is divided by 1 + rate, not left undiscounted.
- **The sign is the decision sketch**: Negative means the rate you demanded was not met. It is not a rupee loss in the cash tin.

### How to use the NPV Calculator

1. **Put the outflow as a positive cost** — The page subtracts it. Do not type the cost as a negative or it will be added.
2. **Use one yearly rate** — 10 means 10% a year. The cash boxes are yearly, not monthly.
3. **Try 0% as a check** — NPV should become total cash in, minus the cost. If it does not, a box is in the wrong unit.

### Guide

#### The three cheques are not worth ₹1,200 today

Three year-end payments of ₹400 add to ₹1,200 in nominal rupees. At 10% they are worth 400/1.1 + 400/1.21 + 400/1.331, about ₹994.74. Subtract the ₹1,000 you spend now and the net present value is about −₹5.26. A different rate flips the sign. The page will not pick the rate, and it will not accept a different amount each year.

### FAQ

**Where does NPV Calculator run?**
NPV Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the NPV of −1000, then 400 for 3 years at 10%?**
About −₹5.26. The inflows discount to about ₹994.74.

**What is it at 0%?**
₹200. You receive ₹1,200 and spent ₹1,000, and waiting is free.

**Why is a negative NPV not “I lose ₹5”?**
It means the project misses your 10% hurdle by a present-value gap of about ₹5. The cash still comes in.

**Can the yearly amounts differ?**
Not here. Use the XIRR page for dated amounts that are not equal.

## NSC Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/nsc-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

NSC Calculator on Toolspire: Compound a National Savings Certificate deposit annually for five years at the rate you type. The term is fixed at five years.

### At a glance

- NSC Calculator on Toolspire: maturity = **deposit × (1 + rate)^5**. The term is five years, not a box you can stretch.
- At **0%**, maturity equals the deposit. Nothing is added in a silent year.
- At **10%**, ₹1,000 becomes **₹1,610.51**.
- Interest is modelled as **annual compound**. A certificate that credits differently will not match to the rupee.

### Features

- **Five years, locked in the formula**: NSC’s retail term is five years. A different tenor is a different product.
- **You type the rate**: The page does not fetch this quarter’s notified percent.
- **One deposit**: There is no yearly top-up. That would be a recurring deposit.

### How to use the NSC Calculator

1. **Type the amount you will actually hand over** — Do not type the maturity you hope for into the deposit box.
2. **Replace 7.7 if the notification has moved** — 7.7 is a starting example, not a promise.
3. **Check ₹1,000 at 10%** — The maturity should be ₹1,610.51. That is 1.1 to the fifth.

### Guide

#### Five annual compounds, then stop

A National Savings Certificate is being sketched here as one deposit compounded once a year for five years. ₹1,000 at 10% grows by 1.61051. The real scheme’s rate is notified, tax on the interest has its own rules, and a premature closure is not “just a shorter power”. None of those are in the power of five.

### FAQ

**Where does NSC Calculator run?**
NSC Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How long is the term?**
Five years. The page does not offer another tenor.

**What does ₹1,000 become at 10%?**
₹1,610.51.

**Is 7.7% the current NSC rate?**
It is the example in the box. Use the rate on the notification or the certificate.

**Does this handle the tax rebate?**
No. The maturity is the compound amount only.

## Post Office MIS Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/post-office-mis-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Post Office MIS Calculator on Toolspire. Turn a principal and an annual rate into a monthly interest cheque: principal × rate ÷ 100 ÷ 12.

### At a glance

- Post Office MIS Calculator on Toolspire: monthly interest = **principal × annual rate ÷ 100 ÷ 12**.
- **₹1,00,000 × 7.4% ÷ 12 = ₹616.67** a month.
- The principal is **not reduced** and **not compounded**. The detail says it is returned at maturity.
- A 7.4% example is **not** a promise of the current notified MIS rate.

### Features

- **Interest only**: The headline is the monthly cheque. You do not get principal dribbled out each month.
- **Simple, not compound**: Dividing the annual rate by 12 is the scheme’s shape. A monthly compound SIP would pay a different number.
- **No tenure box**: The monthly cheque does not depend on whether the lock-in is five years, once the rate is fixed.

### How to use the Post Office MIS Calculator

1. **Type the deposit, not the monthly income you want** — The page solves for the cheque. To hit a cheque, scale the principal.
2. **Use the annual percent** — 7.4 means 7.4% a year. Do not type 0.74 unless you mean under one percent.
3. **Check ₹616.67** — One lakh at 7.4% is ₹7,400 a year, and ₹7,400 ÷ 12 is ₹616.67.

### Guide

#### A cheque, not a compound balance

Monthly income scheme interest, in this sketch, is the annual rate sliced into twelve equal cheques. The deposit stands still and comes back at the end. ₹1,00,000 at 7.4% is ₹7,400 a year and ₹616.67 a month. Compounding would raise a maturity value instead of paying you along the way. Caps on how much one person may deposit are not enforced here.

### FAQ

**What is the monthly interest on ₹1,00,000 at 7.4%?**
₹616.67. The year pays ₹7,400.

**Is the principal compounded?**
No. It is paid back at the end. Each month is interest only.

**Is 7.4% current?**
It is an example. Type the rate from the notification.

**Does the page cap the deposit?**
No. Scheme limits exist and change. The multiplication still runs above them.

## Prorated Salary Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/prorated-salary-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Prorated Salary Calculator on Toolspire: Prorate a monthly salary by days worked over the days in that month. ₹30,000 across 30 days is ₹1,000 a day.

### At a glance

- Prorated Salary Calculator on Toolspire: prorated pay = **monthly ÷ days in the month × days worked**.
- **₹30,000 ÷ 30 × 10 = ₹10,000**.
- Days worked **above the month** are rejected.
- The per-day rate is in the detail: on the sample it is **₹1,000**.

### Features

- **The month length is an input**: A 28-day February does not silently use 30.
- **Weekends are your policy**: If the contract pays calendar days, count calendar days. If it pays working days, count those in both boxes.
- **No statutory deductions**: PF, tax and professional tax are not removed. In-hand salary is the other page.

### How to use the Prorated Salary Calculator

1. **Match both day counts to the same rule** — Do not divide working days by a 30-day calendar month unless that is the written policy.
2. **Include the joining day if payroll does** — The page will not look at a calendar. You count.
3. **Check ₹10,000** — Thirty thousand over 30 days is a thousand a day. Ten days are ten thousand.

### Guide

#### Per day depends on the month you are in

A ₹30,000 salary is not always ₹1,000 a day. In a 31-day month it is ₹967.74, and in a 28-day February it is ₹1,071.43, if the contract divides by calendar days. That is why the day count is a box. Loss-of-pay, weekly offs and a mid-month increment need the count you already resolved. The page multiplies.

### FAQ

**Where does Prorated Salary Calculator run?**
Prorated Salary Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 10 days of a ₹30,000 salary in a 30-day month?**
₹10,000. The day rate is ₹1,000.

**What about February?**
Type 28 or 29 as the days in the month. The page does not assume 30.

**Are Sundays paid?**
Only if you include them in both the month length and the days worked, or in neither. The two counts must follow one rule.

**Does this deduct tax?**
No. It is the prorated gross for the days you counted.

## Leave Encashment Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/leave-encashment-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Leave Encashment Calculator on Toolspire: Value encashed leave as monthly basic ÷ 30 × leave days. ₹30,000 basic and 15 days is ₹15,000.

### At a glance

- Leave Encashment Calculator on Toolspire: encashment = **monthly basic ÷ 30 × days**.
- **₹30,000 ÷ 30 × 15 = ₹15,000**.
- The **30 does not change** in February. It is a payroll divisor, not the calendar.
- DA, HRA and the tax exemption limit are **not** in the formula.

### Features

- **Basic only, unless you fold DA in**: If your letter says basic plus DA, add DA into the basic box before you type.
- **Thirty-day divisor**: The detail shows the one-day rate, which is basic ÷ 30.
- **No tax exemption**: The ₹15,000 is the gross sketch. What is tax-free is a different statute and is not applied.

### How to use the Leave Encashment Calculator

1. **Read the letter for the wage base** — Some policies encash basic, some encash basic plus DA. The box is labelled basic.
2. **Count only days the policy lets you sell** — A balance of 40 with a cap of 15 means you type 15.
3. **Check ₹15,000** — The day rate is ₹1,000. Fifteen days are ₹15,000.

### Guide

#### Thirty is a convention, not the month

Dividing basic by 30 and multiplying by leave days is how many Indian payrolls price earned leave. It does not look up February, and it does not apply the tax exemption that can apply when a salaried person encashes leave at retirement. If the letter says 26 working days, this page will be high, because 30 is hard-coded and said so in the detail.

### FAQ

**Where does Leave Encashment Calculator run?**
Leave Encashment Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 15 days of leave on a ₹30,000 basic?**
₹15,000. One day is ₹1,000.

**Why divide by 30 in February?**
Because this convention uses 30, not the calendar length. A policy that uses 26 will not match.

**Should DA be included?**
Only if the encashment clause says basic plus dearness allowance. Add it into the box.

**Is the amount tax-free?**
The page does not decide. It is the gross day-rate sum.

## Bonus Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/bonus-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Apply a bonus percent to basic pay, capped by a ceiling. ₹20,000 basic, 8.33% and a ₹7,000 ceiling uses ₹7,000 as the base. Open Bonus Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- Bonus Calculator on Toolspire: bonus = **min(basic, ceiling) × percent ÷ 100**.
- Basic **₹20,000**, ceiling **₹7,000**, rate **8.33%** gives **₹583.10**, because the ceiling binds.
- Basic **₹5,000** with the same ceiling gives **₹416.50**. The lower basic is the base.
- 8.33% is the familiar minimum percent people type. The page will use any percent you enter.

### Features

- **The ceiling binds only when basic is higher**: A basic under the ceiling is not lifted up to ₹7,000.
- **One month’s sketch**: Annual bonus arrangements that use a year’s wages need you to type the wage the statute uses, not a guess at twelve times this month.
- **No eligibility test**: Days worked and the salary threshold for cover are not checked.

### How to use the Bonus Calculator

1. **Type the wage the bonus clause uses** — If the clause says basic plus DA, add them before you type.
2. **Set the ceiling from the rule you are illustrating** — ₹7,000 is the sample ceiling, not a citation of the section in force.
3. **Compare ₹20,000 and ₹5,000** — The first should pay ₹583.10 and the second ₹416.50 at 8.33% with a ₹7,000 ceiling.

### Guide

#### The ceiling replaces basic only when basic is larger

A percent bonus with a wage ceiling uses the smaller of basic and the ceiling. Someone on ₹20,000 and someone on ₹50,000 both bonus off ₹7,000 if that is the ceiling, so both get ₹583.10 at 8.33%. Someone on ₹5,000 bonuses off ₹5,000. Allocable surplus, set-on, set-off and who is covered are labour-law questions this multiplication does not answer.

### FAQ

**Where does Bonus Calculator run?**
Bonus Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 8.33% of a ₹7,000 ceiling when basic is ₹20,000?**
₹583.10. Basic is ignored once it exceeds the ceiling.

**What if basic is ₹5,000?**
₹416.50. The ceiling does not raise the base.

**Is ₹7,000 the legal ceiling?**
It is the number in the sample box. Confirm the figure in force before you pay anyone.

**Does this check who is eligible?**
No. It applies a percent to a capped wage. Days worked and the coverage salary are outside it.

## Markup Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/markup-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Markup Calculator on Toolspire: Add a markup percent to cost to get a selling price, and show the margin that markup becomes on the selling price.

### At a glance

- Markup Calculator on Toolspire: selling price = **cost × (1 + markup ÷ 100)**.
- **₹100 × 1.25 = ₹125**.
- Margin on price = **markup ÷ (100 + markup) × 100**. A **25% markup is a 20% margin**.
- The profit-margin page starts from price. This page starts from cost. They are not the same percent.

### Features

- **Price is the headline**: The detail holds the margin, so the two percents are not confused.
- **25 becomes 20**: That pair is the check: markup on cost, margin on price.
- **No discount stacked**: A later 10% off the ₹125 is the discount page, not a second box here.

### How to use the Markup Calculator

1. **Start from cost** — If you already know the selling price, you want margin, not markup.
2. **Type the percent on cost** — 25 means you add a quarter of the cost, not a quarter of the final price.
3. **Read the margin before you quote “20%” and “25%” as the same deal** — They describe one transaction from two ends.

### Guide

#### The same ₹25 is two percents

Add 25% of a ₹100 cost and the price is ₹125. The ₹25 you added is 25% of cost and 20% of price. People lose money by treating those words as synonyms. This page always adds the percent to cost, then reports what percent of the resulting price that addition represents. It does not go backwards from a price.

### FAQ

**Where does Markup Calculator run?**
Markup Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What price is a 25% markup on ₹100?**
₹125.

**What margin is that?**
20%. The ₹25 gain divided by the ₹125 price is 0.2.

**How is this different from the profit-margin calculator?**
Margin there is profit ÷ price. Markup here is profit ÷ cost. A 25% markup is a 20% margin.

**Can the markup be zero?**
Yes. The price equals the cost and the margin is 0%.

## Add Days Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/add-days-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Add Days Calculator on Toolspire. Add a number of days to a calendar date in UTC. 31 January 2024 plus 1 day is 1 February 2024.

### At a glance

- Add Days Calculator on Toolspire: **2024-01-31** plus **1** day is **2024-02-01**.
- **2024-02-28** plus **1** day is **2024-02-29**, because 2024 is a leap year.
- **2023-02-28** plus **1** day is **2023-03-01**. 2023 was not a leap year.
- The count is whole days in **UTC**. A time of day is not accepted.

### Features

- **Month ends roll**: 31 January does not become 32 January.
- **Leap day is real**: 2024 has 29 February. 2023 does not.
- **Negative counts subtract**: One day before 2024-03-01 is 2024-02-29.

### How to use the Add Days Calculator

1. **Pick the start date** — 31 January 2024 is the sample.
2. **Add 1** — The result is 1 February.
3. **Try 28 February 2024 plus 1** — You should land on the 29th, not on March.

### Guide

#### Whole days, including the leap day

Adding days in UTC moves the calendar date by that many midnights. 31 January plus one day is 1 February. In 2024, 28 February plus one day is 29 February, and one more day is 1 March. In 2023 the 28th plus one day is already 1 March. The page uses the UTC calendar so a timezone does not pull the date backward at midnight. It does not add business days. The business-days page skips weekends.

### FAQ

**Where does Add Days Calculator run?**
Add Days Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 31 January 2024 plus one day?**
1 February 2024.

**What is 28 February 2024 plus one day?**
29 February 2024.

**What about 28 February 2023?**
1 March 2023. 2023 was not a leap year.

**Can I subtract days?**
Yes. Use a negative count.

## Quarter Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/quarter-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Quarter Calculator on Toolspire: Name the calendar quarter of a date. January through March are Q1. 15 January 2026 is Q1 2026. October is Q4.

### At a glance

- Quarter Calculator on Toolspire: **15 January 2026** is **Q1 2026**.
- Months **1–3 are Q1**, **4–6 are Q2**, **7–9 are Q3**, **10–12 are Q4**.
- **1 April** is **Q2**, not Q1, on a calendar year.
- The Indian fiscal year calls April Q1. That label is a different page.

### Features

- **Calendar, named**: The headline says Q1 2026, and the detail says January–March.
- **April is Q2 here**: So it cannot be confused with the fiscal page.
- **The date must exist**: 31 February is rejected.

### How to use the Quarter Calculator

1. **Pick 15 January 2026** — The result is Q1 2026.
2. **Pick 1 April** — The result is Q2 of that year.
3. **Open the fiscal page for April-as-Q1** — This page will not relabel it.

### Guide

#### Four blocks of three months

A calendar quarter splits the January-to-December year into four. January, February and March are the first. April starts the second. 15 January 2026 is therefore Q1 2026, and 1 April 2026 is Q2 2026. Companies that close their year on 31 March use a different numbering, and that numbering lives on the Indian fiscal year page so the two Q1s are not the same sentence.

### FAQ

**Where does Quarter Calculator run?**
Quarter Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What quarter is 15 January 2026?**
Q1 2026.

**What quarter is 1 April?**
Q2 of that calendar year.

**What quarter is October?**
Q4.

**Why is April not Q1?**
Because this page starts the year in January. The fiscal-year page starts it in April.

## Indian Fiscal Year

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/indian-fiscal-year/

Last updated: 2026-10-23

Name the Indian financial year of a date. 15 January 2026 is FY 2025-26. 1 April 2026 is FY 2026-27. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Indian Fiscal Year on Toolspire: **15 January 2026** is **FY 2025-26**.
- **1 April 2026** is **FY 2026-27**. April starts the new financial year.
- **31 March 2026** is still **FY 2025-26**. The year ends that day.
- Calendar quarters are not renumbered here. January is not called Q1 of the financial year on this page.

### Features

- **The April boundary**: One day changes the label: 31 March versus 1 April.
- **The January trap**: People write FY 2026 for a January 2026 invoice. The financial year is still 2025-26.
- **No tax computation**: The income-tax page estimates tax. This page only names the year.

### How to use the Indian Fiscal Year

1. **Pick 15 January 2026** — The label is FY 2025-26.
2. **Pick 1 April 2026** — The label moves to FY 2026-27.
3. **Use the quarter page for January-as-Q1** — That is the calendar, not the books.

### Guide

#### The books turn on 1 April

India’s financial year runs from 1 April to 31 March. A date in January, February or March belongs to the year that started the previous April, so 15 January 2026 is FY 2025-26. On 1 April 2026 the label becomes FY 2026-27. The page applies that cut and does not compute tax, and it does not call January “Q4” even though many finance teams do. The calendar quarter page keeps that other numbering.

### FAQ

**Where does Indian Fiscal Year run?**
Indian Fiscal Year runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What financial year is 15 January 2026?**
FY 2025-26.

**What financial year is 1 April 2026?**
FY 2026-27.

**What about 31 March 2026?**
FY 2025-26. The new year has not started.

**Is January Q4?**
This page does not say so. It names the financial year only.

## Body Surface Area

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/body-surface-area/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Body Surface Area on Toolspire. Estimate body surface area with the Mosteller formula, the square root of height times weight over 3600.

### At a glance

- Body Surface Area on Toolspire: mosteller BSA = **√(height cm × weight kg / 3600)**.
- **170 cm and 70 kg** is **√(11900 / 3600)**, about **1.8181 m²**.
- The units are required. Inches and pounds are a different constant.
- The result is a surface estimate, **not a prescription**.

### Features

- **Centimetres and kilograms**: The 3600 belongs to those units.
- **The 170/70 check**: You can recompute the square root from 3.3056.
- **No dose**: A paper that says mg per m² still needs a clinician. This page stops at m².

### How to use the Body Surface Area

1. **Type centimetres and kilograms** — 170 and 70 are the sample.
2. **Read about 1.8181** — That is the square root of 3.3056.
3. **Do not convert the m² into a tablet count** — The page has no drug in it.

### Guide

#### One square-root estimate

Mosteller’s formula estimates body surface area as the square root of height in centimetres times weight in kilograms, divided by 3600. For 170 and 70 the fraction is 3.30555… and the root is about 1.8181 square metres. Other formulas, such as Du Bois, use different exponents. This page is only Mosteller, and it is not an instruction to dose a medicine.

### FAQ

**Where does Body Surface Area run?**
Body Surface Area runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the Mosteller BSA for 170 cm and 70 kg?**
About 1.8181 m².

**What units does the formula want?**
Centimetres and kilograms. The 3600 is for that pair.

**Is this a medicine dose?**
No. It is a surface estimate.

**Why might another calculator differ?**
It may be using Du Bois or another formula, or inches and pounds.

## Mean Arterial Pressure

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/mean-arterial-pressure/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Estimate mean arterial pressure as (systolic + 2 × diastolic) / 3. 120 and 80 give 93.33 mmHg. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Mean Arterial Pressure on Toolspire: MAP ≈ **(systolic + 2 × diastolic) / 3**.
- **(120 + 160) / 3 = 93.33 mmHg**.
- The diastolic term is doubled because diastole lasts longer than systole in this estimate.
- A diastolic pressure **above** the systolic is rejected. That pair is not a readable pressure.

### Features

- **The 2 is visible**: 120 + 2×80 is 280, and 280/3 is the result.
- **mmHg in and out**: The page does not convert to kPa.
- **Not a monitor**: An arterial line measures a mean. This page estimates one from two cuff numbers.

### How to use the Mean Arterial Pressure

1. **Type the two cuff numbers** — 120 and 80 are the sample.
2. **Read 93.33** — That is 280 divided by 3.
3. **Do not treat a single number as a plan** — What to do about a pressure is not this division.

### Guide

#### Why diastole is doubled

The usual estimate of mean arterial pressure weights the diastolic number twice and the systolic number once, then divides by three. On 120/80 that is (120 + 160) / 3 = 93.33. The weighting is a shortcut for the extra time the heart spends in diastole. It is not the mean a monitor computes from the pulse wave. The page also refuses a diastolic number higher than the systolic, because that pair is not a blood-pressure reading.

### FAQ

**Where does Mean Arterial Pressure run?**
Mean Arterial Pressure runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is the MAP of 120/80?**
93.33 mmHg.

**Why is 80 doubled?**
The estimate counts diastole twice and systole once, then divides by three.

**What if the bottom number is higher?**
The page rejects it. Diastole should not exceed systole in a cuff reading.

**Is this a diagnosis?**
No. It is the arithmetic of the two numbers.

## Waist Hip Ratio

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/waist-hip-ratio/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Divide waist circumference by hip circumference. A waist of 80 and a hip of 100 give a ratio of 0.8. The maths stays on your device.

### At a glance

- Waist Hip Ratio on Toolspire: ratio = **waist ÷ hip**.
- **80 ÷ 100 = 0.8**.
- The unit cancels, so centimetres over centimetres and inches over inches match. Mixing them does not.
- A hip of **0** is rejected. There is nothing to divide by.

### Features

- **No cutoff printed**: 0.8 is the sample arithmetic, not a pass mark.
- **Same unit required**: The detail repeats the two numbers so a mixed unit is yours to notice.
- **Tape, not a scale**: Weight is not an input. BMI is the page that uses weight and height.

### How to use the Waist Hip Ratio

1. **Measure both in one unit** — 80 and 100 are the sample.
2. **Read 0.8** — Eighty divided by one hundred.
3. **Do not expect a risk label** — The page divides. It does not classify you.

### Guide

#### Two tapes, one quotient

Waist–hip ratio is the waist measurement divided by the hip measurement. Eighty over one hundred is 0.8, whether those were centimetres or inches, as long as both were the same. A waist in centimetres and a hip in inches is a nonsense quotient. The page does not attach a healthy range. Those ranges differ by source and are not a result of the division.

### FAQ

**Where does Waist Hip Ratio run?**
Waist Hip Ratio runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 80 over 100?**
0.8.

**Do the units matter?**
Only that they match. The unit cancels.

**Is 0.8 a target?**
The page does not say. It is the sample result.

**How is this different from BMI?**
BMI uses weight and height. This uses two circumferences.

## Appliance Energy Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/appliance-energy-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Appliance Energy Calculator on Toolspire: Turn watts and hours into kilowatt-hours, then price them. A 1000 W appliance for 2 hours uses 2 kWh.

### At a glance

- Appliance Energy Calculator on Toolspire: **1000 W × 2 h ÷ 1000 = 2 kWh**.
- At **₹8 per kWh** the cost is **₹16**.
- A watt is a joule per second. The ÷ 1000 turns watt-hours into **kilowatt-hours**, the unit on a bill.
- A fan rated 75 W running 8 hours is **0.6 kWh**, not 600.

### Features

- **One appliance**: The monthly bill page is the whole house. This is one nameplate and one runtime.
- **The 2 kWh check**: A kilowatt for two hours.
- **The rate is yours**: 8 is a sample rupee price, not a tariff lookup.

### How to use the Appliance Energy Calculator

1. **Read watts on the appliance** — 1000 is a small heater, not a phone charger.
2. **Estimate hours actually on** — A rating is not a duty cycle. Two hours means two hours of running.
3. **Check ₹16** — 2 kWh times 8.

### Guide

#### The unit on the bill is the kilowatt-hour

Power in watts times hours is watt-hours. Dividing by 1000 gives kilowatt-hours, which is what a domestic bill calls a unit. A 1000 W load for 2 hours is exactly 2 units. At 8 currency units per kWh the energy costs 16. A device that cycles, or a charger that only draws its rating while charging, uses less than nameplate watts times the hours it is plugged in. The page uses the watts and hours you type.

### FAQ

**Where does Appliance Energy Calculator run?**
Appliance Energy Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**How many kWh is 1000 W for 2 hours?**
2 kWh.

**What does that cost at 8 per kWh?**
16.

**What is 75 W for 8 hours?**
0.6 kWh. 75 × 8 / 1000.

**Is the rate looked up?**
No. Type the per-unit price from your bill.

## Cross Rate Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/cross-rate-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.
Last updated: 2026-10-23

Cross Rate Calculator on Toolspire. Multiply two exchange quotes that share a middle currency. If 1 USD is 83 INR and 1 EUR is 1.1 USD, then 1 EUR is 91.3 INR.

### At a glance

- Cross Rate Calculator on Toolspire: **INR per EUR = INR per USD × USD per EUR**.
- **83 × 1.1 = 91.3**. One euro is 91.3 rupees on these quotes.
- The middle currency must **cancel**. USD in the denominator of the first quote and the numerator of the second.
- No live rate is fetched. 83 and 1.1 are the sample, not a market.

### Features

- **The labels fix the direction**: INR per USD times USD per EUR. Reversing a quote means you should have divided.
- **91.3 is the check**: 83 times 1.1.
- **A sketch, not a desk**: Spreads and fees are not in the product.

### How to use the Cross Rate Calculator

1. **Confirm each quote’s direction** — “Rupees for one dollar” is the first box. “Dollars for one euro” is the second.
2. **Check 91.3** — The sample.
3. **Divide instead if your second quote is euros per dollar** — 1.1 dollars per euro is not 1.1 euros per dollar.

### Guide

#### The middle currency has to cancel

A cross rate multiplies two quotes when the currency you do not want sits on the bottom of one and the top of the other. Rupees per dollar, times dollars per euro, is rupees per euro. 83 × 1.1 is 91.3. If the second quote had been euros per dollar, multiplying would point the wrong way and you would divide. The page will not notice that mistake, because both boxes are just numbers. The labels are the warning.

### FAQ

**Where does Cross Rate Calculator run?**
Cross Rate Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

**What is 83 INR per USD times 1.1 USD per EUR?**
91.3 INR per EUR.

**Does this use today’s market?**
No. You type both quotes.

**What if I have euros per dollar instead?**
Do not multiply. Divide the rupee quote by that euro quote, or invert it to dollars per euro first.

**Are bank fees included?**
No. The product is the two quotes only.

## ESI Calculator

URL: https://justtools.info/calculators/esi-calculator/
Category: Calculators
Processing: Runs entirely in the browser; user data is not uploaded.

Estimate ESI on a monthly wage at or under ₹21,000: employee 0.75% and employer 3.25%. ₹20,000 is ₹150 and ₹650. Open ESI Calculator on Toolspire.

### At a glance

- ESI Calculator on Toolspire: on **₹20,000**, employee **0.75%** is **₹150** and employer **3.25%** is **₹650**.
- A wage **above the ceiling** returns no contribution. ₹25,000 against ₹21,000 is outside.
- The ceiling and both rates are **editable**. 21,000, 0.75 and 3.25 are the starting figures.
- Being under the ceiling is **not** the whole coverage test. Days worked and the establishment matter, and they are not checked.

### Features

- **Both contributions**: The headline is the employee share. The detail is the employer share.
- **The ceiling stops the sum**: Above it, the page does not charge a partial wage.
- **Rates are visible**: Change them if the notified percents move. The page does not fetch a notification.

### How to use the ESI Calculator

1. **Type the monthly wage the scheme would count** — 20,000 is the sample.
2. **Read ₹150 and ₹650** — 0.75% and 3.25% of 20,000.
3. **Try 25,000** — With a 21,000 ceiling the page should refuse a contribution.

### Guide

#### A ceiling, then two percents

On these starting figures, a monthly wage of ₹20,000 is inside a ₹21,000 ceiling, so the employee share is 0.75% (₹150) and the employer share is 3.25% (₹650). A wage above the ceiling is not given a contribution in this sketch. Coverage also depends on the establishment and on days worked. Those tests are not here. If the notified rates or ceiling differ from the boxes, change the boxes.

### FAQ

**Where does ESI Calculator run?**
ESI Calculator runs in this tab on Toolspire. What you type or drop in is not sent to us and is not stored on a server.

₹150 from the employee and ₹650 from the employer.

**What if the wage is ₹25,000?**
It is above a ₹21,000 ceiling, so this page calculates no contribution.

**Are 0.75 and 3.25 permanent?**
No. They are the figures in the boxes. Replace them if the notification says otherwise.

**Does under ₹21,000 prove coverage?**
No. The page only applies a ceiling and two percents.
